The reasons of `waiting_message` are `no tmdb_token is set` (NoToken), `TMDb refused the token (HTTP 401)` (TokenRefused), `no answer within 10 s` (Timeout), the error text (Unreachable), `HTTP <n>` (Status) and the parser's sentence (Unparsed). A round that leaves a lookup `Unparsed` also writes the `job.log` line `lookup <word>: <the parser's sentence>` (design part 8.3).

**Task 26** (the IPC server and the self-test)
- Notifier (spec 12): a `failed` notification whose class is `MediaReadError` gets `"; try the other drive"` appended to the message Task 22 builds.

**Task 28** (`lazymkvctl`'s engine commands)
- T28-a. `status`, `settings get`, `history` and `selftest` print their JSON value with `dumps(&value, Style::INDENT1_ASCII)` and a newline. Every other `ok` prints nothing. Why: `lazymkvctl` then has one JSON style, the one `plan` and `decide` print.
- T28-b. `watch` prints each line exactly as received, without its line ending, followed by `\n`. It exits 0 when the connection closes. Why: a field that a newer engine adds is not lost.
- T28-c. Each run opens one connection and sends at most one request, with `id` 1. Why: one `lazymkvctl` run is one command.
- T28-d. A connection that closes before the reply, or a line `read_reply` refuses, exits 4. The message is `CLOSED` in the first case and `lazymkvctl: <read_reply's message>` in the second. Why: no engine answered, and exit 1 must stay out of `plan`'s codes (RP24).
- T28-e. Error replies and usage errors use ctl's own program name. An `error` reply prints `lazymkvctl: <message>`, then one line `lazymkvctl: <key>: <problem>` per problem; a usage error prints `usage: lazymkvctl <synopsis>` and `lazymkvctl <name>: error: <message>`, then exits 2. Why: these follow argparse's shape. S37 covers only the commands dev runs.
- T28-f. The `secret set` value is the whole of standard input with one trailing `\n` or `\r\n` removed. An empty value is an argument error. Why: a share credential is two lines (design part 9.4).
- T28-g. `relay` sends argv unchanged as `words`. For `retune`, it then appends `--spec <absolute path>`. Why: the engine builds the child's words (design part 9.5), and `Args::flag` lets the last `--spec` win, so the user's words never need rewriting.
- T28-h. Argument checks, the settings file read and the standard-input read all happen before connecting. Why: an argument error exits 2 whatever the engine's state.
- T28-i. `selftest` prints the payload of the last `selftest_result` push received before the `ok`. Without one it prints `NO_SELFTEST` and exits 1. Why: the engine pushes the result and then replies (design part 9.4).
- T28-j. The `watch` test runs in ctl against a scripted peer, not against `Harness::client()`. Why: only a scripted peer controls when the connection closes.

**Task 29** (the whole job)
- T29-a. The test waits by polling `notifications()` until one that is not `disc_done` arrives, and then until `awake().holders() == 0`. It does not use `run_until`. Why: `run_until` stops after the transition into the state is persisted, which is before that transition's actions run (Task 22), so the `filed` notification and the keep-awake release would be missed.
- T29-b. The job folder's later drive listings follow RP-1 (a) (Task 23), through the constant `LATER_LISTINGS`, which is `&["drive-list-2.txt"]`, the listing before the rip. Why: assertion 6 lists the folder exactly.
- T29-c. Assertion 8 lists the data folder exactly, `selftest/` included: Task 26 records the MakeMKV check at engine start there (S79), with the recorded state of RP-9. No self-test runs in this test, so no tool's version file is there.

### The pre-flight rulings (PF-1 to PF-34)

A pre-flight check of the plan's tasks against each other (2026-10-24) found 34 confirmed findings; each ruling below
settles one.

- PF-1 (Tasks 22, 26). Task 26 adds `pub fn deliver(&self)` to `Harness`: it only hands the queued drive events to the `DriveScript`. Every `engine_ipc.rs` test that inserts or removes a disc and then waits only through the IPC `Client` calls `h.deliver()` right after that insert or remove. In `a_retry_with_the_disc_absent_feeds_media_removed_and_frees_the_token`, after `h.remove("tok0"); h.deliver();`, the test waits with `h.wait` until `drives_json()["drives"]["tok0"]["fingerprint"]` is null before sending `retry`. In `a_retry_queues_behind_an_earlier_arrival`, after `h.insert("tok2", DISC_C); h.deliver();`, it waits until that drive's recorded fingerprint equals `h.fingerprint(DISC_C)` with `handled` false before sending `retry`. R22.1 stands. Why: R22.1 queues drive events until the next Harness call, and the IPC Client never touches the harness, so 28 Task 26 tests would time out. Cost if wrong: a few test-only lines; if `deliver` is wrong a test hangs visibly.
- PF-2 (Tasks 10, 22, 23, 25, 28). Task 22's testkit creates the harness root under `std::env::temp_dir()` with a name that contains neither `mkv` nor `lazymkv` (`engine-harness-<pid>-<n>`, removed on drop) and documents why. Task 28's `secret_set_reads_the_value_from_stdin` uses a secret value that cannot occur in a path (for example `hook-secret-7f3a`) and asserts the status lacks that value. Why: the stand-in matches needles by substring, so a root containing `mkv` makes the rip needle match every scan's `--profile=<root>/.../job.mmcp.xml`, and a second scan in one tools folder would replay `rip.txt`. Cost if wrong: one naming rule in test code.
- PF-3 (Tasks 27, 28). Task 28's `relay` checks `retune_spec` before connecting and prints its Err as Task 27's argument error, `lazymkvctl retune: error: <text>`, exit 2, with no usage line. `Args` errors (an ambiguous prefix) take the same form. `RETUNE_RELAY_USAGE` is not defined unless some other relay usage error needs it (none is known). Why: Task 27's `title_without_job_is_an_argument_error` pins the exact stderr, and a leading usage line would break it. Cost if wrong: one stderr form.
- PF-4 (Tasks 22, 25). In Task 25's `awaiting_lookup_recreates_its_timer`, `h.remove("tok0")` is called right before `h.restart()` (it stands for the removal a real eject causes, R22.6), and the `EngineRestart { disc_present: false }` assertion and Step 4's rule stay. Why: on the scripted harness the scripted Eject never calls the ejector, so the disc is still present at restart and the feed would be `true`. Cost if wrong: one test line.
- PF-5 (Tasks 22, 23). In Task 23's `a_scan_past_its_limit_is_killed_listed_again_and_retried_once`, `let id = run_to(&h, "Ejected")` is replaced with `h.insert("tok0", "movie-direct"); h.run_until("Ejected");` (the 60 s cap), and the id is computed as `run_to` does. The note says the test takes more than 10 s. Why: `Harness::wait` panics after 10 s, but the scan's kill alone comes 10 s after the spawn, after the arrival work. Cost if wrong: one test's waiting form.
- PF-6 (Tasks 22, 24, 30). `ExtractSubtitleEvidence` returns `event: None` with no `returned no event` line (design part 5.2: it runs with `Decide` as one unit). The interface sheet's list of no-event actions reads `EmptyTitleDirectory`, `DeletePartialFile` and `ExtractSubtitleEvidence`. Why: Tasks 22 and 24 and the design agree; only the sheet's list was short. Cost if wrong: none.
- PF-7 (Tasks 23, 24). Every Task 23 and 24 test that asserts `h.notifications()` or `h.timers().pending()` after `run_to` calls `h.settle()` first, as `the_eject_fake_failure...` already does: in Task 23 `a_copy_plan_fails...`, `short_staging_space_waits_in_awaiting_space`, `no_makemkvcon_waits_in_awaiting_tool` and `a_scan_licence_message_waits_in_awaiting_makemkv`, and in Task 24 `still_pending_after_the_round...`, `a_refused_token_waits...`, `filed_records_path_size_mtime_and_filed_unix` and `no_ffprobe_lets_the_disc_phase_finish...`. Why: `run_to` returns on the transitions line, which the owner writes before running the transition's Notify and StartTimer actions. Cost if wrong: one call per test.
- PF-8 (Task 26). In the Lookup part of `retry_in_each_waiting_state_rechecks_its_condition`, following S81, the test records the transition count before the retry; after `c.ok("retry", ..)` it waits with `h.wait` until `h.http().requests().len() > asked`, then calls `h.settle()`, then asserts the transition count is unchanged and the state is still AwaitingLookup. The `last.old` assertion is dropped. Why: with an empty RecordedTransport every lookup stays pending and S81 feeds nothing, so the written assertion failed deterministically and the request count read raced. Cost if wrong: one test.
- PF-9 (Task 26). In `a_client_that_falls_behind_is_dropped_without_slowing_the_engine`, `io::sink()` is replaced with a writer that counts the lines it receives (newline bytes, not `write` calls). The test broadcasts `CLIENT_QUEUE` lines, waits with a bounded deadline until that client has taken all of them, then broadcasts 10 more and asserts `count() == 1`, timing only the `broadcast` calls for the under-1-second assertion. The stuck client stays, and its gate is released at the end. Why: as written the sink client could be dropped too when its drain thread lagged, making the count 0 on a busy runner. Cost if wrong: one test.
- PF-10 (Task 17). Task 17 Step 3 runs `mkdir -p crates/dev/src/cmd/decide` before the `scp -r`, still before any other `box.sh` call. Why: without the folder, scp copies the remote folder as `decide/`, and the JSON files land one level too high. Cost if wrong: none.
- PF-11 (Tasks 1, 30). Task 1's implementer runs Steps 1 to 4 and Step 6 (neither `--offline` nor `--locked` can fetch) and stops with nothing committed, writing the full `git diff Cargo.lock` and the Step 4 counts to a file. The controller shows the diff to the owner with Step 5's sentence, waits for an explicit yes in chat, records that yes's date for Task 30, then has the implementer run Step 7. Why: the implementer cannot obtain the owner's yes (P33), and the plan assigned the relay to nobody. Cost if wrong: none; the owner still gates the commit.
- PF-12 (Task 4). The ustar reader checks `offset >= len` (Ok(None)), then `offset + 512 > len` (the truncated error), and only then reads the 512-byte block for the zero-block test. Why: the written order could slice past the end of a truncated archive and panic instead of returning the pinned error. Cost if wrong: none.
- PF-13 (Tasks 5, 11, 27). Docs are completed where the dictated code lacks them: Task 5's `LineReader::new`, `feed` and `finish` name design part 5.3 and end "Def: T5. Use: T13."; Task 11's moved items (`Clock`, `RealClock`, `RealClock::start`, `with_suffix`, `tee_lines`) keep their bodies and gain docs naming design part 7.2, ending "Def: T11. Use: T13, T14, T23."; Task 27's `NOT_RUNNING`, `REFUSED` and `NOT_ELEVATED` name design part 9.6 (exit 4's messages). Why: every `pub` item has a `///` doc naming its design part and ending Def/Use. Cost if wrong: none.
- PF-14 (Task 10). `Response` and `Create` keep `#[serde(default)]` on `exit_code`, `creates`, `stdout` and `timing`, as the existing scenario.rs has them. Lines are split with `split_inclusive(|byte| *byte == b'\n')`. Why: read literally, the brief dropped the defaults, and `split_inclusive(b'\n')` does not compile. Cost if wrong: none.
- PF-15 (Task 10). `a_hanging_run_sleeps_until_killed` reads the first stdout line with a blocking `BufReader::read_line` and asserts it equals `MSG:5015,0,0,"Saving 1 titles"\n`, sleeps about 100 to 200 ms, asserts `try_wait()` is None, kills and waits, then asserts the rest of the pipe is empty. Why: a fixed 500 ms wait before reading is racy on a slow first start of a freshly copied executable (an on-access scan). Cost if wrong: one test.
- PF-16 (Tasks 11, 16, 22, 25). Expected compile-error lists in red steps are examples: the run must fail to compile for the missing items, whichever the compiler names first. Task 16 adds the `pub use chain::{..}` line in Step 7, not Step 5. Task 22 declares its modules in Step 1, with `#[cfg(any(test, feature = "testkit"))] pub mod testkit;` and the owner's `mod tests;`, as Tasks 21, 23, 24 and 25 do. Why: some listed errors named items the named file never imports, and Task 22's compile error could not occur as written. Cost if wrong: none.
- PF-17 (Tasks 15, 16). Task 15 defines `status_word` as `#[cfg(test)] pub(crate) fn status_word<T>(..)`, with a comment saying its callers are its own tests. Task 16's chain writes the status words from `LookupState`, as its ruling says, and never calls `status_word`. Why: otherwise the non-test build warns that `status_word` is never used, from Task 15's commit on. Cost if wrong: none.
- PF-18 (Task 16). In `run_round` item 4, the `tmdb movie/<id>` status line is keyed by `answers.record`'s identity while `Record` is `Answered` with a record, and by `record_to_fetch` over the book otherwise (InFlight, Pending, NotAsked). The tests' final status lines are unchanged. Why: in the late-TheDiscDB scenario the status saved before the refetch would pair the new key with the old fetch's state. Cost if wrong: one status line in one scenario.
- PF-19 (Task 16). In `the_status_line_is_000_while_a_request_is_in_flight`, a drop guard holding a clone of the `Gate` opens it on drop (or the observations are copied out and the gate opened before any assertion, the deadline assertion included); the round thread then always finishes, and a failing assertion fails instead of hanging the scope. Why: `Gate` has no timeout, and `thread::scope` joins the blocked round thread after an unwinding assertion. Cost if wrong: one test.
- PF-20 (Tasks 18, 19). Task 18 writes `subtitle_events`'s doc ending "Def: phase 1, rewritten over `subtitle_events_of` in T18. Use: `subtitle::labels::row14` (retune --subtitles)." Why: Task 19 deletes decide's call, and the function predates T18. Cost if wrong: none.
- PF-21 (Tasks 19, 20, 27, 29). Line citations are located by content, not number: Task 19's reviews build is decide.rs:253-261 and the decision set :277-284; Task 20's `recorded_answers` is plan.rs:280, and HELP closes at decide.rs:85; Task 27's `Args` doc starts at lib.rs:82; Task 29's `is_nav_file` is `lazymkv_core::snapshot::is_nav_file` in crates/core/src/snapshot.rs (Task 12 deletes capture's disc.rs). Why: several citations were off by a few lines, and one named a deleted file. Cost if wrong: none.
- PF-22 (Tasks 22, 24). `Msg` in owner.rs is `pub(crate) enum Msg` (its variants, Task 24's `RoundDone` included, inherit that), and arrival.rs's `spawn_arrival` and `spawn_recording` take `Sender<Msg>`. Why: a Rust-private enum cannot be named from the sibling module. Cost if wrong: none.
- PF-23 (Tasks 22, 26). Task 22's `a_second_drives_disc_queues_and_starts_at_the_first_jobs_ejected` matches with `let Some(Waiting::Arrival(found)) = next else { panic!(..) }; assert_eq!(found.drive, "tok1");`, which keeps compiling when Task 26 adds `Waiting` variants. Why: an exhaustive match over `Waiting` would break (E0004) when Task 26 grows the enum, and Task 26's files omit owner/tests.rs. Cost if wrong: none.
- PF-24 (Task 23). Task 23 Step 4's two "the message above" references mean the tests' texts: `the index guard found <key> moved three times` (4.7.4) and `<tool file stem> is version <found>, below makemkv_min_version <setting>` (4.4.6). Why: the referents were missing from the step text. Cost if wrong: none.
- PF-25 (Task 23). In clone_values.rs only the `for (whence, value) in values.values() { .. }` loop moves into `fn holders(..) -> Vec<String>`; each test keeps its own `clone_values` call, its non-empty assertion and its final assertion with its own wording. Why: the brief named a wider range than must move. Cost if wrong: none.
- PF-26 (Tasks 24, 25). RP-7's VerifyFile-in-Remuxing change (set `TitleRecord.filed` to the final path and the partial's size, mtime 0) is Task 25's, made in its Step 4 and covered by its steps.rs entry. Task 24 does not make it; its File step records `FiledFile` itself. Why: RP-7's text misattributed the change to Task 24. Cost if wrong: none.
- PF-27 (Tasks 24, 25). S74's read-back runs when `RealSteps` holds no in-memory probe results for (job, key), and it applies RP-4: a `PlanEntry::Results` in the read-back inputs has its `.plan` replaced by the job record's persisted plan before `decide_doc`, as `engine_decide_inputs` does; a `Skipped` plan stays as read. Why: Task 24 holds no `DecideInputs` per (job, key), and without RP-4 a Decide after a restart would decide from the folder's fresh plan. Cost if wrong: none.
- PF-28 (Task 26). `start` runs the MakeMKV check (`find_tool`, the drive listing into `<data>/selftest/drive-list.txt`, `MakeMkvState::observe`) to completion before the IPC server accepts its first client. Why: two tests read the check from the first snapshot. Cost if wrong: start takes one drive listing longer.
- PF-29 (Task 26). During a forced shutdown, after the let-finish step's event is fed and persisted (with its Notify actions), the owner starts nothing new: no slot grant or FilePhaseSlot feed, no disc-phase token hand-off, no queued delivery and no new step. It then replies `ok`, closes the clients and stops. Why: otherwise a (FilePhaseSlot, Inspecting) transition breaks the shutdown test's last assertion, and a Probe starts during shutdown. Cost if wrong: none.
- PF-30 (Task 14). Task 14's three debug-log tests use `crate::testutil::temp_dir("debug-key" | "debug-old" | "debug-missing")` and define no `scratch`. Why: Task 11's helper exists for every tools unit test. Cost if wrong: none.
- PF-31 (Task 26). engine_ipc.rs uses `h.job_ids()`, `h.transitions(&id)` and `h.record(&id)` and defines no free `job_ids`, `transitions` or `record`; the then-unused `Transition` import is dropped. Why: Task 22's Harness already offers them and reads the same files, and the free `job_ids` would also list folders without `state.json`. Cost if wrong: none.
- PF-32 (Tasks 27, 30). Abbreviations of `--job`, `--jobs` and `--title` belong to ctl (so `--t` is `--title`, not dev's `--text`); accepted for PR 2 and listed under "Left for later pull requests" below. Why: ctl's parser declares those options, and the divergence is only in abbreviated spellings. Cost if wrong: a user typing `--t` for `--text` sees exit 2.
- PF-33 (Task 28). Task 28 appends a "commands sent to the engine (design part 9.6):" section to Task 27's `USAGE`, one indented line per `ENGINE_COMMANDS` synopsis. Task 27's tests check a prefix and one substring, so they still pass. Why: otherwise `lazymkvctl --help` would list only plan, decide and retune. Cost if wrong: none.
- PF-34 (Task 28). In Task 28's `peer` helper, the closure is written `let send = |..|`, with no `mut`. Why: the closure captures nothing, and `let mut` warns. Cost if wrong: none.

### Rulings made during execution

The controller of the plan's execution made these while the tasks ran, mostly on the reviews of each task.

**Task 4**
- The review's Minor 1 is promoted to one fix round: ustar's tests gain `a_longer_earlier_entry_is_skipped_to_the_next_header`. Why: a dropped 512-byte rounding of a non-empty skipped entry would pass every test of the brief, and the rounding matters in production, where the profile is rarely the first entry. Cost if wrong: one extra test.

**Task 7**
- T7-c1. A `settings_set` whose payload is null or absent is refused with "the settings_set payload is not an object". Why: the brief's step 6 says `settings_set` takes an object as changes, and only every other type reads null as `{}`; `lazymkvctl` always sends an object. Cost if wrong: a one-line change to read null as no changes.
- T7-c2. A payload that is neither null nor an object is refused, on the other nine types that take a payload, with "the <type> payload is not an object", since serde would otherwise accept arrays positionally. Cost if wrong: none.
- T7-c3. A malformed `problems` list in an error reply is an Err from `read_reply`, not silently dropped. Cost if wrong: none.
- T7-c4. `Push` carries `#[allow(clippy::large_enum_variant)]` with a reason comment, since boxing would change the variants the interface sheet fixes. Cost if wrong: none.

**Task 8**
- The review's two documentation Minors (the "sorted" promise of `DriveWatcher::present` belongs to the fake; the `same_volume` doc read as a live link) ride along in the documentation fix round of its two Importants. Why: same files, same kind of edit, one commit. Cost if wrong: none.

**Task 9**
- Hard links on the network-attached storage (NAS) share need no probe in PR 2. Why: PR 2 never writes to the NAS (stand-ins only), and PR 3 replaces the rename with `MoveFileExW` before any share run. Cost if wrong: none in PR 2.

**Task 11**
- The fix round for the review's Important (a panic in `on_line` or `on_output` left `done` unset, so `run_recorded` hung with the tool running) also takes the two robustness Minors of the same function (a file error after the spawn left the child running; a stderr read error, or a failed kill after a stdout tee error, could stall) and corrects the `after_kill` doc: it is S56's pause after any kill, for Task 13 to use. Why: one drop guard that kills the child and sets `done` on any early exit closes all three, in one function and one commit. Cost if wrong: a few lines.

**Task 13**
- T13-c1. `a_scan_past_its_limit_is_killed_and_fails_as_unknown` uses a `limits.scan` of 5 s instead of the brief's 300 ms. Why: a freshly copied stand-in took 0.28 to 1.25 s to start under the parallel Windows run and was killed before it wrote its counter, so the test flaked although the verdict was right. Cost if wrong: one test takes about 5 s.
- T13-c2. The driver also sleeps `after_kill` when `run_recorded` returns Err, because the recorder kills on its error paths without saying so (S56 kept). Cost if wrong: a spawn failure is reported `after_kill` later (10 s in production).
- T13-c3. `Kill::Cancel` means the recorder killed for the cancel; a late cancel gives `Kill::None`, and the summary decides.
- T13-c4. A rip refused by S55 returns an empty `RunResult`.
- T13-c5. `drive_index` keeps the exact match of the drive ID (Review Focus 2).
- Carried to Task 23: it may make `SCAN_PAST_LIMIT` public (a one-line visibility change in tools) instead of copying the string; the rip needles add a rip-only token (the title folder `t00-00800`) on top of PF-2's root naming; after a Done scan it checks its own cancel token.

**Task 16**
- T16-r1. Two divergences between the engine's lookup book and dev's readers are accepted for PR 2, under design part 12.7 and RP-4 (differences in a lookup-pending job are accepted, and `compare-job` in PR 6 explains them): (a) a dischash 200 whose body parses as no disc is `Miss` in the book (S46, which the design binds) and is recorded truthfully as `dischash 200` with its body, although dev's `hit_rate::lookup` reads that as failed or pending; (b) a search left pending writes `search 000`, which dev's plan reader does not read, so dev sees TMDb as not pending while the book says pending. PR 4 aligns dev's lookup readers with S46 and teaches them the `search` word. Why: changing dev's plan reader is outside PR 2's files, and both cases are rare (a 200 body that is not a disc; a job that reaches Plan with the search still pending). Cost if wrong: a dev `plan` over such a job folder shows a different `lookup_pending` than the engine's plan until PR 4.

**Task 21**
- T21-c1. Two extra store tests (RP-8, and the load order of titles and notes) are kept; Task 30 counts from a run.
- T21-c2. RP-8's newline repair in `Store::persist` landed in Task 21, so Task 25's store edit for RP-8 had nothing left to do, and its harness test passed on the first run.
- T21-c3. Format refusals are prefixed with the file's path, like the store's other errors.
- T21-c4. A missing `--data` is reported before leftover words, and a jobs folder that cannot be listed is one Err.
- T21-c5. A private write lock makes `Store` the one writer per path.

**Task 22**
- R22.7 (design over plan). MediaArrived goes only to a disc-phase job with `job.disc == None` (or Failed with `MediaReadError`); a repeat arrival for a job whose disc is present feeds nothing, logs `<drive>: the disc of job <id> is already in <drive>` and records `handled: true`. Why: the brief's item 4 dropped design part 5.4 step 3's "waits for its disc", and could start a second concurrent rip. Cost if wrong: a second copy of a disc in another drive is ignored while the first is ripping.
- R22.8. A same-state transition into Failed or a waiting state whose event brings no message keeps the old message; Tasks 24 to 26 assert against it.
- R22.9. `Harness::restart` hands queued drive events over after the old engine stops (amends R22.1 for restart).
- R22.10. `stop` does not join arrival threads still waiting for a volume; they only read, and a late send hits a closed channel. It is documented as the exception.
- R22.11. A step never posts after its `CancelToken` is raised, because the outbox is keyed by job only.
- The review's Minor 7 (a failed first persist left an unpersisted Detected job in memory with `handled: true`) joins the fix round. Why: the fix touches the same failure path.

**Task 23**
- `ChainInputs` carrying the remembered identity would change the clients interface outside PR 2's remaining files, so it is parked for the final review and PR 4. Why: no PR 2 scenario carries a remembered identity, and P11 holds because dev reads the same files. Cost if wrong: a disc whose remembered identity differs from the search's top result plans without a TMDb record for its own identity until PR 4.

**Task 24**
- R24.c1. `Harness::settle` gains a busy-aware wait: the owner publishes a busy flag (a step or a lookup round outstanding) through the crate-private `EngineHandle::busy()`, and settle needs 300 ms of quiet when idle and waits up to 2 s while busy. Why: starting a freshly copied stand-in on Windows blocks 260 to 480 ms, longer than the old 300 ms quiet window, which made `the_eject_fake_failure...` flaky in the parallel suite. Cost if wrong: settle beside a deliberately hanging step takes up to 2 s.
- R24.c2 (design part 6.4 and spec 7 over the plan's single re-render). After the second render, if its path differs from the first, the library facts are computed again for the new path and the decision rendered once more while they change (it converges at once). Why: the re-render can change the path (the edition name), and a collision measured on the first path only is found only at the rename, after a full remux. Cost if wrong: one extra render.
- R24.c3 (design part 8.3). When a lookup round could not run and the job is in Ejected, the engine decides by the book's own `pending()`, waiting if lookups are pending and proceeding otherwise, instead of always proceeding.
- The review's Minor 3 joins the fix round: `busy` is cleared at the end of `shut_down`, and `EngineHandle::busy`'s doc is corrected.

**Task 25**
- R25.c1 (design part 5.10 over Step 4's L1a wording). In `plan_restart` a drive is an arrival, whatever `drives.json` says, when its fingerprint belongs to a disc-phase job that was fed `disc_present: false`. Why: otherwise a job whose disc sits in a handled drive waits forever. Cost if wrong: one extra MediaArrived that the reducer absorbs.
- R25.c2 (design part 5.8 over `held_promise` and S75). The restart's promise block and `promise_on_return` also rebuild the Remux promise (`planned_remux_promise(plan)` on the plan kind's library volume) for jobs in Ripping, RipVerified, Ejected, Inspecting or Decided. Cost if wrong: a library check undercounts after a restart.
- The fix round also runs the full workspace once (the final tree had only had the engine_restart run) and adds a stop-during-restart test, since the harness can arm the hook before the restart (S70).

**Task 26**
- T26-c1. `cancel_during_a_rip...` waits for `<job>/t00-00800/rip.txt` before cancelling. Why: the Ripping push precedes the rip step's drive listing, so a cancel could land before the rip started. Cost if wrong: one wait line in a test.
- R26.c1 (design part 3.7, "a self-test starts no second makemkvcon", and L2). The MakeMKV check takes the disc-phase token for its listing and holds it until the listing ends; when the token is held, the check reports the last check marked busy (S79) and takes nothing; while the check holds the token, no step that runs `makemkvcon` is handed out. Why: the review found the self-test's own `makemkvcon info disc:9999` was not kept apart from a job's `makemkvcon` start, so S55's check in a concurrent RunScan or RipTitle saw it and failed the step as Unknown. Cost if wrong: a self-test during a rip reports busy instead of a fresh version.
- The fix round also keeps the running check's `CancelToken` in the owner and raises it in `shut_down` and at a forced shutdown, so a listing stuck on a drive cannot hang a stop. It takes Minor 3 (the Java self-test test waits for Filed before its second `run_selftest`, so no two stand-ins of one tool overlap) and Minor 5 (AwaitingShare's re-check requires the roots the return state uses, staging only for a Ripping return, as S81 says).

**Task 27**
- T27-r1. The review's one Important is folded into Task 28's dispatch instead of a separate fix round: ctl's `main.rs` takes dev's lossy `args_os` form; `retune_spec` calls `.rest()?` after `flag("--spec")?`, so an ambiguous retune prefix exits 2 through ctl too (T28-h); `argument_error` gets its Def/Use tail. Why: `std::env::args()` panics on an argument that is not Unicode (exit 101) where dev's main reads it lossily, which breaks P23's identical exit codes on that input, and Task 28 rewrites the same crate. Cost if wrong: none.

**Task 28**
- T28-r1. The review's Minor 3 is promoted to one test-only fix round: the scripted-peer unit tests kept `connector` alive until `server.join()`, so a regression where ctl never connects would hang the test, and CI, instead of failing; `drop(connector)` now comes before each `server.join()`, so `accept` fails with BrokenPipe. Why: a hung CI job costs more than a failing one. Cost if wrong: none.

**Task 29**
- R29.c1. The characterisation test compares the stored document and decide's output as parsed JSON, structurally: every value that is not a number exactly, and numbers within 1e-12 of the larger of their magnitudes, which is an absolute 1e-12 below magnitude 1 (the C library's last-digit differences are about 1e-16); on a difference it names the first differing JSON path. The stored files are not regenerated. Why: the first CI run failed it on ubuntu and macos on `disc-a-eng-only-text.json`, and K5 pins the renderer's structure and values, not the C library's last digit. Cost if wrong: a last-digit float regression in the renderer goes unseen by the characterisation test.
- R29.c1a (amends R29.c1). Under `cfg(windows)` the characterisation keeps the byte comparison `printed == stored`, since the files were generated on Windows and passed byte for byte on windows-latest; elsewhere the structural comparison applies, with an integer-versus-float type difference counted as a difference. Why: the review found that the structural comparison let an integer and a float of the same value pass, and that nothing pinned the made-up fixtures' bytes any more. Cost if wrong: none.
- R29.c2. The testkit's time bounds (`Harness::wait`'s 10 s, `run_until`'s cap and the client's 20 s line wait) scale by 4 when the `CI` environment variable is set, as GitHub sets it; settle's quiet windows stay. Why: windows-latest failed two file-phase tests at the 10 s bound. Cost if wrong: a test that really hangs takes four times longer to fail on CI.
- R29.c2 stays although it was not the cause of those failures, which was that the lookup-timer tests fired the timer before the owner had started it (fixed in `cf01d2e` without changing any assertion). Why: a slow-runner factor is harmless and defensible. `ci_factor` is public because the IPC client lives in another crate.
- R29.c2a. `LOOKUP_WAIT_CAP` scales by `ci_factor` too, and `Harness::fire_timers` waits, scaled, until at least one timer is pending, and panics with a named message if none comes, so the lookup-timer race is loud for every future caller. Why: otherwise a slow runner could turn whole_job's direct line into an AwaitingLookup path.
- R29.c2b (revises the first part of R29.c2a). The `LOOKUP_WAIT_CAP` scaling is reverted, so it stays 5 s; `fire_timers`'s pending wait and the rest of R29.c2 stay. Why: `run_recorded`'s time-limit clock starts before the spawn, so the 20 s lookup wait under CI outlasted the 10 s scan limit and failed `another_discs_listing_with_a_changed_version_returns_a_job_from_awaiting_makemkv_when_the_token_is_free` on all three systems; the scaling was a precaution for a slow-runner path that had not happened. Cost if wrong: on a very slow runner whole_job's direct line could become an AwaitingLookup path, and the ruling is then revisited.
- R29.c3. Every stand-in test helper that executes a freshly copied `fake-tool` retries the spawn on `ErrorKind::ExecutableFileBusy`, up to about 20 times 50 ms apart, in one shared helper where the tests already share one. Why: on Linux a test that copies `fake-tool` and executes it while another thread forks can meet `ETXTBSY` ("text file busy"), because the child briefly holds the copy's write handle; the race is in the tests' copy-then-execute pattern, not in the code under test, a rerun would hide it, and hard links are not available on every CI file system. Cost if wrong: a few lines of test code.
- R29.c4. The `ETXTBSY` retry lives in test support code only: `testkit::BusyRetry`, a `ProcessGuard` wrapper in the engine's testkit that retries `spawn` on `ExecutableFileBusy` up to 20 times 50 ms apart, used by `TestGuard::spawn` and by the three driver test files. `platform::StdProcessGuard` and `tools::run_recorded` stay unchanged, because production copies no executables. Cost if wrong: test-support lines only.
- R29.c5. `wait_for_call`'s 20 s, and any other unscaled wait in the fake-tools engine tests, scales by `ci_factor()` like the harness bounds (R29.c2). Why: two engine_ipc tests failed at that wait on a heavily loaded windows runner. Cost if wrong: a stalled test fails after 80 s instead of 20 s on CI.

**The final review** (the review of the whole branch, with its fix wave)
- I1 (design part 5.3). A disc pulled while its RunScan or RipTitle runs hands the disc-phase token to the sentinel `KILLED_HOLDER` until the killed step has reported; the stale step's report frees it and routes the queue. Why: the after-kill pause runs inside the killed step, and the token used to pass to the next disc at once, whose `makemkvcon` could then start beside the killed one or fail S55's check as Unknown. Cost if wrong: a queued disc waits the pause, 10 s in production. Test: `a_pulled_discs_killed_step_holds_the_token_until_it_reports`.
- I2 (design part 5.2). A step that panics feeds its failure, class Unknown, with the panic's text: `cancel_event`'s row for its main action, with EjectFailed for an Eject and ShareLost for a CheckSpace. Why: a panic used to leave its job in a working state with no step, holding the file-phase slot, KeepAwake and its space promise for ever, with `cancel` and `retry` refused. A no-event result that did not panic still only gives the token back, so the testkit's unscripted steps park jobs as before. Cost if wrong: a step bug fails its job instead of stalling it. Tests: `a_panicking_step_fails_its_job_and_gives_the_token_back` (the pinned test, updated and renamed) and `a_panicking_remux_fails_the_job_frees_the_slot_and_retry_resumes_it`. Amended at review round 1 (V11, RR1-12): ShareLost is fed for a panicking CheckSpace only when its state takes no failure row (Planned); in Decided the state's own row, `cancel_event`'s DecisionFailed, is fed and the job reaches `Failed { Unknown }`, freeing the slot (`failure_event`).
- Task 22's escalated minor. A no-event result neither fails its job nor gives the token back while another step of the job runs (one a posted event handed out). Why: with I2 the path feeds a failure, which must never hit a newer step. Cost if wrong: none.
- I3 (spec 10.3, L1a). At a restart, in each noted drive no job's disc is in, the disc's latest job has its disc noted there when it failed in its disc phase, and that drive becomes its record's drive. Why: the disc stays in after such a failure so that Retry can use it, but a restart left the job's disc unnoted, so a Retry persisted Scanned with no Plan, fed a MediaRemoved that never happened and waited for a pull and a reinsertion. Cost if wrong: none found. Test: `a_retry_after_a_restart_plans_again_with_the_disc_left_in`.
- I4 (S73 widened, design part 8.4). Every loaded job but a Filed or Discarded one has its book rebuilt at load (see S73). Tests: `a_retry_after_a_restart_plans_with_the_saved_answers` and `a_retry_after_a_restart_decides_with_the_saved_late_answer`. The first asserts the retried plan's identity equals the plan's before the restart; no scenario has a TheDiscDB answer that turns a copy plan into a direct one.
- I5 (S83 narrowed, design part 3.8). A forced shutdown keeps the partial files of a job whose cancelled step was File (see S83). Test: `a_forced_shutdown_during_file_keeps_the_verified_partial`.
- I6 (spec 9.2, design part 12.2). Two driver tests pin the rip's "the rip ended without a success summary" verdict: output cut before `MSG:5036` by `stop_after_line`, and one `.mkv` on disk with exit 0 but no `MSG:5036`. Dropping the `title_saved` check fails the second. The stand-in cannot write a partial file and exit with a non-zero code in one response.
- M1 (S57). `run_makemkv` gives `run_recorded` no limit; its watcher kills at the limit only while no final message has come, so a scan that printed `MSG:5011` and `TCOUNT` and then hangs is the exit wait's, "did not exit", and is never reported past its limit and scanned again. The limit's clock still starts before the spawn (R29.c2b). Test: `a_scan_that_hangs_after_its_final_message_is_killed_by_the_exit_wait`.
- M2 (design part 5.3, spec 10.4). A job's drive listing that the exit wait killed fails its step as Unknown with `EXIT_WAIT_PASSED`, now public. Test: `a_drive_listing_killed_by_the_exit_wait_fails_the_scan`. The self-test's MakeMKV check does not read it yet (below, PR 3).
- M3. `cancel` picks the worker of the job's current generation, or one an earlier `cancel` is stopping, and refuses with "no running step" when only a stale step is listed. Why: it could pick a pulled disc's killed step and leave a newer step running with its result dropped. Test: `a_cancel_while_only_a_pulled_discs_killed_step_runs_is_refused`.
- M7. The engine binary reads its arguments lossily, as dev and ctl do, so an argument that is not Unicode exits 1 instead of panicking with 101. Test: `an_argument_that_is_not_unicode_is_read_lossily`. Amended at review round 1 of pull request 3 (R5), below.
- M8 (design part 9.4). `settings_set` merges object-valued keys one level deep, as the testkit does (R22.5), so naming one tool path keeps the other four. Test: `settings_set_of_one_tool_path_keeps_the_others`.
- M10 (S79 narrowed). A listing that printed no version is not observed (see S79). Test: `a_listing_with_no_version_is_not_observed`.
- M12. The self-test's library-root probe fails `<root> is not a folder` for a root that does not exist, and makes only the partial folder inside an existing root. Test: `a_missing_library_root_fails_its_check_and_is_not_created`.
- M13. `Harness::drop` panics when stopping the engine reports a panicked engine thread, unless the test is already panicking.
- M17 and M18. The docs of `SelfTestCheck.found`, `Snapshot.problems`, `HISTORY_PAGE`, `DiscMeta`'s and `PacketsFile`'s fields and the tools crate's three public modules now say what the code does, and the Use lists of `LICENCE_CODES`, `CANCELLED`, `JOB_PROFILE_FILE` and `FALLBACK_PROFILE_FILE` name their real users.
- M21, M22 and M23. The characterisation test has no write mode any more, and its panic text says the files are never regenerated (R29.c1); `DecideInputs.answers` names `replay_answers` (S49) as its source; the test's doc says that its Windows leg compares bytes after `masked`, which writes path separators as `/`.
- M25 to M28 (tests). The tool-level kill tests have 30 s backstops so a regressed kill fails instead of hanging CI; three "nothing happens" assertions settle the engine first; the IPC test client writes a line and its newline in one write; the progress test checks the title and the job.
- M31 and M32 (docs). `README.md` and `corpus/README.md` describe the stand-in and the scenario family, and this file expands its acronyms at first use and words the characterisation tolerance exactly.
- N1 (the re-review of the fix wave, design part 5.3). The token `KILLED_HOLDER` holds is freed after every worker's report, not only a stale one's, once no `makemkvcon` step is listed, and after the job's own result is handled. Why: a disc pulled while a cancelled rip paused after its kill passed the token to `KILLED_HOLDER`, and the cancelled step's report, which feeds the cancel's failure, never freed it, so every later arrival, Retry and return queued and every self-test reported MakeMKV busy until a restart. The release comes after the result is handled so that a queued reinsertion of the same disc never resumes the job before its failure is fed. Test: `a_disc_pulled_during_a_cancelled_rip_frees_the_token_once_the_rip_reports`.
- N2 (the re-review). The guard on a no-event result counts only a step of the job's current generation, so a pulled disc's stale step no longer keeps the token with nothing running after a quick reinsertion. Test: `a_no_event_step_gives_the_token_back_beside_a_stale_step_of_its_job`.
- Not taken from the review's minors, as the fix list leaves them: M4 to M6, M9, M11, M14 to M16, M19, M20, M24, M29 and M30; the PR 3 and PR 4 ones are listed below.

The owner may overturn any of them.

### Review round 1 (the review of pull request #6)

The fifteen findings of the first review of pull request #6 (V1 to V15), each verified against the code; the rulings
below are the controller's of 2026-10-27, labelled RR1-1 to RR1-15 beside the finding each decides, and RR1-16, on the
serious finding of the round's final review (S1), beside V1, whose fix it completes. The final review's minor findings
(N1, C1 to C7) are folded into the entries they touch. Every amendment is recorded in this file only; the spec and the
design text stay untouched, as the plan and the final review left them (RR1-15).

- V1 (spec 10.1, design part 7.3; I2 extended; S70 step 6, S72 and S78 amended). A transition of an existing job that cannot be persisted no longer strands the job. `persist_transition` writes its engine.log line and gives the token back as before, then calls `unpersisted`: unless a forced shutdown or a stop runs, or a step of the job's current generation runs (Task 22's escalated minor), the failure row of the job's state (`failure_event` with no tag, V11) is fed with `the <event> transition into <state> could not be persisted: <error>` and persisted as usual, so the job reaches `Failed { Unknown }`, releases its promises, the token and its file-phase place, is notified, and can be retried. RR1-1: when that failure cannot be persisted either, no in-memory state is published (spec 10.1, R22.3, S84): the job keeps its persisted state with no step, its promises are released, it leaves its file-phase place, which is granted again, and engine.log says `<id>: keeps its persisted state <state> until a restart resumes it`; `retry` and `cancel` refuse it until then. A state with no failure row gets nothing fed: a FilePhaseSlot whose transition cannot be persisted is undone in `grant_next`, the job going to the back of the queue to be granted at the next grant, and a Retry or approval delivered with the slot leaves the queue with the job.log line `<event> was not delivered: its transition could not be persisted`, so a later `retry` or approval queues it again; a head already tried in one pass ends the pass, so no refused grant is tried in a loop. RR1-5: the restart seeds the slot only with the planned holders whose in-memory state still holds a place, so a job whose `EngineRestart` could not be persisted, and was then failed, holds none. RR1-4: a cancel whose failure transition could not be persisted is answered with the error `the step of <job> was cancelled, but its failure could not be persisted; a restart resumes it`, not `ok`. RR1-3: when state.json was replaced but the transitions line could not be appended or flushed, `Store::persist` gives `Persisted::LogLineLost`; the transition counts as persisted, its actions run, and engine.log says `<id>: the transitions line of <event> into <state> was lost: <error>`. RR1-2: on Windows the state.json replace is tried again on os errors 5, 32 and 33, and the format check's read before it on os errors 32 and 33, so a holder that shares no reading is waited for too (the final review's C2), the whole at most 5 times 100 ms apart, and the transitions append never is; the retry stays when pull request 3 swaps in `MoveFileExW`; it is the engine's first production retry loop, and R29.c3 and R29.c4 are untouched. `Store::persist`'s errors, the lost line's included, name their file in the store's `<path>: <reason>` form, the path added after the retry has read the raw OS error, so the failure's message reads `the <event> transition into <state> could not be persisted: <path>: <error>` (the final review's C3); the testkit's injected faults name none. RR1-16 (the final review's S1): a step's posted record edits land only in the write of the transition they came with (R22.3). `step_done` no longer applies them to the job's record before it feeds; they travel with the feed in `Job::edits`, and `persist_transition` applies them to its copy of the record, before the RecordNote and RefreshIdentity record actions, so a persisted transition installs them and one that could not be persisted drops them before `unpersisted` feeds the failure row, which is written over the persisted record. They go with the first transition the step's report feeds, a posted event's or else the result's, as they did, so every reader of the record sees what it saw before whenever the persist succeeds; edits no transition took, because every event was rejected or the step reported none, are dropped, so a panic's failure carries none. A lost transition's posted edits are therefore dropped with it: a lost `TitleVerified { last: true }` leaves the title unverified, so the Retry rips it again, where before this ruling the failure was persisted with `verified: true` and the Retry's RipTitle found no title left, stranding the job in Ripping with no step for good; and a lost first `TitleVerifyFailed` is not counted (V12: the count follows the persisted record). `feed` and `commit` now return whether the transition was persisted. The testkit's `Harness::refuse_persists`, `lose_log_lines` and `allow_persists` drive a `PersistFault` the store asks before each write, under `cfg(any(test, feature = "testkit"))`, passed through `start_until`'s `TestHooks` beside the stop hook. Why: one refused rename of state.json, or a full data volume, left a Remuxing job holding the only file-phase slot with no step and KeepAwake held, with `retry` and `cancel` refused and every later disc stopped at Ejected, and a restart, which seeded the slot from the plan alone, reproduced it; a grant or a slot delivery that could not be persisted stalled the slot the same way. Cost if wrong: a transient disk fault fails a job, which a Retry resumes, and the owner thread waits up to about 0.85 s on a state.json another process holds (0.4 s before T3-4-a's own tries inside each replace; amended in pull request 3 by PF3-S4-6-2). Tests: `a_remux_result_whose_failure_persists_fails_the_job_and_frees_the_slot`, `a_remux_result_and_its_failure_that_cannot_be_persisted_free_the_slot_until_a_restart`, `a_file_phase_slot_grant_that_cannot_be_persisted_lets_the_next_job_have_the_slot`, `a_retry_whose_slot_delivery_cannot_be_persisted_leaves_the_slot_free`, `a_restart_whose_engine_restart_cannot_be_persisted_holds_no_slot` (here rather than in `engine_restart.rs`, for its IPC client) and `a_cancel_whose_failure_cannot_be_persisted_is_answered_with_the_error` (`engine_ipc.rs`); `a_rip_result_that_cannot_be_persisted_fails_the_job_and_gives_the_token_back` and `a_transition_whose_log_line_is_lost_still_runs_its_actions` (`owner/tests.rs`); `persist_errors_name_the_file_they_met` and, Windows only, `persist_retries_a_state_json_another_process_holds_open`, now also with a holder that shares no reading (`store.rs`); for RR1-16, over the real steps, `a_lost_last_verification_is_not_persisted_with_its_failure_and_retry_rips_again` and `a_lost_second_verify_failure_is_not_counted_and_retry_rips_again` (`engine_ipc.rs`, scenarios `movie-direct` and `movie-direct-verify-twice`; both fail without the ruling).
- V2 (I3's precedent; design part 5.4 step 3 kept; R22.7 untouched). A waiting job's disc noted in another drive makes that drive its record's drive (`Job::note_disc`, which the MediaArrived arm and I3's noting now use too), written with the job's next transition; nothing is fed and no token is taken, as design part 5.4 step 3 says. At a restart, every fed job that may still run a disc-phase step (`restart::reads_drive`: a disc-phase state, or a wait returning to one) takes the drive its disc is in as its record's drive, written with its `EngineRestart` line; a file-phase job keeps the drive it ripped in, which its notifications and summary name. `Owner::restart`'s doc step 3 is amended. Why: the drive listing that picks `makemkvcon`'s `disc:` index, the Eject and the ejecting mark read the record's drive, so a disc moved during a wait was listed, scanned, ripped and ejected in the old drive: with that drive empty the job failed on every Retry until a restart, and with another waiting disc in it, that disc was ripped under the job's identity and ejected. Cost if wrong: none found. Tests: `a_waiting_jobs_disc_moved_to_another_drive_becomes_its_records_drive` and `a_restart_makes_a_waiting_jobs_moved_disc_drive_its_records_drive` (`owner/tests.rs`), `only_a_job_that_may_still_run_a_disc_phase_step_reads_the_drive` (`restart.rs`) and `a_waiting_jobs_disc_moved_to_the_other_drive_is_scanned_ripped_and_ejected_there` (`engine_ipc.rs`).
- V3 (design part 5.2, K9 kept; S54 and S75 extended; R23.4 narrowed). RR1-6: (a) the engine's `mkv_files` reports every listing error of a title folder, a missing folder included, as `<folder>: <error>`, so Probe and Remux fail Unknown naming the folder and the OS error instead of "holds 0 .mkv files, not one"; (b) the Decided check reads the staged sizes with `space::staged_bytes` before it locks the promises and, when they cannot be read, feeds ShareLost only when `reach(staging)` finds staging unreachable (its sentence names staging, why, and the staging error, and no promise is touched), and otherwise checks and promises the rip check's estimate, `planned_remux_promise(plan)`, writing `check space: the staged files could not be read: <error>` in job.log; that sentence travels with SpaceOk, but a SpaceShort carries none, so AwaitingSpace's message and notification say what any shortage says, `waiting: space`, and the sentence stays a job.log line (`space_result`; the final review's N1); a plain ShareLost on any error would cycle through AwaitingShare, because S81's re-check tests only `reach`; (c) the restart and return rebuild of a Remuxing job's promise falls back to the same estimate (amended in pull request 3 by T3-17-a: the rebuild always takes the estimate and reads no staged file). The finding's "skip the promise" is not taken: design part 5.8 holds that a passed check means the job can finish. The AwaitingSpace re-check runs the same `staged_remux_check` (S81). Because `staged_bytes` needs the staging root, the Decided check now gives ShareLost `staging_root is unset` when it is unset, as the Planned check does; before, it summed 0 staged bytes and checked. A VerifyFile in Ripping that cannot list the folder is the entry "V3 with V9" below. The tools crate's `mkv_files`, which reads a missing folder as empty for the rip's own count, stays separate. Why: an unreadable folder read as empty gave a false "holds 0 .mkv files", a 0-byte rip-summary.json, and 0-byte promises that let a remux pass its check. Cost if wrong: a promise slightly above the real size while the staged files cannot be read. Tests: `mkv_files_lists_the_regular_mkv_files_sorted_in_any_case`, `a_missing_title_folder_is_an_error_naming_it` and `a_title_folder_that_is_a_file_is_an_error_naming_it` (`steps.rs`); `staged_bytes_sums_every_planned_titles_staged_file`, `an_unreadable_title_folder_gives_share_lost_only_when_staging_is_unreachable`, `a_rebuilt_remux_promise_is_the_plans_estimate_and_reads_no_staged_file` and `the_remux_step_replaces_the_estimate_with_the_staged_sizes` (`space.rs`, since Ruling T3-17-a of pull request 3, in place of `an_unreadable_staging_folder_rebuilds_the_remux_promise_from_the_plan`); `an_unreadable_title_folder_fails_the_probe_with_its_error`, `an_unreadable_title_folder_fails_the_remux_with_its_error`, `an_unreadable_title_folder_at_the_remux_check_uses_the_planned_promise` and, for N1, `an_unreadable_title_folder_at_a_short_remux_check_waits_naming_only_the_shortage` (`engine_file_phase.rs`); `an_unreachable_staging_at_the_remux_check_waits_for_the_share_and_retry_files_it` (`engine_ipc.rs`).
- V4 (design parts 3.8 and 5.6; M3 applied to `shutdown`; S83 amended). RR1-10: a forced shutdown cancels only the steps `cancel` would pick (`is_running_step`: the worker of the job's current generation, or one a cancel or a forced shutdown is stopping), and a plain `shutdown` counts only those, so a pulled disc's killed step is neither cancelled nor counted; the owner still waits for it, and its result is dropped as a stale one. This withdraws the item, accepted in pull request 2, that a plain stop is refused while such a step reports: design part 3.8 says waiting jobs do not count, and the refusal's "stop --force cancels it" would be false for a stale step. Why: the forced loop gave the stale step a cancel, so its report failed a job that only waited for its disc as `Failed { Unknown, from: Ripping }` with a false `failed` notification, and the disc inserted again started a new job that ripped every title again. Cost if wrong: none. Tests: `a_forced_shutdown_leaves_a_pulled_discs_job_waiting_for_its_disc`, `a_shutdown_without_force_is_accepted_while_only_a_pulled_discs_killed_step_runs` and `a_pulled_disc_reinserted_after_a_forced_shutdown_resumes_its_job` (`engine_ipc.rs`).
- V5 (spec 7, 10.1 and 10.2's last paragraph; design part 5.6 extended; PF-6 kept). RR1-8: RipTitle classes a failure to empty or make the title's staging folder with `classify_title_failure(share_healthy, out_of_space, true, false)` (`staging_write_failure`), reading the staging check once, after the failure, on the worker thread, so an unreachable or unwritable staging waits in AwaitingShare and a full one in AwaitingSpace, both returning to Ripping, with `<folder>: <error>` as the message and a waiting notification. This extends design part 5.6's "using only what is known when the process exits" to the emptying that precedes the run; EmptyTitleDirectory itself still returns no event (PF-6). Why: the failure was Unknown, and the disc inserted again then started a new job that ripped every title again (spec 6.1 step 2 leaves a disc-phase Unknown failure for Retry). Cost if wrong: a local cause, such as a file another program holds open in the folder, reads as a share wait until a Retry runs the emptying again. Tests: `a_title_folder_that_cannot_be_emptied_is_classed_by_the_share_check` (`steps.rs`), `a_title_folder_that_cannot_be_made_waits_in_awaiting_share` (`engine_disc_phase.rs`), `a_restart_in_ripping_with_the_staging_share_gone_waits_in_awaiting_share` (`engine_restart.rs`) and `a_rip_waiting_for_the_share_rips_again_after_retry` (`engine_ipc.rs`).
- V6 (design part 5.6 step 6, spec 10.2; I6 kept). A title folder that cannot be listed after a rip MakeMKV ended by itself is a failed title, classed like any other: `rip_outcome` reads the staging check and calls `classify_title_failure` (Output with the share down, out of space or a write error, MediaReadError with `2003`, else Unknown), and appends the listing error, naming the folder, to MakeMKV's verdict, which is "the title folder could not be listed (exit code N)" when MakeMKV reported the title saved. A folder that cannot be listed never gives `Ripped`. `rip` returns `Err` only for a run that could not be started or recorded, so the engine's "the rip could not run" now means exactly that; I6's messages are unchanged when the folder lists, and the exit-wait and cancel verdicts still come first (PIPE-3). Why: the listing error made `rip` return `Err`, so the engine fed `TitleRipFailed { Unknown }` with "the rip could not run", skipping the share check, the job.log exit line (only `ended <key>/rip.txt: <error>` was written) and, with `keep_tuning_evidence` on, the saved debug log. Cost if wrong: none. Tests: `a_rip_whose_title_folder_cannot_be_listed_is_classed_with_the_staging_share` (`makemkv_driver.rs`) and `a_title_folder_that_cannot_be_listed_reads_the_staging_health_once` (`crates/tools/src/makemkv.rs`).
- V7 (design part 6.1; design part 5.2 kept; spec 10.1's AwaitingShare is phase 3's target). RR1-9, option (a): File asks `reach` of each title's library root before it touches any path, and again before it reports a partial file gone; only a NotFound on a share that answers counts as a partial file gone or a final path holding no file. A root that does not answer gives `FileFailed { Unknown }` with `<key>: the library share holding <root> does not answer (<why>); the partial file <partial> was not checked or renamed, and a retry once the share answers files it`; any other metadata error on the partial file or the final path gives the same class naming the title, the path and the error, and says the path was not checked. Option (b), ShareLost so that the job waits in AwaitingShare as spec 10.1 says for a file-phase state, reverses design part 5.2's designer's call and is recorded as phase 3's target, not done here. Why: std reads Windows' ERROR_BAD_NETPATH and ERROR_BAD_NET_NAME as NotFound, so a `try_exists` check alone still says "gone"; and `reach` is the call that connects with the stored credential (design part 6.1), which neither File nor its Retry made, so where access depends on that credential every Retry failed the same way. Cost if wrong: one share probe per title, two when a partial file is gone. Tests: `file_asks_the_library_share_before_touching_the_partial` and, Unix only, `a_partial_that_cannot_be_read_fails_file_naming_the_error_not_gone` and `an_unreadable_final_folder_fails_file_naming_the_final_path_not_holds_no_file` (`engine_restart.rs`); `file_with_the_library_share_unreachable_fails_naming_the_share_and_a_retry_files_it` (`engine_ipc.rs`).
- V8 (spec 10.2, "Without mkvmerge no disc is started"). RunScan and RipTitle feed `ToolMissing { Mkvmerge }` when mkvmerge is missing, right after their makemkvcon check: RunScan before it lists the drives, starts the lookups or writes the job profile, RipTitle before it moves an attempt aside or empties the staging folder. VerifyFile's own check stays, for a tool lost during a rip. This adds to design part 5.2's RunScan row and to the PR 2 plan's Task 23 brief. S81's disc-phase re-check already needs mkvmerge, so a Retry returns the job only once mkvmerge is found. Why: the disc phase's first mkvmerge run was VerifyFile's, after a rip, and `AwaitingTool { Ripping }`'s return empties the title's folder, deleting that rip. Cost if wrong: none. Tests: `no_mkvmerge_waits_in_awaiting_tool_before_the_scan` and `mkvmerge_lost_before_the_rip_waits_without_ripping` (`engine_disc_phase.rs`), `a_job_waiting_for_mkvmerge_scans_once_a_retry_finds_it` (`engine_ipc.rs`); `a_makemkv_check_holds_the_disc_phase_token_until_its_listing_ends` now keeps mkvmerge set.
- V9 (design part 5.3, spec 6.2 step 7, design part 9.4's cancel table). In Ripping, a header read that fails (its time limit, an exit code other than 0 or 1, JSON that does not parse, a spawn error), a planned scan that cannot be read and a scan title whose streams cannot be selected give `TitleRipFailed { Unknown }` with their sentence, and job.log says `<key>: the rip's header read failed: <sentence>` or `<key>: the rip could not be verified: <sentence>`; `TitleVerifyFailed` is a mismatch or a wrong `.mkv` count only, and only it is counted (V12). This supersedes the PR 2 plan's Task 23 step 8 sentence and `verify_rip`'s old doc; design part 5.2's VerifyFile row gains `TitleRipFailed { Unknown }` in Ripping, as the cancel table already counts the step. Why: a tool error was counted as a mismatch and re-ripped with no notification, its sentence dropped from the record, and after a second one the title went on with an unreadable `mkvmerge.json`, so Probe or Decide failed on every Retry. Cost if wrong: a transient stall stops the job for a Retry, which re-rips, instead of re-ripping on its own. Tests: `an_ident_exit_2_in_ripping_fails_the_rip_as_unknown_without_a_rerip` (scenario `movie-direct-ident-error`) and `an_ident_past_the_header_limit_in_ripping_fails_the_rip_as_unknown` (`engine_disc_phase.rs`).
- V3 with V9 (RR1-7; design part 5.2 extended; R23.4 narrowed). VerifyFile in Ripping whose title folder cannot be listed writes no rip-summary.json, writes `<key>/rip-summary.json was not written: <error>` in job.log, and feeds `TitleRipFailed` classed by `unlisted_title_failure`, `classify_title_failure(share_healthy, out_of_space, false, false)`, with `<folder>: <error>`: a dropped or full staging waits in AwaitingShare or AwaitingSpace, returning to Ripping, and any other error fails Unknown; it is never counted as a verification failure. Design part 5.2's "only a rip is classed by the share check" extends to the rip's own verification step, which the cancel table already counts as TitleRipFailed. Why: the folder read as empty wrote a rip-summary.json with `ok: true`, `bytes: 0` and no files, and was counted as a failed verification, a re-rip. Cost if wrong: none found. Tests: `a_title_folder_verify_cannot_list_is_unknown_on_a_healthy_staging` (`steps.rs`, the final review's C1), which pins the classification, so a write error a healthy staging would wait on in AwaitingShare fails it; V3's `mkv_files` unit tests; no harness seam reaches between the rip's own listing and VerifyFile's.
- V10 (S29; beside R-B2). Nav copies are always writable by their owner: `tools::snapshot` makes an existing copy writable before it copies over it and makes each copy writable after, adding only the owner's write bit on Unix and clearing only the read-only attribute on Windows, so S29's second snapshot into a job folder (a restart in Detected, a Retry, a return) replaces them. RR1-11: `capture disc`'s bytes are unchanged, which is what R-B2 and design part 4.1's "unchanged output" protect, but its nav copies are no longer read-only; phase 0's scripts handle both. Why: `fs::copy` kept the disc's read-only attribute (`docs/phase0/environment.md`), so every later snapshot into the same folder failed with access denied, and the job failed Unknown from Detected on every Retry. Cost if wrong: none. Tests: `a_second_snapshot_into_the_same_folder_replaces_read_only_nav_copies`, `nav_copies_are_writable_when_the_disc_files_are_read_only` and `a_nav_copy_an_earlier_snapshot_left_read_only_is_replaced` (`crates/tools/src/snapshot.rs`), and end to end, with the steps' fixes of this round, `a_restart_in_detected_after_a_finished_recording_fingerprints_again`.
- V11 (design part 5.2; I2 amended). RR1-12: a step that panicked feeds `failure_event` of its main action: EjectFailed for an Eject, else `cancel_event`'s row when its state takes it, and ShareLost only for a CheckSpace whose state takes no failure row (Planned). A panicking CheckSpace in Decided therefore feeds DecisionFailed and reaches `Failed { Unknown, from: Decided }`, freeing the slot and KeepAwake, and its Retry waits for the slot and decides again; a panicking CheckSpace in Planned still waits in AwaitingShare, with the panic's text in its message. The same function, with no tag, is the failure row V1 feeds. Why: it fed ShareLost, which parked the job in `AwaitingShare { Decided }` holding the slot and KeepAwake, the harm I2 removes, and a Retry decided and panicked again. Cost if wrong: the recorded event of such a panic is DecisionFailed though Decide did not fail; job.log and the record's message name the real step. Tests: `a_failure_event_is_the_states_failure_row_and_share_lost_only_for_a_space_check_in_planned` (`ipc_server.rs`), `a_panicking_remux_space_check_fails_the_job_frees_the_slot_and_retry_resumes_it` (`engine_ipc.rs`) and the pin of the unchanged Planned path, `a_panicking_space_check_before_the_rip_waits_for_the_share_and_gives_the_token_back` (`owner/tests.rs`).
- V12 (spec 6.2 step 7, design parts 5.6 step 7 and 7.3; R22.3, R22.11). RR1-13: `core::record::TitleRecord` gains `verify_failures: u32` (`#[serde(default)]`), kept in state.json; VerifyFile's `second` is `verify_failures > 0` on the record handed to the step, which holds the earlier failure's posted edit (R22.3), and `verify_failed` posts one edit that adds 1, with the second failure's reasons and read errors. Only a mismatch or a wrong `.mkv` count is counted (V9). The format stays `lazymkv job v1`: Task 6's ruling lets an added key read, and no v1 file has shipped. It supersedes the PR 2 plan's "in this engine run" (Task 23 step 8); rebuilding the count from transitions.jsonl is rejected, because design part 7.3 forbids replaying the log. A Retry after a restart now behaves as one within a run: no fresh pair of rips. Why: the in-memory set was lost at a restart, so a title failing every rip was ripped three times. Cost if wrong: one key per title in state.json. Tests: `a_restart_after_a_first_verify_failure_rips_the_title_only_once_more` (`engine_restart.rs`, through `Harness::run_until_event`), the strengthened `a_first_verify_failure_moves_the_attempt_aside_and_rips_again` and `a_second_verify_failure_goes_on_and_records_verify_failed` (`engine_disc_phase.rs`), and `a_title_record_without_verify_failures_reads_as_zero` with the extended `a_job_record_round_trips_with_its_format_first` (`crates/core/src/record.rs`).
- V13 (design part 9.4, design part 7.4's "reported, never overwritten"; Task 21's ruling kept; S76's text reused). A `settings_set` while settings.json cannot be read (not JSON, an unknown or ill-typed key, not UTF-8, a byte-order mark, a read error other than NotFound) is refused with `settings_set refused: 1 problems` and the store's `settings.json` problem, and the file is left as it is: nothing is merged or written, the slot keeps its capacity and no disc becomes an arrival. `Store::settings_file` gives the file's settings, or that problem as an Err; `Store::settings` keeps Task 21's stand-in defaults byte for byte. An absent file still reads as the defaults, which a `settings_set` then writes. An arrival's refusal now names the settings.json problem before the unset `staging_root` of the stand-in defaults, as the snapshot does. Design part 9.4's refusal covers an unreadable current file; the design text stays as it is (RR1-15). Why: a partial payload such as `{"tuning_root": ...}` or `{"keep_tuning_evidence": false}`, merged into the stand-in defaults, validated clean and replaced the owner's whole file, and `lazymkvctl` exited 0. Cost if wrong: the owner fixes or deletes the file by hand before a `settings_set` works. Tests: `settings_set_while_settings_json_is_unreadable_is_refused_and_leaves_the_file` and `settings_set_with_no_settings_json_merges_into_the_defaults` (`engine_ipc.rs`), `settings_file_is_err_for_an_unreadable_file_and_ok_for_an_absent_one` (`store.rs`).
- V14 (spec 5, 6.1 step 2 and 11's Remember; phase 1's F10; R-B1 and P11 kept; design part 7.1's "for its plan section", design part 5.7 step 11.3's list and spec 6.4 step 11's list of the decision's inputs widened). RR1-14: (a) the decision's inputs include the track overrides of the remembered title matched to the decided title. `DecideInputs` gains `remembered`, filled by the new `replay_remembered`: `--remembered`'s file read with `option_text` and parsed as `Remembered`, as `plan_doc` reads it, and None without the flag or for a file that cannot be read or parsed, which `plan_doc` refuses, so the plan and the decision are skipped as before (S49's reasoning). `decision_section` passes the matched title's audio and subtitle overrides as `AudioFacts.remembered` and `SubtitleFacts.remembered`, which every caller left None. The engine's `engine_decide_inputs` fills the field with `replay_remembered` over the job's own remembered.json, the copy its plan applied, and never reads `Store::remembered` at Decide, whose file can change after Plan: decide.json therefore stays what `lazymkvctl decide --remembered <job>/remembered.json` prints (R-B1), and `read_back_inputs` inherits the field through `read_decide_inputs` (PF-27). The matched title's entries are re-keyed to the rip's title (`remembered_overrides`): they are applied over the scan title's streams, where Remember counts signature positions (core's `decision::remember`), and each stream so overridden then gets its signature over the narrowed title, because the eng selection drops flagged English audio by flags no signature holds, which moves positions; over `Selection::All`, the engine's, the re-keyed entries name the same streams. (b) The overrides apply per matched title, also when the plan refuses the remembered title roles on a partial match (`plan::assigned_roles`), as core's `matched_titles` and F10 apply them. A remembered Drop or Commentary drops the stream (`AudioDrop::Remembered`), a Keep fixes its order and default, subtitle keeps and drops apply with `decided_by` "remembered", and overrides covering every stream of a part set its confidence to 1.00. Without a remembered decision, or with empty lists, the output is byte for byte as before. The finding's subtitle example (two English streams held at 0.60) cannot happen in the engine: such a title fails at S32 before the decision, with or without the overrides, and remains possible only for dev's and ctl's `decide` over a recording. `decide -h` now says the flag serves the plan section and the decision's track overrides. The prerequisite landed with this round's steps fixes: a Plan whose data folder holds no remembered decision, or one it refuses, or whose own copy cannot be written (the final review's C7), removes a job copy an earlier Plan left, so Decide never applies overrides the plan did not use, unless that copy can be neither replaced nor removed, both errors then in job.log (`plan_removes_a_remembered_copy_the_data_folder_no_longer_holds` and `a_remembered_copy_that_cannot_be_written_removes_the_earlier_copy`, `engine_disc_phase.rs`). The spec and the design text stay as they are (RR1-15). Why: remembered audio and subtitle choices never reached the decision, so a remembered disc was filed with a stream the owner had dropped, or without one the owner had kept, at 0.95 rather than 1.00, and dev's replay made the same omission, so R-B1 could not see it. Cost if wrong: none for a disc without a remembered decision, whose output is unchanged. Tests: `decide_applies_a_remembered_decisions_track_overrides`, `a_remembered_override_follows_its_stream_under_the_eng_selection` (it fails without the re-keying) and `replay_remembered_is_none_without_the_flag_and_for_a_file_plan_refuses` (`crates/dev/src/cmd/decide.rs`); `a_remembered_decisions_track_overrides_reach_decide_json` (`engine_file_phase.rs`, with its R-B1 check) and `a_first_pass_decide_applies_the_jobs_remembered_copy_not_the_data_folders_file` (`engine_file_phase.rs`, the first-pass source with the data folder's file removed after Plan; the final review's C6); `a_decide_after_restart_applies_the_jobs_remembered_track_overrides` (`engine_restart.rs`, the read-back path with the data folder's file removed).
- V15 (design part 6.1, "the error says which"; R24.6 and RP-3's precedent; S81 and the final review's Minor 5 restored). CheckSpace's ShareLost carries `<root> is unreachable: <why>` (staging first) or `the free space of <root> could not be read: <error>` in the record's message, the waiting notification and job.log; the step writes the job.log line `check space: <sentence>` itself, the unset-root sentences included, since `with(..)` writes none. `rip_check` and `remux_check` now return `(Event, Option<String>)`, changing the signatures the PR 2 plan dictated. `space_freed` still feeds nothing for a condition not cleared (S81): ShareLost is never fed from a re-check, and the reducer rejects it in a waiting state. AwaitingShare's re-check of a Planned return now reaches the library root too, the roots its CheckSpace reads; every other disc-phase return, a rip, still reaches staging only (Minor 5). Why: an unreachable root or an unreadable free space was the only silent way into AwaitingShare, and a retry while the library was down returned the job to a CheckSpace that fed ShareLost again, in a loop. Cost if wrong: connector text reaches state.json and notifications (pull request 3 must keep secrets out of it; listed below). Tests: `an_unreachable_root_is_named_with_its_reason`, `the_remux_check_names_an_unreachable_library` and `a_free_space_error_is_named_with_its_root` (`space.rs`); the extended `a_share_recheck_reaches_the_roots_its_return_uses` (`selftest.rs`); the extended `an_unreachable_root_at_check_space_feeds_share_lost` and `an_unreachable_library_at_check_space_names_it_in_the_waiting_message` (`engine_disc_phase.rs`); `retry_with_the_library_still_unreachable_feeds_nothing` (`engine_ipc.rs`).

### Pull request 3: the planner's rulings (W1 to W28)

The planner's rulings of 2026-10-27, where the design decides or allows a builder's call. Owner decision 11 lists the
ones that change an earlier ruling or the design's letter.

- W1 (`--data`). The engine's `--data DIR` is optional, as design part 3.1 says, and on Windows its default is `%ProgramData%\lazymkv`, with the ProgramData folder read from the registry's `ProgramData` value, not from the environment, and a `ProgramData` variable that names another folder refused before the mutex (amended after the whole-branch review (F2), below); a missing value or words left over stay usage errors under `usage: lazymkv-engine [--data DIR]`, exit 2. Outside Windows the arguments are read the same way and the S28 sentence follows.
- W2 (exit codes). Every start refusal exits 1, after S28's precedent, a usage error exits 2 and a clean stop exits 0.
- W3 (start order). The engine reads its arguments, then checks its elevation, takes the mutex, protects the data folder, creates the first pipe instance and calls `start`, in that order (design parts 3.2, 3.3, 3.5); without `--data`, the default data folder's check comes between the elevation check and the mutex (amended after the whole-branch review (F2), below). The elevation check only reads the process token, so the mutex still comes before anything with an effect, and an engine that is not elevated says so rather than meeting the mutex's access error (amends design part 3.3's "before anything else, the engine takes the named mutex"; Owner decision 11: no answer recorded by 2026-10-29, so it stands until the owner overturns it).
- W4 (names). A mutex squatter's account is named on standard error only (design part 3.3); `engine.log` names no account, so a refused pipe client is logged by its process id and the verdict, and a data folder's foreign owner by its security identifier (SID).
- W5 (the mutex owner). The engine sets the mutex's owner `O:BA` itself (`MUTEX_SDDL`), so "owned by Administrators" does not depend on the token's default owner (design part 3.3). An owner that cannot be read is reported as a squatter named `an account lazymkv cannot read`.
- W6 (the data folder). The owner's account reads the data folder with `FR`, a folder the engine creates is owned by Administrators (`BA`), and the protected list is set at every start with `SetNamedSecurityInfoW` and inherited below (design part 3.5); its cost grows with the jobs tree, which phase 2's job count keeps small. Its cost line amended at review round 1 of pull request 3 (V3, R32), below.
- W7 (the Job Object runner). `JobProcessGuard` starts each child with `CREATE_SUSPENDED` (and `BELOW_NORMAL_PRIORITY_CLASS` when asked), assigns it to a Job Object of its own and resumes its one thread, so a kill ends the tree (design part 4.2), and T9-a to T9-e hold unchanged. On Windows the testkit's `TestGuard` wraps `native_guard()`, so every Windows whole fake job runs the Job Object runner, while the driver tests in `crates/fake-tools/tests/` keep `StdProcessGuard`. Amended at review round 1 of pull request 3 (R1, V9, V1), below.
- W8 (the drive watcher's window). `WinDrives` owns a thread with a hidden top-level window that registers for optical drive notifications, scans at every `WM_DEVICECHANGE` and every 2 s (`DRIVE_POLL_EVERY`), and feeds a queue the engine's drive thread drains through `next`; `new` scans once first, so `present()` is the state at start and `next()` never reports those discs again (L1a), while a disc whose volume does not answer yet arrives through `next()` with the usual settle and readiness wait (design part 5.4). A scan is `GetLogicalDrives`, `GetDriveTypeW == DRIVE_CDROM` and a successful `GetVolumeInformationW`, the drive id is the letter (`D:`), and no handle notification is registered, so nothing needs closing before an eject (amends design part 4.4's "the drive thread runs the window's message loop" in which thread owns the loop, and Ruling T3-16-a, made during execution, amends the scan sentence; Owner decision 11: no answer recorded by 2026-10-29, so it stands until the owner overturns it). Amended at review round 1 of pull request 3 (R6), below.
- W9 (KeepAwake). `WinKeepAwake` owns a thread that calls `SetThreadExecutionState` when the count leaves or reaches zero, so the state never depends on the caller's thread (design part 4.2). KeepAwake held through the waits entered after a file-phase slot grant is confirmed by spec 4.3's "while any job is active".
- W10 (the pipe server). The server hands a connection out only after creating the next instance, so the name is never unheld, and after the client's token check; `accept` returns `BrokenPipe` only after `close`, a per-client error has another kind and never ends the serve thread, and the writer's `flush` is a no-op, so a silent client never blocks a push (design parts 3.3, 3.4; K12). `command_output` goes only to the client that sent the `command`, because every `lazymkvctl` run uses `id` 1, and the relay waits for room in that client's queue through `Clients::send_waiting` instead of dropping it (amends design part 9.2's broadcast for `command_output` and S52 for `command_output` only, and PF3-S10-12-2 and PF3-S10-12-3, made during execution, amend its accept errors; Owner decision 11: no answer recorded by 2026-10-29, so it stands until the owner overturns it).
