- Besides the eighteen, the draft pull request: yes: push `phase-2-windows-pieces` and open pull request 3 as a draft at Task T3-2's last step, then push each later task's commit to it, 2026-10-28 (answered at the plan's approval, before Task T3-1)

### Pull request 3: rulings made during execution

The controller of the plan's execution made these while the tasks ran, the ones named PF3 on a pre-flight check of the
plan (2026-10-28). That check found 52 findings, one of them blocking; its rulings listed here change an earlier ruling
(W8, W10, S75, V3, RR1-2) or what the plan's tasks build or ask (PF3-S15-17-1, PF3-S10-12-3, PF3-S13-14-5), and the
others settled the plan's wording only. The ones named F1 and F2 followed the review of the whole branch (2026-10-29)
and change W18, W1 and W3. Each line says what was decided, why and the cost if wrong.

**All tasks**
- The plan ran in the repository's main checkout, on its branch, with no separate worktree, because `scripts/box.sh` mirrors the checkout's top folder to `C:\lazymkv\src`, as for pull request 2. Cost if wrong: none, unless the checkout's branch is switched mid-run.

**Task T3-1**
- PF3-S15-17-1 (21 features; amends the plan's Tech Stack and Task T3-1). `Win32_Graphics_Gdi` is the 21st feature of `windows-sys`, after `Win32_Foundation`. Why: `RegisterClassW` and `WNDCLASSW`, which Task T3-16's window needs, exist only with it, so Task T3-16 would not compile with the plan's 20; a feature adds no package, so the lock delta and Owner decision 1 stand. Cost if wrong: one line of `crates/platform/Cargo.toml` if the feature turns out unneeded.
- The crates.io read of T3-1-a ran while the pre-flight check ran, and the fetch only after the owner's yes and the check's verdict. Cost if wrong: none; the read was scratch.

**Task T3-3**
- A test that prints the owner's SID on a failed assertion does not breach the plan's constraint, which names a MakeMKV key, an account name, a drive serial or a credential. Why: a SID is not an account name, and the output stays on the mini PC or the runner. Cost if wrong: none found.

**Task T3-4**
- T3-4-c (long paths; amends Task T3-4's `wide_path`, which gave no long-path prefix). `wide_path` mirrors std's long-path handling: a path longer than 248 units that does not start with `\\?\` or `\??\` is made absolute with `GetFullPathNameW` and given the `\\?\` prefix (`\\?\UNC\` for `\\server\share`, and `\\?\` in place of `\\.\`), and one test moves a file whose path exceeds 260 characters. Why: pull request 2's filing went through std and reached long library paths, and design part 6.5 swaps the call, not its reach. Cost if wrong: a long title or a deep library root fails to file on Windows. Amended at review round 1 of pull request 3 (R10, R49), below.
- PF3-S4-6-2 (amends RR1-2's cost line, V1). With T3-4-a's tries inside each of the store's five, a state.json another process holds for good costs the owner thread up to about 0.85 s, not 0.4 s; the behaviour is kept. Cost if wrong: a stale cost line.

**Tasks T3-10 and T3-11** (the accept contract)
- PF3-S10-12-2 (amends W10's "any other OS error keeps its kind"). An operating-system error of kind `BrokenPipe`, `PermissionDenied` or `ConnectionAborted` becomes `Other`, its text kept, so only the client check returns `PermissionDenied` (a refused client) or `ConnectionAborted` (a client gone before its check), and the trait's doc says so. Why: an access-denied accept would otherwise be logged as a refused client and tried again at once, with no `ACCEPT_PAUSE`, flooding `engine.log`. Cost if wrong: a change local to the pipe server's error mapping and one test.
- PF3-S10-12-3 (amends Task T3-10's accept contract). `accept` returns `BrokenPipe` once `close` ran, or once no client can ever connect again, as a `MemoryIpc` whose connectors are all dropped does (T8-e), and the trait's doc says both. Why: `lazymkvctl`'s scripted-peer tests rely on the second. Cost if wrong: none found.

**Task T3-13**
- Owner decision 8's persistence question, non-blocking (PF3-S13-14-5), was asked with Task T3-25's prerequisites before Task T3-24 started, not before Task T3-13's commit was pushed. Why: a question blocks the controller, and an overturn changes one constant that no test asserts. Cost if wrong: one constant and one push.

**Task T3-16**
- T3-16-a (amends W8's scan sentence; PF3-S15-17-2). A scan whose `GetVolumeInformationW` fails with os error 5 or 32 keeps that drive's last answer, so a volume lock, the engine's own eject's or another program's, is never reported as a removal (design part 5.6 step 8). On the mini PC a volume lock held for 6 s gave no event. Cost if wrong: a reviewer finding inside that task's own files; nothing downstream builds on it.
- The window class is registered with the window's creation, and unregistered at each watcher thread's end, under one process-wide lock (`CLASS_LOCK`), kept although the review called it sound but unneeded. Why: the tests run several watchers in one process, which would otherwise flake between `RegisterClassW` and `CreateWindowExW`, and it has no deadlock path. Cost if wrong: one process-wide static, and a rule later editors of the window code keep.

**Task T3-17**
- T3-17-a (amends S75 and V3 (c); PF3-S15-17-5). A Remuxing job's rebuilt Remux promise is `planned_remux_promise(plan)`, reading no staged file, and the Remux step replaces it with the promise of the staged sizes (`refine_remux_promise`); in V3's test list `an_unreadable_staging_folder_rebuilds_the_remux_promise_from_the_plan` gives way to `a_rebuilt_remux_promise_is_the_plans_estimate_and_reads_no_staged_file` and `the_remux_step_replaces_the_estimate_with_the_staged_sizes`, and review round 1 of pull request 3 (R21) adds the whole-engine `a_resumed_remux_step_refines_the_rebuilt_promise_before_another_jobs_rip_check`. Why: the rebuild runs on the owner thread, which reads no share in pull request 3. Cost if wrong: a reviewer finding inside that task's own files; nothing downstream builds on it.
- `Owner::delete_partials` stays as it is: at a forced shutdown it still lists and deletes the library's `.lazymkv-partial` files on the owner thread, with no `reach` first, and it is left for later (below). Why: it runs only after every step has ended, and no task of the plan covers it. Cost if wrong: a forced shutdown waits on a stalled share.

**Task T3-18**
- T3-18-a (L7's test helper; amends Task T3-18's Windows test helper, an `icacls` deny on the folder). On Windows the test helper `Closed` holds the title's folder open with share mode 0 and `FILE_FLAG_BACKUP_SEMANTICS`, so `read_dir` and the rename fail with os error 32 until it closes; L7's rule already counts any `read_dir` error, so the test exercises the real branch. Why: an access-list deny cannot hold against the backup privileges of the elevated SSH session, and skipping L7 on both Windows runners would leave it unproven there. Cost if wrong: the test stands in for an inaccessible folder with a sharing violation rather than an access denial; both reach the same branch.
- Task T3-18's `snapshot_aside` contract (the swap of the fresh snapshot into the job folder) is amended at review round 1 of pull request 3 (V12), below.

**Task T3-20**
- T3-20-a (amends the plan's design for Task T3-20). The refeed timer sends its own message, `Msg::Refeed { job }`, and the owner keeps a per-job armed mark apart from the kept event: a timer starts only when the mark is set, and the Refeed handler clears it and feeds the kept event if one is left. Why: the plan's "the two kinds of timer never meet in one job" failed across a state change inside the minute, so a stale timer could start a lookup round. Cost if wrong: one more message variant.
- T3-20-b (widens W23's kept events). A refused `LookupPending` for an `Ejected { after_copy: false }` job is kept and fed again too. Why: the job leaves the slot queue before that feed, so a refused persist stranded it in Ejected with no timer until a restart, the stranding Task T3-20 closes. Cost if wrong: an extra refeed branch that fires only under a persist fault.

**Task T3-22**
- Task T3-10's review found that `shut_down` dropped the `engine.log` lines arriving while it waited for stale workers; Task T3-22 writes them, since it edits that function. Cost if wrong: one extra match arm.
- The relay forwards the client's words to the child `lazymkvctl` after the built ones, so a client may pass any of its flags, `--settings`, `--evidence` or a path among them; an automated security review's high finding on this is acknowledged and not fixed. Why: design part 9.5 and W24 forward the words on purpose, and the pipe admits only elevated processes of the owner's own account (P6), so the client already holds every right the child has and no privilege boundary is crossed; W24's refusals cover malformed words. Cost if wrong: a later widening of the pipe's access list needs an allowlist here.

**Task T3-24**
- T3-24-c (the run folder's parent and links; widens T3-24-a's checks and Owner decision 13, hence its addendum). `engine-task.ps1` checks the run folder's parent `C:\lazymkv` with the two build folders, so `-AcceptWritableBuild` names it too and the refusal says the run folder itself could be replaced; it refuses a run folder that is a link before any change, and removes each program before copying it, so each copy is a new file that inherits the protected list. Why: design part 3.1 fixes the run folder's place but not its parent's list, and the data folder already guards the same two routes (T3-3-b, T3-3-c). Cost if wrong: one more refusal text and a few script lines; a protected parent waits for a later pull request (Owner decision 13's addendum).

**Task T3-25**
- The first desktop drive job, which timed out waiting for the insertion, was run again rather than recorded as found, with its cues read anywhere in a line. Why: `testutil::cue` writes `CUE: <text>` with no leading newline, so libtest's progress text shares its line and the plan's `findstr /b CUE:` never matched, which delayed the relayed cue past the test's wait. Cost if wrong: none; the second run passed.
- The three discs that planned whole-disc copy mode were recorded as found (P1), and the owner offered a fourth disc, with one main feature, for the rip. Cost if wrong: none; design part 5.5 fails such a plan with that message.

**The whole-branch review**
- F1 (the default install folders; amends W18 and the briefs of Tasks T3-7 and T3-8). `WinHost::default_install_path` reads the Program Files folder from the value `ProgramFilesDir (x86)` (MakeMKV) or `ProgramFilesDir` (MKVToolNix) under `HKEY_LOCAL_MACHINE\SOFTWARE\Microsoft\Windows\CurrentVersion` with `RegGetValueW`, as `REG_SZ` with `RRF_NOEXPAND`, and no longer from the `ProgramFiles(x86)` or `ProgramFiles` variable; a value that cannot be read, still holds a `%` or is not absolute gives no install path. `find_tool`'s step 3 stays unjudged; `makemkv_debug_log` read `USERPROFILE` until review round 1 of pull request 3 (V14), below, and now reads the registry's profile folder. Tests: `default_install_paths_are_the_registrys_program_files_folders_and_none_for_ffprobe` compares the paths with the values `reg query` prints, and `the_program_files_variables_do_not_move_the_install_paths` starts the test binary as a child whose two variables name a temporary folder and finds that its paths have not moved. Why: Windows builds the elevated engine's environment with the user's own variables (`HKEY_CURRENT_USER\Environment`) over the system's, and a non-elevated process of the user can write those, so a planted `ProgramFiles(x86)` made the engine run a program that process chose, elevated, at its next start (P6, design part 3.6). Judging step 3 with W17's writability test instead would be false comfort, since a process that chooses the whole path can lock its own folder so that the test passes. Cost if wrong: a machine whose registry and environment disagree on the Program Files folders looks for MakeMKV and MKVToolNix where the registry says.
- F2 (the default data folder; amends W1 and W3). With no `--data`, the data folder is the value `ProgramData` under `HKEY_LOCAL_MACHINE\SOFTWARE\Microsoft\Windows NT\CurrentVersion\ProfileList` (`%SystemDrive%\ProgramData`), read with `RRF_NOEXPAND`, its `%SystemDrive%` replaced by the drive of `GetSystemWindowsDirectoryW`, joined with `lazymkv` (`win::program_data`); no environment variable is read. When the process's `ProgramData` variable is set and names another folder, compared part by part and case-insensitively, the engine prints `lazymkv-engine: the ProgramData variable (<variable>) differs from the registry's ProgramData (<registry>); give --data DIR (design part 3.5)` and exits 1, after the elevation check and before the mutex, since the check only reads the process's own inputs; a registry that gives no folder prints `lazymkv-engine: the registry's ProgramData could not be read; give --data DIR`, which replaces `%ProgramData% is not set`. An explicit `--data` is untouched. `NativeOptions` gains one field, `program_data_var`, through which `a_native_engine_refuses_a_program_data_variable_that_differs_from_the_registry` gives the variable, and `program_data_is_derived_from_the_registry` replaces `program_data_is_read_from_the_environment`. Why: a user variable `ProgramData` moved the elevated engine's data folder below a parent the non-elevated process owns, where it could rename the protected folder away, swap a junction, or plant a `settings.json` naming any program as a tool, which the running engine reads at every arrival and self-test (design part 3.5, MISS-ARCH-18; P6); T3-24-c closed the same class for the run folder. Cost if wrong: a machine with a relocated ProgramData folder refuses to start until `--data` is given, and says why in one sentence.

The owner may overturn any of them.

### Review round 1 (the review of pull request #14)

The seventy findings of the review of pull request #14 (V1 to V15 inline, G1 from code scanning, R1 to R54 in the
roll-up), each verified against the code one by one; 63 are fixed, in 59 signed commits (`8fe4be0..73e412b`), six are
declined and one is deferred. The rulings below are the controller's, of 2026-10-29 (verification and plan) and
2026-11-04 (implementation complete), each labelled by the finding it decides; a pair fixed together shares one entry.
Every amendment is recorded in this file only; the spec and the design text stay untouched, as RR1-15 left them. The
round's final review, over the whole branch (2026-11-04; seven lenses, each finding checked by an adversarial verifier),
found no high finding; its fifteen confirmed items (W1 to W16, one refuted) are fixed in 18 more commits
(`73e412b..9f84b71`, the last one the CI fix under G1) and folded into the entries they touch, each marked "the final
review". Two sets of commits were rewritten before anything was pushed, each by an autosquash rebase that kept the
commits' author and committer dates and signed each rewritten commit again: the R48 and V14 commits, to fix their
labels, and the R26, R36 and R31 commits, to fold in three fixups that wrap lines over 120 columns.

- V1 (design part 3.5 and MISS-ARCH-18 applied to the children's environment; W7 amended; T9-e and S43 unchanged; reverses the whole-branch review's refutation of platform-win-a-3). `JobProcessGuard` starts every child with an empty environment, then the inherited variables whose names `security::CHILD_ENV_NAMES` lists (the 31 Windows defines for a logon session, matched ignoring ASCII case, values inherited), then `Spawn::env`. Building `PATH` and the other kept values from `HKEY_LOCAL_MACHINE` and the token is not done now; it joins the deferred `PATH` item (After pull request 3's whole-branch review, below), since the system `Path` itself holds `%SystemRoot%`. Why: Windows builds the elevated engine's environment over `HKCU\Environment`, which a non-elevated process of the owner can write (F1's premise). `JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS` (`-agentpath`) therefore chose what an elevated `java` loaded, and `FFREPORT` or `MKVTOOLNIX_OPTIONS` (`--redirect-output`) where an elevated `ffprobe`, `ffmpeg` or `mkvmerge` wrote. `makemkvcon` passes its environment to the Java it starts for BD-J. The refutation's premise that no `java` runs was false: `selftest.rs` runs `java -version`. An allowlist, because `makemkvcon` is closed source and the JVM, Qt and .NET read vendor variables nobody lists. Cost if wrong: a tool that needs a variable outside the list misbehaves where the self-test or the next job shows it; the name is added, from a trusted source if the user can write it. A variable the owner set to steer a tool no longer reaches it (ENG-19 fixes flags per job), nor do the engine's `LAZYMKV_EVIDENCE` and `LAZYMKV_OCR_TEXT` reach the relay's `lazymkvctl`, whose paths come from its flags (V10). Tests: `the_child_environment_keeps_windows_own_variables_whatever_their_case`, `the_child_environment_drops_every_variable_it_does_not_name` and `a_spawns_own_variables_come_after_the_kept_ones` (`security.rs`), and, Windows only, `option_variables_in_the_engines_environment_do_not_reach_a_guarded_child` (`win::job`, whose test child prints variable names only, never values). The final review (W1): the kept names' values still come from the engine's environment, which `HKCU\Environment` and Volatile Environment shape (`USERPROFILE`, `APPDATA`, `LOCALAPPDATA`, `TEMP`, `TMP` and `PATH`'s user part), so a non-elevated process of the owner can still choose where an elevated child that follows one of them reads or writes, the write side of V14's debug log among them; the `CHILD_ENV_NAMES` doc says so, and the fix stays with the deferred item (below), since an override from the registry's ProfileList would hard-code folders Windows lets a user relocate.
- V2 (W18 amended; design part 3.6's resources sentence extended in this file only). `find_tool` skips a `resources` program file that `host.user_writable` calls writable, as it skips a `PATH` one; a writable `resources` folder is still skipped too. Why: a protected resources folder could hold a program a non-elevated process can rewrite (a user-owned file an administrator moved in keeps its owner's `WRITE_DAC`), which the engine then ran elevated. Cost if wrong: a resources tool wrongly called writable is skipped for the install folder or `PATH`, or reported missing; visible and recoverable. The deferred owner-based predicate (below) now names a `resources` folder and its program file. Test: `a_writable_program_file_in_a_protected_resources_folder_is_skipped` (`crates/tools/src/lookup.rs`), which removes its folder at the end, as the crate's tests do (the final review, W13; the crate's move to `Scratch` is noted, not scheduled, below).
- V3 (design part 3.5's second bullet and Rulings T3-3-b and T3-3-c widened from the folder to every entry below; W6's cost line amended; Owner decision 11's class, since it widens the design's letter). `protect_data_folder` refuses a data folder holding, at any depth and without following links, a link or junction or an entry owned by neither Administrators nor SYSTEM, naming the entry; every refusal comes before any change. Not taken: `TreeResetNamedSecurityInfoW`, which would silently adopt the planted content design part 3.5 refuses, and needs `WRITE_OWNER` on entries whose list may exclude Administrators. The finding's reach is corrected: a data folder the engine did not create itself. W6's cost line, with R32's decline folded in, now reads: each start walks every entry below the data folder and propagates the protected list again; job folders are never deleted (design part 5.9), so the cost grows by a few entries per job kept: milliseconds in phase 2, possibly seconds after years, once per start, before the pipe is bound; skipping the set when the root already holds `folder_sddl` was considered and not taken, since the root's list does not show stale inherited entries below (R32). T3-25-f gains: the console run's `settings.json`, like the folder, must be owned by Administrators before the first start. Why: `SetNamedSecurityInfoW`'s propagation replaces only inherited entries, so a user-owned `settings.json` kept its owner's implicit `WRITE_DAC`, and a non-elevated process could grant itself write and name the program the elevated engine runs. It happens on a hand-made `%ProgramData%\lazymkv` or a `--data` folder made under `C:\` (T3-25-f). What the walk does not see is left for later (After pull request 3's whole-branch review, below). Cost if wrong: a start refused, naming the entry, until an administrator removes it or gives it to Administrators. Tests (Windows only, `win::folder`): `a_file_below_owned_by_another_account_is_refused_and_the_folder_keeps_its_list`, `an_entry_owned_by_another_account_deeper_down_is_refused`, `a_junction_below_the_folder_is_refused_and_its_target_keeps_its_list`, and the canary `a_restart_accepts_the_entries_its_own_token_wrote_below`, which passes because the elevated token's default owner is Administrators; `an_existing_folder_owned_by_administrators_gets_the_list_again` now gives its `settings.json` to Administrators first.
- V4 with V8 (design part 3.8 cited, unchanged; W19 amended; S56 and S83 cited, unchanged). The stop events as they reach the engine: Ctrl+C and Ctrl+Break have no limit, and the forced shutdown runs to its end; the console window's close is cut off by Windows 5 s after it, so a cancelled tool step (S56's 10 s pause) is resumed at the next start (Ripping empties its title folder and rips again; Remuxing deletes its partial file and remuxes again); a logoff, a restart or a shutdown gets Windows' default on the drive watcher's window, and the engine ends without the request. The console routine now declines `CTRL_LOGOFF_EVENT` and `CTRL_SHUTDOWN_EVENT`, which never reach it, and this file's description of the binary says so (Pull request 3: the Windows pieces, above). `WM_QUERYENDSESSION` and `WM_ENDSESSION` are not handled with an orderly stop: Windows gives about 5 s, less than S56's 10 s pause; a finished forced shutdown would leave jobs Failed where an abrupt end resumes them by itself; and a wait on the watcher's thread deadlocks with the stop's join of that thread. The close's forced shutdown is not made to finish (that would need S56's pause skipped across crates), and a plain `shutdown` on a close was considered and not taken (the controller's call). Why: the docs, W19 and the routine described events that never arrive and a close that never finishes. Cost if wrong: none; behaviour unchanged. Tests: `ctrl_c_and_ctrl_break_return_at_once_close_waits_and_logoff_and_shutdown_are_declined` (`win::console`, in place of `ctrl_c_and_ctrl_break_return_at_once_and_close_logoff_and_shutdown_wait`) and `the_window_lets_a_logoff_or_a_restart_go_on` (`win::drives`), a pin that passed before the change too, since the behaviour is unchanged. A logoff that waits for a step is noted, not scheduled (below).
- V5 (design part 3.5's "In a console run the same lines also go to standard error" and part 4.4's list of threads, amended in this file only, per RR1-15; W26 kept; T3-10's `EngineConfig.console`; Owner decision 4 unchanged; Owner decision 11's class). engine.log's console copy is written by a console mirror thread from a bounded queue of `CONSOLE_QUEUE` (1024) lines; `Store::log` only queues it, so no engine thread waits on the console, and a conhost selection, mark, scroll-thumb hold or Pause blocks only the mirror. A full queue drops the console copy, never engine.log's line, and the mirror then says how many lines it did not show (`lazymkv-engine: <n> engine.log lines were not shown here because the console took no output; engine.log holds them`). `EngineHandle::stop` waits at most `CONSOLE_DRAIN` (1 s) for the queue to empty. Design part 3.5's sentence gains "unless the console takes no output, when the console misses lines engine.log keeps", and part 4.4's list gains the console mirror thread; it follows part 4.4's own rule for a client's writer. Turning QuickEdit off was not taken: Mark, holding the scroll thumb and Pause block conhost's writes too. Why: one click in the shown task window stalled every engine.log writer, and `lazymkvctl stop` replied ok while the process kept the engine mutex, so a new start reported `ENGINE_RUNNING` and a redeploy refused until a key was pressed. Residual risks: the two exit-path writes (`STOPPED_STARTING` and `run_with`'s final error text) still write standard error directly, and a panic's message can still wait on a blocked console. Cost if wrong: the console misses lines engine.log has. Tests: `a_console_that_takes_no_output_never_holds_up_engine_log`, `a_full_console_queue_drops_only_the_console_copy_and_says_so` and `a_console_that_is_gone_drains_at_once` (`store.rs`), and `a_held_console_stalls_neither_the_engine_nor_its_stop` (`engine_ipc.rs`, through the testkit's `Harness::hold_console`), which failed after 10 s before the change rather than hanging. The final review (W3, W4, W5): that 10 s bound and `store.rs`'s 2 s bound scale by `ci_factor` (R29.c5), the 500 ms assertion of `a_console_that_is_gone_drains_at_once` is dropped, since `drain` returning true already pins it, and the Store docs say the console is the mirror, never standard error held under the lock.
- V6 (extends Task T3-16 step 1's watcher-thread `SetThreadErrorMode` to the process and its children; W3 unchanged). After every start-up refusal, the engine sets `SEM_FAILCRITICALERRORS` for the whole process (`win::fail_critical_errors`, which keeps the mode's other flags), kept by every thread and inherited by every child, so a disc removed or swapped mid-read gives an error, never Windows' "No Disk" box blocking the arrival, recording, Fingerprint or eject thread (and the disc-phase token) until answered. One process-wide call rather than per-thread calls, because the engine crate has no `windows-sys` and a future thread could forget the call; the Job Object runner still never passes `CREATE_DEFAULT_ERROR_MODE`. Cost if wrong: a tool that would have waited on the box for a disc put back fails its read instead. Test: `fail_critical_errors_adds_only_its_flag` (`win::tests`), which first clears the flag, because the SSH session's inherited mode (0x8001) already holds it and the test as planned passed against an empty stub.
- V7 (amends the kept-event mechanics of review round 1 of pull request 2 (V1) as W23 and T3-20-a implement them). A transition that leaves the state as it was and runs only `ArmRipAgain` or a `RecordNote` leaves a kept refeed entry; every other persisted transition takes it out. Why: a `rip_again` on an Ejected job with a kept `LookupPending` or `FilePhaseSlot` removed the entry, and the job sat outside the slot queue with no timer until a restart. Cost if wrong: a kept event survives a transition that changed nothing, and `feed_again` checks it again; the worst is a `rejected <event> in <state>` line. `rip_again`'s reply is unchanged (noted, not scheduled, below). Tests: `a_rip_again_beside_a_kept_lookup_pending_leaves_it_to_its_refeed_timer` and `a_rip_again_beside_a_kept_slot_grant_leaves_it_to_its_refeed_timer` (`engine_ipc.rs`).
- V9 (W7 and Task T3-5's flags amended; W19 and design part 3.8 unchanged in text). Every guarded child is created with `CREATE_NO_WINDOW`, so Ctrl+C, Ctrl+Break and the close of the engine's console reach the engine alone, whose handler cancels the steps through the Job Objects; the hidden console is a process of its own, so T3-5-b's one thread at resume holds. Not `DETACHED_PROCESS` (a console grandchild would show a window); not `CREATE_NEW_PROCESS_GROUP` (Ctrl+Break still reaches the child). Why: the tools died by their own default handler and raced the cancel, recording the tool's exit instead of "cancelled by the owner" and skipping S83's partial-file deletion (the whole-branch review's platform-win-a-8 with engine-owner-5). The finding's consequences were overstated: a cancel also ends in Failed with a notification, and the race changes the message and S83's deletion. Cost if wrong: one hidden conhost per child, a few MB and tens of milliseconds per start. Test: `a_ctrl_break_on_the_engines_console_does_not_reach_a_guarded_child` (`win::job`), whose probe child reported `probe ended -1073741510` (`STATUS_CONTROL_C_EXIT`) before the flag and `probe alive` after.
- V10 (extends K7; clarifies design part 9.5's "Paths in other flags ... are read as the desktop session sees them" for relative paths, in this file only; W24 unchanged; Owner decision 11's class). `lazymkvctl` sends a relayed plan's path flags (`--evidence`, `--settings`, `--tmdb-search`, `--tmdb-record`, `--remembered`) and decide's (those plus `--rec` and `--text`) whose value is relative again, made absolute with `Ctx::abs` before any `--`; the engine still passes every word unchanged. Sending the client's working folder to the engine was rejected: it lets a client choose an elevated child's folder. Why: the child runs in the engine's working folder, so a relative path read another folder's file or none, with a message naming only the relative text. Residual: a drive-relative value (`D:alt.json`) stays drive-relative; retune's path flags wait for pull request 5 (below). Tests: `a_relayed_plan_sends_its_relative_path_flags_again_made_absolute`, `a_relayed_decide_makes_rec_and_text_absolute_and_leaves_selection_alone`, `the_absolute_flags_go_before_a_double_dash` and `relayed_words_with_no_relative_path_or_a_flag_missing_its_value_go_unchanged` (`crates/ctl/src/engine_cmds.rs`), `the_relay_reads_plans_and_decides_own_flag_lists` (`crates/ctl/src/tests.rs`, over dev's `plan::FLAGS` and `decide::FLAGS`), and `ctl_plan_job_sends_a_relative_settings_path_made_absolute_against_its_own_folder` (`engine_command.rs`).
- V11 (no ruling amended; it makes W21's "a staging root that is not a folder ... keeps the job waiting" true). `make_root` and `root_ready` refuse a root that exists and is not a folder, so CheckSpace feeds ShareLost with `<root> is not a folder` and the job waits in `AwaitingShare { Planned }`. Before, on Windows, such a root passed CheckSpace and the job reached AwaitingShare only from the title folder's write error (os error 183), under the title folder's name. Cost if wrong: a root whose metadata reads as a non-folder waits naming itself instead of failing later in RipTitle under a misleading name. Tests: `a_root_that_is_a_file_is_not_a_folder_and_is_left_alone` (`space.rs`), `a_share_recheck_waits_while_the_staging_root_is_a_file` (`selftest.rs`) and `a_staging_root_that_is_a_file_waits_and_a_retry_feeds_nothing` (`engine_ipc.rs`).
- V12 (Task T3-18's `snapshot_aside` contract amended; closes the final review's engine-steps-3 by its "rename aside" option; S29, review round 1 of pull request 2 (V10, RR1-11) and PF3-S18-20-3 kept). The swap moves the job's entries aside (into `OLD_SNAPSHOT`, `fingerprint.lazymkv-old`), then the fresh ones in, and on any failure puts every moved entry back, so the job's recording is never short of an entry; a leftover from a crash is put back at the next run. The suggested "rename replaces the file" was not taken: it cannot replace `nav`, a folder that is never empty. Why: a failure part way left files.json, nav and meta.json part new, part old or missing, with no rollback. Cost if wrong: the recording short of an entry, today's state, which the next successful Fingerprint rewrites. Tests: `a_swap_that_fails_at_any_move_leaves_the_jobs_own_snapshot_whole` and `an_entry_left_aside_by_a_failed_rollback_or_a_crash_is_put_back_by_the_next_run` (`steps.rs`, every system), which pin the rollback; `a_matching_disc_swaps_in_the_new_snapshot` now also finds no `OLD_SNAPSHOT`; and, Windows only, `a_held_job_file_stops_the_swap_and_leaves_the_snapshot_whole`, which passed on the old code too, since for the same disc the replaced files.json and nav are byte-identical. The final review (W2): a crash between two moves, or a rollback whose own move back fails, can leave the folder mixed between two snapshots of one fingerprint (the fingerprint compared equal before the swap started), and the next successful Fingerprint rewrites it; the docs say so rather than "whole", and "put back by the next run" holds for an entry left aside whose name the job folder lacks.
- V13 (Owner decision 5 kept; `-Data`'s form constrained). `engine-task.ps1` refuses, before it reads or changes anything, a `-Data` that is not a full local folder path free of space, quote and percent sign, a drive's root, one inside `C:\lazymkv\run`, or one whose parent does not exist. Quoting the argument was rejected: a trailing backslash escapes the closing quote. Why: the task's argument line is unquoted, so a space split it and the engine exited 2 with no log, and a relative folder was created and protected inside the run folder, after which every deploy refused. None of the script entries needs the owner's yes, and the registered task's action is unchanged (the current deploy passes no `-Data`). Test: `the_task_scripts_data_check_refuses_what_the_task_line_would_split_or_misplace` (`scripts.rs`, Windows only), which calls `Get-DataProblem` alone from the script's syntax tree, the script itself never running (Owner decision 3). The final review (W12): the check also refuses `C:\lazymkv`, a folder inside `C:\lazymkv\src` or `C:\lazymkv\target`, and any folder that holds the run folder or a build tree, since the build or the deploy recreates them and the engine's protected list would reach them, and its first pattern matches to the string's end, so a value ending in a newline is refused by name; a folder under the run folder's parent but outside a build tree is refused as one a non-elevated process may write (Owner decision 13, Ruling T3-24-c), not as one the build recreates; the test holds those cases.
- V14 (amends F1's sentence on `makemkv_debug_log`; the place design part 7.2 reads the log from; the Task T3-7 brief; S61). `WinHost::makemkv_debug_log` is `<ProfileImagePath>\MakeMKV_log.txt`, the value under `HKEY_LOCAL_MACHINE`'s ProfileList for the process's own SID, read with `RRF_NOEXPAND` as F2 reads ProgramData (`win::folder::registry_folder`), never `%USERPROFILE%`; `save_debug_log` opens the log once, refuses a link, and takes the mtime and the bytes from that one handle (S61 gains "a log that is a link is not saved; the check and the read use one open"). The registry's ProfileList rather than `GetUserProfileDirectoryW`: it reuses F2's reader and needs no new `windows-sys` feature. Why: a non-elevated process of the owner can set `USERPROFILE` (`HKCU\Environment` or Volatile Environment), and the elevated engine copied whatever file it named into the data folder, which the owner's token can read. The two opens let a link swap defeat the mtime check. Cost if wrong: a machine whose MakeMKV follows a `USERPROFILE` other than the registry's keeps no debug log, and job.log says why; jobs never fail on it (design part 7.2). The `app_Java` question stays open (below). Tests: `the_debug_log_is_makemkv_log_txt_in_the_registrys_profile` (in place of `the_debug_log_is_makemkv_log_txt_in_the_profile`) and `the_profile_variable_does_not_move_the_debug_log` (`win::host`, on F1's child pattern), and, Windows only, `a_debug_log_that_is_a_link_is_not_saved` (`crates/tools/src/makemkv.rs`). The final review (W7): the link refusal holds on Windows; off Windows the open follows a link, and the docs say so.
- V15 (F2 applied to the script's text). When `lazymkvctl status` does not answer, `engine-task.ps1`'s text follows the task's state and last result: a running engine (read engine.log), a start-up refusal (it reaches only the task's console window, so rerun the printed command in the elevated console), or a task that did not run (the owner must be logged on at the monitor); the data folder comes from the registry, as the engine reads it, and no `$env:ProgramData` read remains. Logging start-up refusals to engine.log from the engine was rejected: the refusals before `protect_data_folder` would be elevated writes into a folder not yet protected. Tests: `the_task_scripts_no_answer_text_follows_the_tasks_state_and_result` and `the_task_scripts_data_folder_is_the_engines` (`scripts.rs`, Windows only), the second comparing `Get-DataFolder` with `win::program_data`. The final review (W11): a last result that is Task Scheduler's own (its high word 0x0004, 0x8004 or 0x8007) keeps the did-not-run text; any other result is the engine's exit code, since a 0, a 101 or an NTSTATUS crash code means the engine ran, and the text says so and names engine.log; the test holds those rows.
- G1 (widens T3-24-c's checked paths inside the trees Owner decision 13 and its addendum accept; no new risk accepted). `engine-task.ps1`'s writers check meets the release folder and each program copied from it as well as the trees, after the required-program check; `-AcceptWritableBuild` and the write mask are unchanged. Recursing over the trees is declined as false comfort (F1's argument), since cargo, rustc and the crate registry live in the user's profile. Severity corrected: the gain is the owner's elevated token, not SYSTEM, and on the mini PC today the trees are already accepted as writable (Owner decision 13). Tests: the every-system needle of the new loop in `the_task_script_names_the_task_the_run_folder_and_no_trigger`, and, Windows only, `the_writers_check_sees_an_entry_a_program_file_alone_carries` (`scripts.rs`), which passed before the change too, since `Get-Writers` already read a file's own list; the needle is the one that failed. Residual: cargo and rustc run from the user-writable `%USERPROFILE%\.cargo\bin`, so a clean writers check does not vouch for the build itself, the state Owner decision 13 accepts. The CI run of the round's head failed this test on `windows-latest` alone: the runner sets `PSModulePath` for PowerShell 7, whose edition of `Microsoft.PowerShell.Security` Windows PowerShell 5.1 then finds first and cannot load, so `Get-Acl` fails with `CouldNotAutoloadMatchingModule`; the test harness and the script now put `$PSHOME\Modules` first on `PSModulePath`, which a planted PowerShell 7-only module reproduced and settled on the mini PC (Pull request 3 on the mini PC, below).
- R1 (W7 and T3-5 amended; design part 5.3 unchanged). Each Job Object carries `JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_DIE_ON_UNHANDLED_EXCEPTION` beside `KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE`, so a crashing tool process, grandchildren included, ends at once with its exception code. Why: the rip, a job's drive listing, the remux and the relay command have no time limit, and a fault dialog in the desktop session held the step and the disc-phase token until answered. Cost if wrong: tool crashes leave no WER report or dump. Tests: `a_job_kills_on_close_and_lets_no_fault_dialog_hold_its_processes` and `a_guarded_child_that_raises_an_unhandled_exception_ends_with_its_code` (`win::job`).
- R2 (narrows Task T3-14's brief rule 6). After lazymkv's stored credential connects, a refusal at the second probe keeps "refused access again"; any other error is `<share> does not answer: <error>`, as the first probe reads it. Test: `a_second_probe_the_server_does_not_answer_says_so` (`shares.rs`).
- R3 (Owner decision 8 implemented as worded; T3-25-h's parse test). The vault tests' variable is set for a filter that is `win::creds`, `win::creds::` or a path inside it, and the log's first line prints it. Test: `the_platform_ignored_script_parses_and_runs_one_session_of_platform_tests` (`scripts.rs`) asserts the new lines. The final review (W6): `creds.rs`'s doc of the variable's filter follows the script.
- R4 (T3-24-a kept; the T3-24 brief's read-back detail). The read-back compares with the list as Windows writes it back (`Get-WrittenAces`), so an account whose SID Windows writes as an alias (the built-in Administrator's `LA`) is not refused on every deploy. Test: `the_task_scripts_read_back_expects_aliases_as_windows_writes_them` (`scripts.rs`, Windows only), which builds its SIDs in PowerShell at run time and prints only its verdicts, so no SID is in the source or the output (RP21). On the mini PC the `-500` account reads back as `LA`; if `windows-latest` reads it back as the literal SID, that one assertion is dropped and the fact recorded here. The final review (W14): `scripts.rs`'s failure prints redact the temporary folder's path and any account SID (`<temp>`, `<sid>`), so a failure on CI prints no account name.
- R5 (M7 amended; W2 and S28 consistent). The engine binary refuses an argument that is not Unicode before reading the others, with `lazymkv-engine: an argument is not Unicode: <the argument, U+FFFD for each unit that is not>`, exit 1, nothing created, on every system; it never exits 101 (`lazymkv_engine::unicode_args`). Why: since pull request 3 the Windows binary runs the engine, and a lossy `--data` named another folder, which the engine created, protected and ran on. The finding's "before, it was refused with exit 1" was not right: the Windows binary refused every start before pull request 3. Tests: `unicode_arguments_pass_through_in_order` and `an_argument_that_is_not_unicode_is_refused_by_name` (`crates/engine/src/lib.rs`), and `an_argument_that_is_not_unicode_is_refused` (`crates/engine/tests/binary.rs`), one test for every system in place of the two legs of `an_argument_that_is_not_unicode_is_read_lossily`, which kills an engine that starts after a fixed 30 s. The final review (W8): `binary.rs`'s module doc names the exit 1 for an argument that is not Unicode on every system.
- R6 (W8 amended). The drive watcher's window stops on a private `WM_APP` message that `WinDrives`' drop posts (`STOP`); an outside `WM_CLOSE` is ignored. Why: an outside `WM_CLOSE` ended the watcher for good while the engine ran on, so no disc change was reported until a restart. The trigger is corrected: it needs a process at high integrity on the engine's desktop. Cost if wrong: a drop that never ends the window thread would hang the engine's stop; two tests bound it. Tests: `an_outside_wm_close_leaves_the_watcher_running` and V4's `the_window_lets_a_logoff_or_a_restart_go_on` (`win::drives`), each ending with a drop bounded at 5 s.
- R7 (no ruling; W10, PF3-S10-12-2 and K12 unchanged). Each in-flight `OVERLAPPED` of the pipe sits in an `UnsafeCell` (`overlapped_on`), reached only through its pointer, since the kernel writes its fields until the operation ends while `result_of` and `cancel` hold a shared reference to it.
- R8 (design part 6.1's "only when the first attempt is refused with ERROR_ACCESS_DENIED or ERROR_LOGON_FAILURE ... any other error means the share is unreachable", and design part 13's rows 4.3 and 7, amended in this file only; P7's order and W14 kept; Owner decision 11's class). The stored credential is tried when Windows refuses the session's credential or its guest logon: 5, 86, 1272, 1326, 1327, 1328, 1329, 1330, 1331, 1385, 1793, 1907 or 1909 (`REFUSED`; the round's final review added 1328 logon hours, 1329 workstation, 1385 logon type not granted, 1793 account expired and 1907 password must change, W10, since R8's reason covers them and none is a wrong-password code); the same set marks a refused stored credential at connect, and a refusal at the probe after a connection; never on a network error. Why: design part 6.1's own reason for its two codes is "how Windows reports that the session holds no credential the server accepts"; 1272 (a guest session Windows refuses) and the account-state codes are that case, and a NAS that maps unknown users to guest would otherwise wait in AwaitingShare forever, ignoring the credential stored for it. The finding's severity is corrected to Important. Cost if wrong: one connect per stored value after a refusal the credential cannot cure, remembered per value; no lockout path is added. Tests: `a_refused_session_access_falls_back_to_the_stored_credential_once` (in place of `access_denied_and_logon_failure_fall_back_to_the_stored_credential_once`, over all thirteen codes), with `denied_with_no_readable_credential_says_so`, `a_refused_stored_credential_is_not_tried_again_until_it_changes` and `no_text_holds_the_sentinel_user_or_password` extended (`shares.rs`). A real 1272 cannot be seen on the mini PC, which has no guest-mapping share.
- R9 (declined, by design). Design part 6.1 names `ERROR_SESSION_CREDENTIAL_CONFLICT` and rules that the engine reports it by name and does not retry. No engine change can free the session's own SMB session: an implicit UNC session has no use record to cancel, and the remedy (grant the session's identity the share, or remove the Windows credential that authenticates it) lies outside the engine. The conflict follows only a first-probe 5 after the server authenticated the session; 1326 and R8's added codes set up no session.
- R10 with R49 (T3-4-c amended). A path that is not absolute in std's sense, of any length, or whose wide form reaches 248 units, and that does not start with `\\?\` or `\??\`, is made absolute with `std::path::absolute` (`GetFullPathNameW`) and given the verbatim prefix, as std's `get_long_path` does. Why: a short relative path whose absolute form passed `MAX_PATH` reached std's `File::create` but failed at `MoveFileExW`, against T3-4-c's aim that the swap keeps std's reach. Cost if wrong: an absolute path wrongly resolved names the same file. Tests: `a_short_relative_path_is_made_absolute_and_verbatim` and `verbatim_prefixes_each_absolute_form` (`win::files`), the second a pin that passed before the change.
- R11 with R33 (amends Task T3-17's brief for `check_space`; W14 kept). `check_space` reaches each root once, staging, then `make_root`, then the library, and writes each access line right after its own root's reach; a share that does not answer costs one timeout, and job.log names only accesses this check used. Before, staging was reached twice, and a staging share that did not answer still left a `share access for` line. Tests: `an_unreachable_staging_is_reached_once_and_logs_no_stale_access` and `a_reachable_staging_is_reached_once_at_check_space` (`engine_disc_phase.rs`), over the fake shares' reach counters (listed under R20, below).
- R12 (W10 and PF3-S10-12-2 brought in line, not amended; departs from Task T3-11's brief text). A token read that fails because the client's process id names no process (`OpenProcess`'s 87) is `ConnectionAborted`, a client gone before its check, passed over with no log line; any other failure stays a refusal (`token_unread`). The trigger is corrected: it needs every handle to the client's process to be closed. Cost if wrong: if 87 could arise for a live client, its refusal would go unlogged, though still refused. Test: `a_token_read_that_finds_no_process_is_a_client_gone_and_any_other_failure_a_refusal` (`win::pipe`).
- R13 (no ruling; W10 and PF3-S10-12-3 met). `MemoryIpc::accept` hands out no stream once `close` ran, so the fake now meets W10's and PF3-S10-12-3's close contract: a stream that comes while `close` runs is dropped, and its client reads end of file. Test: `a_stream_that_comes_while_close_runs_is_not_handed_out` (`fakes.rs`).
- R14 (W10 and design part 3.3 restored). When the next instance cannot be created, the instance just taken listens again, so the pipe name is never unheld and a recovery never joins another process's pipe without `FILE_FLAG_FIRST_PIPE_INSTANCE`; its client is served by the next accept. The finding's severity is corrected to Minor. Test: `a_next_instance_that_cannot_be_created_leaves_the_name_held_and_the_server_serves_on` (`win::pipe`, through the test-only `bind_capped`).
- R15 (W24's and T3-22-b's `COMMAND_WRITABLE` condition and text amended; W24 is on Owner decision 11's list, and its list of refusals is unchanged). `COMMAND_WRITABLE` also when `lazymkvctl` itself is a file a non-elevated process could write; a missing `lazymkvctl` stays `not_started`. Its text is now `lazymkvctl or its folder can be written by a non-elevated process, so the engine will not run it (design part 3.6)`. Cost if wrong: every `command` refuses, and the owner runs plan or decide locally by path (design part 9.5's first form). Tests: `a_writable_lazymkvctl_in_a_protected_folder_is_refused` (`engine_command.rs`); `a_missing_lazymkvctl_is_refused_as_not_started` now marks the missing path writable, as `WinHost` answers for a path it cannot read (W17), and so pins the `is_file` guard.
- R16 (deferred to phase 5). The fix is an abort hook on `IpcStream` that S52's drop calls, not a write timeout, which would cut a slowly paged `command` (W10, T3-22-a); it crosses both transports and adds a second wait-and-cancel path to the overlapped write. Phase 2 meets the case with `lazymkvctl watch`: it is bounded per client, uses no CPU, recovers when the client reads or exits, K12 holds, and a stop waits at most `CLIENT_DRAIN`. It moves from "Noted, not scheduled" to the Phase 5 list (Left for later pull requests, below).
- R17 (S13's `write_atomic` description and design part 7.4's write discipline extended; T3-4-a and RR1-2 unchanged). A `write_atomic` that fails after its temporary file was made removes the file and returns that failure's own error. Why: a file written once per job (the profile, decide.json, rip-summary.json) could keep a stray temp file in a failed job's folder for good. Tests: `a_write_whose_move_fails_leaves_no_temporary_file` (`crates/platform/src/lib.rs`), and `win::files`' held-target test, which now also finds no `state.json.lazymkv-tmp` after the failed write.
- R18 (no ruling; the tests table's `engine_disc_phase.rs` row stays true). `a_missing_staging_root_under_an_existing_parent_is_created_at_check_space` now stops in AwaitingSpace, so it pins that CheckSpace itself makes the root before its space check; a mutation that took `make_root` out of the check failed it.
- R19 (T3-22-a gains its test; T3-22-c behaviour kept; T8-d respected). The relay's `forward` takes its kill as a closure, so a test drives it over a held client. Test: `forward_waits_for_room_in_a_held_clients_queue_and_keeps_every_line` (`relay.rs`), over `ipc_server`'s test-only `held_writer`, which fails with the dropping `send` in place of `send_waiting`; `engine_command.rs`'s burst test keeps the whole stack's order, since the memory pipe's writer never blocks.
- R20 (no ruling; W16, R23.4 and R23.7 gain tests that can fail). `FakeHost`'s clock can be moved (`set_now_ms`), and the rip-summary `started` tests move it during the rip and before the restart, so a `started` read at VerifyFile instead of from the record fails them. Tests: `the_fake_hosts_clock_moves_only_when_set` (`fakes.rs`), and the changed `the_rip_summary_has_started_from_the_rip_start_between_bytes_and_elapsed_s` and `a_restart_between_rip_and_verify_keeps_started` (`engine_disc_phase.rs`). The same round gave the fake shares reach and free-space counters and a delayed reach answer (`reaches`, `free_reads`, `set_reach_after`), which R11 with R33, R34 and R28 use: `the_fake_counts_reaches_and_free_space_reads` and `set_reach_after_answers_as_before_then_the_new_answer`.
- R21 (no ruling; T3-17-a gains a whole-engine test pinning the refined promise at 17). Test: `a_resumed_remux_step_refines_the_rebuilt_promise_before_another_jobs_rip_check` (`engine_restart.rs`), which fails without the `refine_remux_promise` call; `a_rebuilt_remux_promise_counts_against_another_jobs_rip_check` now leaves 8618 free, so its other job waits with the estimate or the refined promise. The planned acceptance filter `remux_promise` does not match the new test's name; it ran under `promise`.
- R22 (no ruling; T8-a). The fake shares' `access` is None while their reach is Unreachable, so the fake now meets `ShareConnector::access`'s doc. Test: `the_fake_reports_the_access_it_was_given` extended (`fakes.rs`).
- R23 (W3 gains a test that can fail; Owner decision 11 untouched). `a_native_engine_refuses_a_data_folder_owned_by_the_user` (`engine_native.rs`) holds the pipe's name first, as another process would, so the folder's text proves that the folder check comes before the pipe; with the pipe bound before the folder, the test fails with the held-pipe text.
- R24 (no ruling; the refusal switch W13 allows). `MemorySecrets::refuse_sets` makes every later `set` fail, storing nothing, as Credential Manager's can. Tests: `memory_secrets_can_refuse_every_set` (`fakes.rs`) and `a_secret_the_store_refuses_is_named_and_its_share_not_listed` (`engine_ipc.rs`).
- R25 (W15 extended to the Remux step's partial folder; consistent with M12; design parts 5.7 step 13 and 6.5 unchanged). Remux makes only the partial folder inside an existing library root and never the root; a missing root fails the remux naming it (`<key>: the library root <root> is not a folder (<error>); the engine never creates a library root, so the remux was not started, and a retry once the folder exists runs it`), and a retry once the folder exists runs it. A repeat run (design part 6.3) creates `repeat-<n>\Movies` before pointing `movies_root` at it, as the design's "fresh, empty subfolder" and M12 already assume. Whether the engine should create a missing library root, the finding's alternative, is the owner's later call; the recommendation is no, W15 and M12 standing. Cost if wrong: a job whose library root is missing fails at Remux, naming the root, instead of making it. Test: `a_missing_library_root_fails_the_remux_and_is_never_created` (`engine_file_phase.rs`).
- R26 (W21's mechanism amended: for `staging_root`, `space::make_root` replaces `space::root_ready`, its aim unchanged; W15 amended: the staging root is also made at AwaitingShare's re-check). AwaitingShare's re-check makes the staging root with `make_root` once every root answered, so a root that cannot be made keeps the job waiting; `root_ready` is now `make_root`'s private first step. Two existing self-test tests, `a_share_recheck_reaches_the_roots_its_return_uses` and `a_share_recheck_waits_while_the_free_space_cannot_be_read`, now take their staging root from a `Scratch` folder instead of the literal `/staging`, which the re-check would now make (Pull request 3 on the mini PC, below); their assertions are unchanged. Cost if wrong: a retry makes the staging root's last folder just before the CheckSpace that would have made it. Tests: `a_share_recheck_waits_while_the_staging_root_cannot_be_made` (amended: ShareBack and the made folder), and, Unix only, `a_share_recheck_waits_while_the_staging_root_cannot_be_created` (`selftest.rs`, a read-only parent; when run as root, which writes through the mode, it writes a `skipped:` line and returns; the final review, W16).
- R27 (W23 widened, as T3-20-b did; review round 1 of pull request 2's V1 sentence "a waiting, failed or held job gets nothing" narrowed for AwaitingLookup and AwaitingMakeMkv). A refused `LookupPending`, `LookupAnswered` or `EngineRestart` in AwaitingLookup, and a refused `MakeMkvChanged` in AwaitingMakeMkv, are kept and fed again at the refeed timer (`kept_on_refusal`). Why: the first three ended the hourly lookup chain; the last was lost for good, because MakeMKV's state had already recorded the change, so neither a retry nor a restart saw it again. The noted item on a refused `LookupPending` is closed (Left for later pull requests, below), and AwaitingNetwork's `NetworkRetry` is noted under "Never reached in phase 2". Tests: `the_events_kept_after_a_refused_persist_are_those_nothing_else_feeds_again` (`owner/tests.rs`, eight kept and seven not), `an_awaiting_lookup_jobs_refused_lookup_pending_is_kept_and_its_hourly_timer_restarts`, `an_awaiting_lookup_jobs_refused_lookup_answered_is_kept_and_the_job_files` and `a_refused_engine_restart_in_awaiting_lookup_is_kept_and_starts_the_hourly_timer` (`engine_restart.rs`), and `a_refused_makemkv_changed_is_kept_and_fed_again_after_makemkv_recorded_the_change` (`engine_ipc.rs`).
- R28 (W22 amended: Decide asks `reach` of the library root first "and again once its library reads are done, before anything is written or posted"; W22 stands under Owner decision 11; decide's inputs and output for error-free reads unchanged, R-B1). A library share that no longer answers after Decide's reads fails Decide with `DecisionFailed { Unknown }` and `the library share holding <root> does not answer (<why>); what was read of the library was not used, and a retry once the share answers decides again`, with no decide.json and no decision or collision recorded. Why: std reads Windows' 53 and 67 as NotFound, so a share that dropped during Decide's reads read as an empty library. Cost if wrong: one more share-root probe per Decide. Test: `a_library_share_that_stops_answering_during_decide_fails_it_and_writes_nothing` (`engine_file_phase.rs`, through `set_reach_after`).
- R29 (no ruling; W10, PF3-S10-12-2 and K12 unchanged). The pipe server's accept passes every operating-system error of its steps through `not_closed` once, at the trait's boundary (`accept_steps`), instead of at four inner sites.
- R30 (T3-17-a and S75 not amended). `refine_remux_promise` replaces only a held Remux promise (`Promises::replace`) instead of checking the step's `CancelToken`. No behaviour changes: every way into Remuxing holds the promise before the step starts, and the owner releases it only after the step ends. Fixed after a second look overturned the first verifier's decline. Why: the guard's stated ordering was not the one that holds, and the safety now rests on `space.rs` alone. Cost if wrong: none. Test: `the_remux_step_replaces_the_estimate_with_the_staged_sizes` (`space.rs`) gains a leg in which a released promise stays released.
- R31 (S57's "the private Kill" gains "public for the listings", mechanism only; W20's behaviour unchanged). A drive listing carries the public `Kill` that ended it (`DriveListing::kill`, in place of `past_limit`), which the self-test and a job's listing read instead of `past_limit` and `timed_out`. Tests: `makemkv_driver.rs`'s listing tests read `Kill::Limit`, `Kill::Cancel` and `Kill::None`.
- R32 (declined, mandated). Design part 3.5 sets the list "at every start", and W6 records the cost. Skipping the set when the root already holds the list would leave stale inherited entries below (a moved-in entry, or a root set without propagation) and would undercut V3's walk. The cost note is in V3's W6 amendment, above.
- R34 (no ruling). A rip failure's staging health reads free space only from a staging share that answered (`staging_health_of`). Test: `a_staging_share_that_does_not_answer_reads_no_free_space` (`steps.rs`).
- R35 (the T3-10 interface wording of `wait_for_exit`). `wait_for_exit` waits on a channel the owner thread's end closes, a panic included, instead of polling every 20 ms for the engine's life. No new test: `wait_for_exit_returns_after_a_shutdown_request` and `a_forced_shutdown_from_the_handle_needs_no_client` (`engine_ipc.rs`) cover it.
- R36 (amends W15's self-test sentence). Within one self-test, a root on a volume whose share did not answer takes that answer without asking again; a volume that answered is asked for each root. Fixed after a second look overturned the first verifier's decline. Why: the owner's three roots share one NAS share, only one check runs at a time, a stop joins the check thread, and `reach` has no time bound until phase 3, so three timeouts held the self-test, every queued retry re-check and a stop. Cost if wrong: a share that comes back between two root checks shows as down for that run. Tests: `a_share_that_does_not_answer_is_asked_once_per_selftest`, `a_share_that_answers_is_asked_again_for_each_root` and `another_volume_is_still_asked_after_one_did_not_answer` (`selftest.rs`).
- R37 (declined, by design, T3-17-a). The second read is one listing and one stat per title, next to a remux of tens of gigabytes. The same read is the only refinement on a restart, a return, a Retry or an unreadable Decided check, and skipping it on the normal path would need promise provenance and its own tests.
- R38 (declined, T3-5-b). `main_thread_handle` and `spawn_with_attributes` are nightly-only on 1.99.0, `NtResumeProcess` is undocumented, and a hand-written `CreateProcessW` would give up std's handle discipline. The cost is a few milliseconds per spawn. Revisit with a toolchain that stabilizes `main_thread_handle`.
- R39 (declined, cost over benefit). A handful of token builds of tens of microseconds per step, against tool runs of minutes. Caching would break `WinHost`'s "holds no handle between calls".
- R40 (no ruling; the test client only). The file-phase retry test sends `retry` through the shared test `Client` (`tests/common`), not a copy of it, so pull request 3's pre-flight PF3-S21-22-2 gains a third consumer.
- R41 with R42 (no ruling). `lazymkv-platform`'s own tests compile `fakes` and use core's `Scratch` and the fakes' `MemorySecrets`, not copies in `testutil` (design part 3's "behind a `fakes` feature" still holds for every other crate); the test count is unchanged. The temporary folder holds no new `lazymkv-*` entry after the crate's run (Pull request 3 on the mini PC, below). R42 had two removal guards, not three.
- R43 (no ruling; T3-2-a, T3-2-b, T3-2-c and W5 unchanged). The pipe test's lowered token uses `host.rs`'s `owned` and `set_medium_integrity`, not copies of them.
- R44 (no ruling; T3-2-a, T3-2-b, T3-2-c and W5 unchanged). The eject and volume-lock control codes come from `windows-sys` and the drive type from `drives`; the literals of `every_step_runs_after_an_earlier_one_fails` still pin them to the values experiment 7 ran.
- R45 (no ruling; T3-2-a, T3-2-b, T3-2-c and W5 unchanged). `last_error` sits in `win` and every module's `GetLastError` read goes through it.
- R46 (declined). The two uses of 5, 32 and 33 are two retry layers with their own policies (RR1-2 around every `write_atomic` error, T3-4-a around `MoveFileExW`), and the read arm's 32 and 33 deliberately exclude 5. A shared predicate would cover two of the three production sites, add a cross-crate Windows error-code API, and tie the layers together. `windows-latest` pins the contract (`files.rs` and `store.rs`).
- R47 (no ruling; T3-2-a, T3-2-b, T3-2-c and W5 unchanged). The mutex, eject, token and folder modules own their handles with `OwnedHandle` and their `LocalAlloc` memory with `win::LocalMemory`; four out-pointers that had no null check now get one, on paths Windows does not reach. R47 had more sites than four. The final review (W15): `mutex.rs`'s `refusal_by_owner` returns early on a null owner, so the pointer is read only inside the non-null branch, as `owner_of` does. CodeQL's alerts stay at zero after the push.
- R48 (no ruling; T3-2-c and W17 unchanged). The token module reads the elevation type and opens the linked token (`elevation_type`, `linked_token`), which `WinHost`'s filtered token uses instead of its own copy. The linked branch runs only with a split token, in the desktop session; over SSH and on `windows-latest` the token is Default. Test: `a_linked_token_exists_exactly_when_the_token_is_split` (`win::token`).
- R50 (no ruling). The share connector's target takes its key from `volume_key`, so the two cannot differ.
- R51 with R52 (no ruling). The steps take the plan kind's library root from `space::library_root` (R51), and the head of every sentence about a library share that does not answer from `library_unanswered` (R52), each sentence byte for byte as before.
- R53 (no ruling). `recheck` reads the shares from its own `CheckEnv` instead of a second copy the owner passes in; `CheckEnv.shares`' doc names `recheck` among its readers (the final review, W9).
- R54 (no ruling; T3-22-a's semantics unchanged). `take_one` takes one from a client's count with `try_update`, which leaves 0 alone; `try_update`, since `fetch_update` is deprecated on 1.99.0. Test: `take_one_takes_one_and_never_goes_below_zero` (`ipc_server.rs`).

The design's letter and the rulings this round amends (Owner decision 11's class): the design's letter, recorded in this
file only, for V3 (design part 3.5's second bullet widened to the entries below), V5 (part 3.5's console sentence and
part 4.4's thread list), V10 (part 9.5's relative paths) and R8 (part 6.1's codes and part 13's rows 4.3 and 7); on
Owner decision 11's list, W8 (R6), W21 (R26), W22 (R28) and W24 (R15) are amended, and W10 is restored to its letter
(R12, R14, R29); the other planner rulings amended are W7 (V1, V9, R1), W15 (R25, R26, R36), W18 (V2), W19 (V4, V8), M7
(R5) and T3-4-c (R10, R49); and Task T3-18's `snapshot_aside` contract (V12) and F1's sentence on `makemkv_debug_log`
(V14) change too; the final review widened R8's codes (W10) and the task script's checks (W11, W12). None needed the
owner's yes before it was built; each stands until the owner overturns it.

The owner may overturn any of them.

### Pull request 4: the planner's rulings (T4-R1 to T4-R23)

The planner's rulings of 2026-11-04 (the plan's Owner prerequisites item 26), where the design leaves the letter open;
each names, in the plan, the task that builds it. On 2026-11-04 the owner answered the four that asked a yes (T4-R15,
T4-R17, T4-R19 and T4-R21), overturned T4-R17, and let every other ruling stand.

- T4-R1 (status words, design part 7.1). `lookup-status.txt`'s keys are `dischash`, `discid`, `search` (the cleaned name), `search-tagged` (J8's second search), `search-entry` (S47), `tmdb <kind>/<id>` (the record) and `season-<n>`, each first word unique so that dev's `lookup_statuses` holds, and `job.log`'s `lookup <word>: ...` lines use the same words, the record's staying `tmdb record`. R23.2's test guard counts per kind: every TheDiscDB, record and season URL of a scenario's merged `http.json` needs a status line of its kind, the search URLs at least as many `search`, `search-tagged` or `search-entry` lines, no line may end in `000`, and a URL under neither root (a webhook) needs none.
- T4-R2 (one search file). `tmdb-search.json` holds the page the plan reads: the entry-title page when core's `entry_search_title` names a title, else the merged name page, its `queries` saying which; once a title is named, a name search's answer updates the book but never writes the file, and `rebuild_book` reads the file as the entry page when an answered `search-entry` line is there. Amended during execution by T3-R1 and T3-R2, below.
- T4-R3 (partial J8). The two name searches are two lookups, TMDb stays pending until both answer, and the file is written with the cleaned page and one query, then rewritten merged with both queries.
- T4-R4 (seasons). With a parsed season, that season only, fetched even when the record does not list it (a 404 is a miss); with none, every season the record lists, season 0 included (design part 8.2 item 5's letter), while spec 8.1's runtime check still leaves season 0 out. Each answer's episodes are spliced into `tmdb-record.json`, and no season file is saved.
- T4-R5 (the entry search's query). The entry's title alone (S20: `query` is the only parameter); core checks the year.
- T4-R6 (the remembered identity in the chain). A round's `ChainInputs.remembered` is the TMDb identity of the job's own `remembered.json` copy when it exists (V14), else of the data folder's `remembered/<FP>.json`, whose inner fingerprint must match, ASCII case ignored; a refused file gives none, and the round writes the `engine.log` line Plan writes.
- T4-R7 (stale). A copy is stale when its `fetched_at` is missing or malformed, or when today is later than `fetched_at` plus six calendar months, the day clamped to the month's last day (2026-08-31 plus six months is 2027-02-28); a `fetched_at` after today is fresh, and a malformed `today` makes nothing stale. A missing `fetched_at` makes a copy stale, and a missing copy under a `200` status line counts as deleted, only in an engine job folder (one holding `state.json`). The cleanup deletes only a copy whose `fetched_at` reads as a date more than six months old.
- T4-R8 (restart stays offline). `rebuild_book` reads a stale or deleted TMDb copy as `Pending { Stale(<file>) }`, so the engine's start sends no request (design part 4.4) and the next round or refetch asks again.
- T4-R9 (where the engine refetches). Before every Plan (a job's first finds its copies fetched that day and asks nothing), before every Decide, and on the relay's own thread before `child_words`, with the TMDb ID from the plan in `state.json` and the queries from the file or, for a deleted page, `core::lookup::search_queries`. The relay's exit 3 is a `command_output` line `lazymkvctl: <reason>` on standard error, then `ok {"exit_code": 3}`, and `retune --jobs` keeps W25's `RETUNE_WAITS` refusal (exit 2); its exit 3 arrives with pull request 5. Amended at review round 1 of pull request 4 (R2, with RR1-R2a and RR1-R2b), below: a TV record's refetch keeps the record and the seasons it fetched, stops at its first pending season and owes that season and the ones after it, which the next round or refetch asks alone; a season still owed waits a Decide and fails the relay with exit 3, never a Plan. Amended at the same round (R4), below: a refetch answers 200 only the seasons it merged and rewrites `lookup-status.txt`. Amended at the same round (R1), below: the relay's refetch thread is not joined at shutdown.
- T4-R10 (dev's fetch). `Ctx` gains `http`, which only `Ctx::with_live_transport`, called by the two binaries' `main`, sets to `UreqTransport::new()`; `TMDB_TOKEN` is read from `ctx.env`. The gate sits at the `plan`, `decide` and `retune` command entries only, so the engine's in-process use of dev's library never fetches, and a folder holding `state.json` (an engine job folder) and a `--tmdb-search` or `--tmdb-record` file given by hand are read as they are. Amended at review round 1 of pull request 4 (R3, R2 and R9), below: dev asks a TV record's seasons only when `tmdb-record.json` is among the stale copies, writes nothing for a stale `tmdb-record.json` with a season still pending and exits 3 naming the season, and writes each copy with `lazymkv_platform::write_atomic`.
- T4-R11 (the cleanup's schedule and reach). `EngineConfig.cleanup_minutes` (None in tests, `Some(60)` in the binary) drives a thread of its own, not `Services.timers`; the owner thread builds the pass's job list, and the cleanup thread deletes after `ShareConnector::reach` of the job's own settings' `staging_root` (ENG-19), asked once per root per pass (FR-1). A job whose book a round holds is skipped that pass, only `<32 lower-case hex>-<n>` folders are deleted, and `engine.log` gets a `cleanup: <job id>: ...` line for each deleted copy and each deleted staging folder, and one `cleanup: the staging root <root> was not reached: ...` line per pass for each root not reached (FR-1). Amended at review round 1 of pull request 4 (R6), below: the pass holds a job's book only while it deletes that job's stale TMDb copies, never across the share's reach or the staging folder's deletion.
- T4-R12 (transport). `HttpTransport` gains `post` and `connect(host, port)`; no redirect is followed (a 3xx is `Status(3xx)`, so a bearer token never reaches another host), no proxy is read from the environment, an answer of 8 MiB or longer is `Unreachable(BODY_TOO_LONG)`, errors use a fixed vocabulary, and a POST carries `Content-Type: application/json`, without which Home Assistant leaves `trigger.json` empty.
- T4-R13 (notifier). With both URLs unset nothing is logged (design part 8.6: "skipped"). A failed channel's reason is exactly one of `<name>: HTTP <n> after 3 attempts`, `<name>: no answer within 10 s after 3 attempts`, `<name>: <transport words> after 3 attempts` or `<name>: <outcome> after <n> attempt[s] (the engine was stopping)`, several joined with `"; "`. At stop the sink skips every wait and gives each channel one attempt per queued notification, and `EngineHandle::stop` waits at most `NOTIFY_DRAIN_S` (15 s) for the notifier.
- T4-R14 (the self-test's webhook checks). `ha_webhook_url` then `webhook_url`, after `tmdb_token`; an unset URL is ok (`not set; its notifications are skipped`); the check is a TCP (Transmission Control Protocol) connection to the URL's host and port within 10 s, with no TLS handshake, and its details show `<host>:<port>` only. Amended at review round 1 of pull request 4 (R5), below: the host's name is resolved within the same 10 s, on a helper thread, so a slow resolver gives a timeout, never a wait past it.
- T4-R15 (a webhook value's shape). `secret_set` refuses a webhook value that is not an http or https URL with a host, with `<name> could not be stored: the value must be an http or https URL with a host`, never echoing it; an addition beyond the sources, so the owner's yes was asked: yes, 2026-11-04.
- T4-R16 (W26's doubles). `NoDelivery` and `NO_DELIVERY` are removed (by Task T4-17, with the binary's change), `OfflineTransport` stays as a test double with `OFFLINE = "the offline transport asks nothing (a test double)"`, and `Harness::use_binary_services` becomes `use_offline_services`.
- T4-R17 (dev's readers). `plan`'s reader and `census` read TheDiscDB answers with an S46 reader of their own and read any `search`, `search-tagged`, `search-entry` or `season-<n>` key that is present and neither `200` nor `404` as TMDb pending, whether or not `--tmdb-search` is given (SR8 unchanged); `hit_rate::lookup` was to stay the Python port. Overturned by the owner on 2026-11-04: `hit_rate::lookup` is aligned with S46 too, and `hit-rate`'s pinned lines are re-pinned where they change (none changed over the clone, Task T4-15). Amended at review round 1 of pull request 4 (R11), below: `hit_rate::lookup` is gone, and `hit-rate` calls `discdb_answer` directly, as `census` does.
- T4-R18 (4 MiB). `lazymkvctl` checks the whole serialised request line against `MAX_LINE_BYTES` before connecting, for every command, `secret set` included, and exits 2 with `REQUEST_TOO_LONG`. Amended at review round 1 of pull request 4 (R8), below: the line is serialised once, measured, and that line is sent.
- T4-R19 (live records). A shape record replaces every scalar with its JSON type name, keeps objects' keys, and turns an array into a one-element array of its elements' merged shape; the live tests date their records, and the slim records' `fetched_at`, with the UTC (Coordinated Universal Time) date, since `clients` has no `platform` edge; the replay of saved records lives in `clients`' tests only, a narrowing of design part 8.5's "The core and clients tests replay the saved records", so the owner's yes was asked: yes, 2026-11-04.
- T4-R20 (the several-playlist item). Pull request 4 closes, on CI, the identified case of pull request 3's ultra-high-definition (UHD) discs with several feature-length playlists: synthetic discs with two feature-length playlists and with three of the same length, each with a TheDiscDB record whose `MainMovie` item names the main playlist, rip that one title and file. The unidentified case stays open: a disc with several same-length playlists that TheDiscDB does not know, identified by TMDb alone, still has no main-title pick and plans copy mode (design part 5.5, phase 3).
- T4-R21 (the unwritable remembered copy, the second half of the V14 item). Accepted as V14 left it, with no code: Decide reads only the job's own copy (R-B1), so a copy that cannot be written plans with the remembered identity and titles, decides without the track overrides, and the job's `engine.log` line names the failure, pinned by `an_unwritable_remembered_copy_decides_without_overrides_and_logs_it`; a narrowing of engine.md's V14 item, so the owner's yes was asked: yes, 2026-11-04.
- T4-R22 (the census). The main title is the longest by seconds, the lower number on a tie; an unknown title prints `?`; the English count takes `eng` streams only and leaves out derived-forced-only ones; a disc is UHD when `meta.json`'s `format` is `bluray` and the main title's height is 2000 or more; a recording without `scan.txt` is listed with `?` columns (exit 0), and an unreadable file exits 2 (hit-rate's crash rule). The test's recordings are built at test time from public files under `corpus/`, and no test reads the clone.
- T4-R23 (design part 4.1's crates table). The `dev` row's dependencies become `core`, `ocr` (R-A5) and `clients` (Owner decision 25), recorded in the crates table above; the design file stays unedited. Amended at review round 1 of pull request 4 (R9), below: the row gains `platform`, on the owner's yes of 2026-11-06.

### Pull request 4: the task-level rulings

Each task of pull request 4's plan recorded its rulings beside its steps, named `T4-<task>-<letter>`; only Task T4-4
recorded one.

**Task T4-4** (TV records and their seasons)
- T4-4-a. A `200` season answer that is no season is unparsed, never a season with no episodes: `parse_season_answer` gives `<shown>: the season answer has no episodes` when `episodes` is absent or null, and serde's sentence for another value that is not an array.
