### Pull request 4: rulings made during execution

The controller of the plan's execution made these while the tasks ran, from 2026-11-04 to 2026-11-06. The ones named PF4
answer a pre-flight check of the plan (2026-11-04), which found 58 findings, 7 of them blocking, each ruled and the
plan's text fixed before Task T4-1 committed it; the ones listed here change what the plan's tasks build, test or ask,
and the others settled the plan's wording, anchors and counts only. The others keep the names of the execution's ledger:
`T<task>-R<n>` for a finding of a task's review, `T<task>-M<n>` for a minor one, and `T<task>-CI` or `T-CI-<n>` for a CI
run, where `<task>` is the task's number in this plan (T3-R1 is a ruling on Task T4-3, not a pull request 3 name). Each
line says what was decided and, where it was recorded, why and the cost if wrong.

**The pre-flight check**
- PF4-1 (Task T4-1's version pick, blocking). Task T4-1 Step 1 reads crates.io with `lock_delta.py --published-by 2026-10-21`: each crate is the newest release, neither yanked nor a prerelease, published on or before 2026-10-21, the owner's plan-time cutoff, so `ureq` is 3.5.0, and the plan-time list's `utf8-zero` stands in place of `utf-8`, with no `fnv` (PF4-3). Cost if wrong: rework of Task T4-1.
- PF4-22 and PF4-23 (Task T4-10's fixtures, blocking). The refetch test gives `plan` a TMDb identity as plan's existing tests with a TheDiscDB hit do (a `dischash 200` line and a record naming `"tmdb": "6537"`), and the copy without `fetched_at` is a record `parse_recorded` accepts, so both tests prove what their names say. Cost if wrong: rework of Task T4-10.
- PF4-36 (Task T4-18's bearer assertion, blocking). The refused search is asked in every round before AwaitingLookup, so `a_new_token_reaches_the_next_round_without_a_restart` asserts that the last bearer is the new token, every earlier one the old token, and that there are at least two. Cost if wrong: rework of Task T4-18, visible in its review.
- PF4-37 (Owner decision 17's answer, blocking). The controller copies the live records into the evidence clone with one `scp -r` and makes the signed commit there, touching only the new folder, and no subagent reads or writes the clone; the plan's Global Constraints, Task T4-20 Step 2 and How to run it say so.
- PF4-49 (Task T4-9's `rebuild_book` rule against the restart tests, blocking; re-ruled after the plan's re-check). T4-R7 stands; core's `testsupport` writers of slim pages gain `"fetched_at": "2026-10-23"`, the fixed test date, in Task T4-8, and `a_200_whose_saved_file_does_not_parse_is_pending_and_no_line_is_not_asked` is re-pinned to `Pending { Stale(..) }` and renamed, since a copy whose `fetched_at` cannot be read is stale in a job folder and fetching it again is the remedy. Cost if wrong: one test's name and reason text.
- PF4-50 (Task T4-9 against `a_missing_replay_file_adds_no_flag`, blocking). Under T4-R9 a deleted TMDb copy whose status line says `200` is fetched again before the relay's words, so the test splits into `a_missing_tmdb_copy_is_fetched_again_before_the_words` and `a_missing_replay_file_outside_the_lookups_adds_no_flag`.
- PF4-8 to PF4-12 and PF4-38 (the T4-R17 overturn). Task T4-15 aligns `hit_rate::lookup` with S46 (`Ok(None)` from `parse_dischash` is a miss), renames `hit_rate_a_200_answer_that_is_not_a_disc_record_is_a_failure` to `..._is_a_miss`, and re-pins `hit-rate`'s lines where they change; the plan's Not covered line on `hit-rate` is removed.
- PF4-18 (the census's object audio). The column reads the distinct object formats of the main title's audio streams, Atmos before DTS:X, each once.
- PF4-29 (`quiet_message` and the cleanup). While the engine stops, `quiet_message` handles `Msg::CleanupDone` as the normal handler does and drops `Msg::Cleanup`.
- PF4-35 (`secret_host`). No second URL reader: `secret_host` calls `lazymkv_clients::webhook_target`, so it is None for a URL that is not http or https.
- PF4-40 (Task T4-17's native whole job). The test waits for the `filed` notification, as `whole_job.rs` does, before it asserts the list.
- PF4-45 (Task T4-19's test that runs on CI). It only checks that the public ID files exist and name the IDs; no body is replayed through a transport, so S69 is untouched.
- PF4-46 (the UTC date). The date arithmetic moves into `lazymkv_core` as `civil_date`, which dev's `civil_time` and the live tests call, rather than a second copy.
- PF4-47 (R23.2's guard, re-ruled after the plan's re-check). The guard counts per kind, as T4-R1 says, and its test adds the case its name claims: a URL under neither root needs no status line. Cost if wrong: flaky whole-job tests.
- PF4-53 (Task T4-9's tests). Each test fails before Task T4-9's code: the refetch tests assert that the record URL's request count grew by one, and the restart test reads the stale reading's own observable.

**Task T4-2**

The plan stands, parked for the owner: TMDb's `/search/multi` answers 200 with an empty list for no results, and both searches share the endpoint, so the split is implausible. Both name-search saves are gated on no entry page in hand, so the name pages stay on disk until an entry page replaces them; before it, an entry search that failed left `search 200` with no file, and every restart asked TMDb again. Cost if wrong: one extra file write per round before the entry page lands.

**The final review**
- FR-1 (final review, 2026-11-06): a cleanup pass asks `reach` once per distinct staging root and keeps the answer for the rest of the pass, writes one `cleanup: the staging root <root> was not reached: <text>; <k> job(s) skipped` line for each root not reached in place of each job's line, and reads the engine's stop flag before each job, so `lazymkvctl stop` waits on at most one share call, since the Filed jobs it reaches grow without limit and `shut_down` joins the pass; it amends T4-R11. Cost if wrong: a pass skips a root that became reachable during it, and the next hour's pass catches it.
- RR1-R2a (review round 1 of pull request #15, 2026-11-06, on R2): a season still owed after a refetch asked it is reported apart from a copy that could not be fetched; Plan drops it, since its book read already marks TMDb pending and the Ejected round gate waits, while Decide and the relay read it as a failure (a Decide in Inspecting goes to AwaitingLookup, any other gets `DecisionFailed { Unknown }`, and the relay exits 3 naming the season), so neither a Decide nor a relayed dev `decide` runs with a season missing, and a Plan never fails for a pending lookup; each copy written and each owed season merged gets its `job.log` line (`the TMDb copy <file> was fetched again`, `season <n> of the TMDb copy tmdb-record.json was fetched again`). Cost if wrong: a Decide that could have gone on waits an hour for a season TMDb is slow on.
- RR1-R2b (review round 1 of pull request #15, 2026-11-06, on R2): the relay's refetch, which runs on its own thread, takes the book with a blocking lock for its owed-season check, so it waits for a running round to end, while Plan and Decide keep `try_lock` and skip the check while a round holds the book (Decide never runs beside a round). Cost if wrong: a relayed command waits a round's length, bounded by the requests' timeouts.
- RR1-R5a (review round 1 of pull request #15, 2026-11-06, on R5): a name-resolution helper thread the system cannot start makes `connect` fail with the existing `CONNECTION_FAILED` words, rather than panicking or resolving inline with no bound. Cost if wrong: none worth naming: an existing fixed text, in a case where the system cannot start a thread.

The owner may overturn any of them.

### Review round 1 (the review of pull request #15)

The owner's twelve inline comments on pull request #15 (2026-11-06; R1 to R12, in the order they were filed), each
verified against the code at `3c92a2e` one by one: all twelve are confirmed, R1, R7 and R10 in part, and all twelve are
fixed, in 14 signed commits (`3c92a2e..1a850b1`), one per finding and two follow-ups to R2. The rulings below are the
controller's, of 2026-11-06 (verification, plan and implementation), each labelled by the finding it decides; the owner
answered the two that changed the plan, R9 and R2, on 2026-11-06 (Pull request 4: the owner's decisions, above), and
the controller's three rulings during the fixes, RR1-R2a, RR1-R2b and RR1-R5a, are under Pull request 4: rulings made
during execution, above. A parenthesis names what a finding amends or closes: a ruling of this file, or an item the
execution's ledger had parked, a task's parked note, a carry-forward item (`CF<n>`) or a minor of the final review.
Every amendment is recorded in this file only; the spec, the design text and the plan stay untouched. The workspace
passed on the mini PC after each group of fixes, the last on `1a850b1` with 3079 passed, 0 failed and 13 ignored
(Tests, below).

- R1 (reverses the final review's deferred Minor 6, a stop that waits on the relay's refetch; Ruling T3-22-d unchanged, STOPPING is still answered first). `spawn_command_refetch` detaches the relay's refetch thread and returns nothing, so `shut_down` no longer joins it, as it never joined a lookup round; a late `Msg::CommandRefetched` finds no command waiting and is dropped. Why: a `shutdown` during a relayed `plan` or `decide` waited for the refetch's TMDb requests after the client had been answered STOPPING. The verification corrected the comment's bound: each fetch stops at its first pending answer, so an unreachable TMDb costs about 20 s, and only a slow TMDb that answers each season just within 10 s reaches one timeout per season; still nothing the owner controls bounded the wait. A stop flag read between requests was not taken: it would thread through `clients`' public `refetch_record` and `refetch_search` and through the steps' refetch, which a step cancel already bounds, for a result the stop discards anyway. Cost if wrong: a detached refetch can still write a TMDb copy while the engine exits; `write_atomic` leaves the old file or the new one, so the job folder stays readable, the argument that lets rounds go unjoined. Tests: `a_shutdown_while_a_relayed_command_refetches_answers_without_waiting_for_the_refetch` (`engine_command.rs`); `the_relays_refetch_runs_off_the_owner_thread` unchanged.
- R2 (amends Task T4-9's all-or-nothing refetch, which the plan's Task T4-8 Step 4 mandated, and Ruling T4-R9; built as the owner answered on 2026-11-06; with the follow-ups RR1-R2a, `377292b`, and RR1-R2b, `855d5d5`). `refetch_record` fails only when the record itself is a miss or pending. A TV record's seasons are asked in order, and the refetch stops at the first pending one (`refetch_seasons`, `SeasonsFetched`, `RecordRefetch`, from R4's commit): both slim files are written with the record and the seasons merged before it, and that season and the ones after it stay `Pending` in the book with its reason, which `lookup-status.txt` keeps (`season-2 429`, say). When no copy is stale but the book owes a season, even one read back from `lookup-status.txt` after a restart, the next Plan, Decide or relayed command asks the owed seasons alone, splices them into `tmdb-record.json`, whose `fetched_at` stays the record's, and rewrites the status file. A season still owed gives `tmdb-record.json: season <n> could not be fetched again: <reason>`, which waits a Decide and makes the relay exit 3 but never fails a Plan (RR1-R2a). Dev writes a stale `tmdb.json` even when a season is pending, since it keeps no season, but writes nothing for a stale `tmdb-record.json` with a season pending and exits 3 with `season <n>: <reason>`, since a recording folder has no book to keep the hole. Why: one transient 429 or timeout among a show's season requests threw away the record and both files already fetched and marked the copies pending again, and the next attempt repeated every request, likely into the same rate limit. Asking the seasons after a pending one was not taken: the refetch stops at its first pending season, as the owner answered, which keeps R1's bound and does not press a rate limit that has just answered 429. Cost if wrong: after a pending answer the owed seasons wait for the next round or refetch, each attempt stopping at its first pending season, and a Decide waits for them. Tests: `a_pending_season_keeps_the_record_and_the_seasons_before_it`, `a_pending_season_stops_the_season_requests_and_leaves_the_rest_unasked` and `refetch_seasons_splices_only_the_seasons_it_fetched` (`crates/clients/src/refetch.rs`); `a_refetch_with_one_pending_season_writes_the_record_files_and_owes_that_season`, `the_next_refetch_asks_only_the_owed_season`, `an_owed_season_read_back_after_a_restart_is_asked_again`, `an_owed_season_still_pending_stays_owed_and_a_plan_goes_on`, `the_owed_seasons_are_not_asked_while_a_round_holds_the_book` and `the_relays_refetch_waits_for_a_round_holding_the_book_then_names_the_owed_season` (`crates/engine/src/file_phase.rs`); `a_pending_season_writes_the_stale_tmdb_json_and_refuses_tmdb_record_json` (`crates/dev/src/stale.rs`); `a_first_decide_with_an_owed_season_it_cannot_fetch_waits_for_its_lookup` (`engine_file_phase.rs`) and `a_relayed_plan_with_an_owed_season_it_cannot_fetch_exits_3_naming_the_season` (`engine_command.rs`).
- R3 (no ruling amended; Ruling T4-R10 and Owner decision 23 kept). `refetch_record` takes `Seasons` (`None`, `One(n)` or `Listed`) in place of an optional season; dev passes `Listed` only when `tmdb-record.json` is among a recording's stale copies, else `None`, and the engine passes `One(n)` for a parsed season, else `Listed`, so its requests are unchanged. Why: refreshing a phase 0 folder's stale `tmdb.json`, which keeps no season and has no `tmdb-record.json` beside it, asked every season the record listed, threw the answers away, and failed dev's gate with exit 3 if any one of them was pending. Cost if wrong: none found, since `slim_tmdb` keeps no season and no reader of `tmdb.json` can miss one. Tests: `a_stale_tmdb_json_alone_asks_no_season` (`crates/dev/src/stale.rs`) and `refetch_record_with_no_seasons_asks_the_record_only` (`crates/clients/src/refetch.rs`); `refetch_record_of_a_tv_show_fetches_its_seasons_again` re-pinned with `Seasons::One(2)`.
- R4 (closes CF14, parked at Task T4-9; S46's status words unchanged). After a record refetch the book's `Record` is answered 200, each season the refetch merged is answered 200 (added when the book lacked it, so the status file names it), each season TMDb answered 404 is answered 404, and a season the book held that the refetch neither merged nor reported, because the record no longer lists it, is answered 404; the search copy keeps `answer_stale`. Once the record is installed, the refetch writes `lookup-status.txt` from the book through `clients`' `status_file`, now public, and `write_atomic`, so a restart reads the same answers; a status file that cannot be written adds its sentence to the refetch's failures. Why: every stale season was answered 200, a 404'd one included, so the record had no episodes for it and the next round wrote `season-<n> 200`, which a restart never asked again. The comment's suggestion, `refetch_record` reporting the seasons it merged, is what was built. Cost if wrong: a season TMDb adds back later stays answered 404 until the record copy is stale again. Tests: `a_season_answered_404_on_refetch_is_answered_404_and_written_so`, `a_season_the_refetched_record_no_longer_lists_is_answered_404` and `only_the_seasons_a_refetch_merged_become_answered_200` (`crates/engine/src/file_phase.rs`); `a_season_answered_404_is_reported_missing` (`crates/clients/src/refetch.rs`); `refetch_record_writes_both_slim_files_with_todays_date` and `refetch_record_of_a_tv_show_fetches_its_seasons_again` re-pinned to the new return value with the same bytes.
- R5 (closes Task T4-7's parked note on an unbounded name lookup in `connect`; Ruling T4-R14's 10 s now holds for the lookup too; with RR1-R5a). `connect` resolves the host's name on a helper thread, `lazymkv-resolve`, which sends its answer over a channel that `connect` waits on for at most the transport's timeout: no answer in time is `Timeout`, a failed lookup is `NO_HOST`, and the connections get what is left of the 10 s, as before; a helper that timed out is left to finish on its own, holding only the host name, and the lookup's own error text is never read. `get` and `post` are unchanged. Why: the standard library's name lookup (`to_socket_addrs`, the system's `getaddrinfo`) blocks with no bound, so the self-test's webhook check against a host on a slow resolver could wait out the system resolver's timeout, tens of seconds on Windows with several DNS (Domain Name System) servers, before `NO_HOST`, past the 10 s the transport's doc promises. Documenting the exception was not taken, since the self-test relies on that promise. Cost if wrong: one leftover thread per timed-out webhook check until the system's resolver gives up. Tests: `a_resolution_slower_than_the_timeout_is_a_timeout_within_it`, `a_failed_resolution_is_no_host` and `a_quick_resolution_gives_its_addresses` (`crates/clients/src/live.rs`, each through a stand-in resolver); `connect_reaches_a_listening_port_and_refuses_a_closed_one` unchanged.
- R6 (amends Ruling T4-R11 and Task T4-11's brief, whose "held to the job's end" kept the book across the share's input and output; closes CF18, parked at Task T4-11). The cleanup pass holds a job's lookup book only while it deletes the job's stale TMDb copies; the retention check, the staging share's reach and the staging folder's deletion run without it, and a job whose book a round holds is still skipped whole for the pass. Why: the guard lived to the job's end, across `shares.reach` and the `remove_dir_all` over the share, and the owner thread takes that lock blocking (`lookup_message`, `on_round_done`), so a slow deletion could stall the owner. Cost if wrong: none worth naming, since the staging work reads nothing the book holds. Tests: `a_pass_holds_no_book_while_it_reaches_the_staging_share` (`crates/engine/src/cleanup.rs`); `a_job_whose_book_a_round_holds_is_skipped_this_pass` unchanged.
- R7 (no ruling). `rebuild_book` reads a job's chain inputs once and uses them for both the chain and the TV record's seasons. Why: `chain_inputs` (`meta.json`, the disc's metadata listing and the name it parses) was read a second time per job at every engine start; the verification narrowed that to a job whose book holds an answered TV record. Cost if wrong: none; the behaviour is unchanged. Tests: none new; `restart.rs`'s tests and `engine_restart.rs` pin it unchanged.
- R8 (Ruling T4-R18 kept: the check still comes before any connection, for every command). `lazymkvctl` builds a request's line once: `Exchange::ask` builds it, `within_line_limit` measures it against `MAX_LINE_BYTES`, and `talk` writes it, then its newline; `request_line` takes the payload by value, so no copy of it is made. Why: the line was serialised to be measured, dropped, and serialised again to be sent, so a 4 MiB `settings set` or a `secret set` held two full copies, and cloning the payload made a third. Cost if wrong: none; the bytes sent are unchanged. Tests: `an_exchange_sends_the_line_it_measured` (`crates/ctl/src/engine_cmds.rs`); the exact-limit and over-limit tests unchanged.
- R9 (amends the plan's Global Constraint that `dev` has no `platform` edge and writes its own temporary file, its constraint that no later task edits a `Cargo.toml` dependency list, and Ruling T4-R23's crates table; built on the owner's yes of 2026-11-06). `lazymkv-dev` depends on `lazymkv-platform` and writes each refetched copy with `write_atomic`; dev's `write_copy` and its second `.lazymkv-tmp` constant are gone, and the lock gains one dependency line and no package (The Cargo.lock delta, above). Why: `write_copy` re-implemented `write_atomic` without its `sync_all`, its removal of a temporary file an earlier crash left, or Windows' `MoveFileExW` replace with its retries, and two copies drift. Moving `write_atomic` into core was not taken: core stays free of input and output (Ruling RP19), and the Windows branch needs `windows-sys`. Cost if wrong: dev builds `lazymkv-platform`, which on Linux and macOS adds only `lazymkv-core`, already dev's, and on Windows `windows-sys`, already in the lock. Tests: none new; dev's `stale.rs` tests pin it unchanged.
- R10 (no ruling; J8's second search unchanged, its rule now defined once). `DiscName::has_tagged_form` (`crates/core/src/lookup/name.rs`) holds when the tagged and cleaned names fold apart, and core's `search_queries` and `plausible_by_name`, the chain's round and `rebuild_book` all call it. Why: the comment found the rule written in three places, and the verification four, core holding two; had one copy drifted, a restart could rebuild a tagged search the round never asks, whose `NotAsked` state keeps the lookup pending for good. Reusing `search_queries` was not taken: the round and the restart need the predicate, and `search_queries` also applies the entry-title rule. Cost if wrong: none; core's plan output and the goldens are unchanged. Tests: `has_tagged_form_holds_only_when_the_folded_forms_differ` (`name.rs`); `search_queries_follow_the_entry_or_the_two_name_forms`, the chain's tagged tests and `the_rebuilt_book_reads_the_tagged_search_line` unchanged.
- R11 (closes CF25, parked at Task T4-15; the owner's overturn of Ruling T4-R17 unchanged). `hit-rate`'s `report` calls `discdb_answer` directly, as `census` does, and the pass-through `hit_rate::lookup` and its doc are gone; `plan.rs`'s `LookupFile` doc names `discdb_answer`. Why: since Task T4-15 the wrapper only forwarded. Cost if wrong: none; `hit-rate`'s lines are unchanged. Tests: none new; `crates/dev/tests/readers.rs` and `hit_rate.rs`'s tests unchanged, the goldens byte-identical.
- R12 (Task T4-8 Step 1's slim reading of core's test writers made true). Core's `search_json` test writer drops `page`, `total_pages` and `total_results`, so it writes the slim search copy its doc names, less `queries`, and the doc says the tests also serve it as TMDb's `/search/multi` answer, since `parse_search` and `slim_search` read only `results`. Why: the doc said slim while the writer still wrote the raw page's three fields, so a later shape test could be written against a shape the engine never writes. Keeping the fields and the raw-page doc was not taken, since Task T4-8 asked for the slim reading; nor was adding `queries`, since engine tests rely on a page without it falling back to `search_queries`. Cost if wrong: none; every consumer reads only `results`. Tests: `search_json_writes_movie_and_tv_results_in_their_own_fields` (`crates/core/src/testsupport/mod.rs`) re-pinned to `results` and `fetched_at`.

The rulings this round amends: T4-R9 (R1, R2, R4), T4-R10 (R2, R3, R9), T4-R11 (R6), T4-R14 (R5, its 10 s kept),
T4-R17's `hit_rate::lookup` (R11), T4-R18 (R8, its mechanism only), T4-R23 and the plan's constraint that `dev` has
no `platform` edge (R9), and Task T4-9's all-or-nothing refetch (R2), each sentence marked where it stands. The owner
answered R9 and R2 before they were built; each other ruling stands until the owner overturns it.

The owner may overturn any of them.

### Pull request 5: the planner's rulings (T5-R1 to T5-R65)

The planner's rulings of 2026-11-06, each settling an item the notes leave open or where two sources disagree, and each
naming the task that builds it. On 2026-11-07 the owner let every ruling stand.

- T5-R1 (R2-decoders-4, R3-worker-3, R4-commands-15, R4-commands-68). The `ocr` crate is call 23's two packages; the models' build script belongs to `lazymkv-ocr-models`; engine.md's crates table records the split, and T5-26 amends design 4.1, 4.7 and 10.11 for it (T5-R50).
- T5-R2 (R3-worker-4, R4-commands-11). `lazymkvctl` links `lazymkv-ocr-models` only (E3 binds over spec 4.4's "compiled into `lazymkvctl.exe`" and design 11.1's ocrs row); T5-12 rewords spec 4.4's ocrs row and T5-26 design 11.1's.
- T5-R3 (R2-decoders-45, R6-phase0-10, R6-phase0-29). `--stream N` is ffprobe's subtitle-relative index, counted from 0 over the evidence file's subtitle tracks in track order, the `n` of `subs\sub-<n>.packets.json`; the worker maps it to the n-th track of type subtitle (17).
- T5-R4 (R2-decoders-50, R3-worker-17 to 19, R5-code-41). `--out` names the local folder `<data>\work\ocr-<job id>-<title key>\s<n>\`, outside `jobs\` (B1 (the worker's folder) amends design 10.2 step 3). The engine copies `<staging>\<job id>\evidence\<title key>.mks` to `<data>\work\ocr-<job id>-<title key>\evidence.mks` before the title's first run, moves each stream's output into the staged `subs-ocr\` after its run (both through T5-R55's checked copy), deletes a killed run's `s<n>\` and the whole title folder after the last stream, and deletes `<data>\work\` at engine start.
- T5-R5 (R2-decoders-26). A VobSub alpha of 0 to 15 maps to `alpha * 17` (0 to 255); T5-11's VobSub golden checks it.
- T5-R6 (R2-decoders-31). The prepared event is composited onto a white canvas of the video frame (the PGS composition's size, the VobSub idx frame size), and the crop's 16-pixel growth is clamped to that canvas, as phase 0's crop trial clamped to the rendered frame; T5-11's goldens check the result.
- T5-R7 (design 10.6's triggers 1 and 2). A run is a maximal block of contiguous inked rows; a line is a maximal group of runs joined across a single empty row (2 or more empty rows separate lines); trigger 1 holds when a run is taller than 1.6 times the median, trigger 2 when a line is; the median is over the line heights of the stream's events with one or two lines, and without such an event neither trigger holds.
- T5-R8 (R3-worker-5). Worker exit 3 leaves the stream unread with the first line of its standard error as the reason; exit 2 fails the step (`DecisionFailed { Unknown }`, `lazymkv-ocr refused its arguments: <line>`), an engine bug; any other code (101 is a panic) leaves the stream unread with `lazymkv-ocr exited with code <c>` and quotes no standard error; a run that ends with no exit code leaves it unread with `lazymkv-ocr ended without an exit code`; a worker that is not beside the engine fails the step with `OCR_MISSING` (`lazymkv-ocr is not beside the engine, so step 10 cannot read subtitles`).
- T5-R9 (R3-worker-24). `{s}` is the cap in seconds with one decimal (`1800.6` for 887 events, `60.0` at the floor); `{k}` counts the whole lines of the killed run's `sub-<n>.ocr.jsonl` (a torn last line does not count), read before its folder is deleted; `{n}` is the scan stream number, as `Noted.stream`.
- T5-R10 (R3-worker-60). The cap stays fixed: the engine and `lazymkv-dev ocr-trial read` always compute it with `time_cap_s(events)`; the seams tests set short are `tools::ocr::run_worker`'s `cap` parameter and `read_streams`' `cap` function (T5-R58), and neither is a setting.
- T5-R11 (R2-decoders-47, R3-worker-15, R6-phase0-14). A text subtitle stream (`text::is_text_codec`) is never given to the worker and gets no note (set aside, spec 8.7); every English or no-language stream (`eng`, `en`, `und` or none) of any other codec goes to the worker, which refuses a codec other than `S_HDMV/PGS` and `S_VOBSUB` with exit 3 naming it.
- T5-R12 (R1-evidence-30). `subs-ocr\summary.json` is written into the job folder's `<title key>\subs-ocr\` through `write_atomic` and copied into the staged `subs-ocr\` through `copy_checked`, the staged one last, after every stream's output has moved; C3 reads the staged one.
- T5-R13 (R1-evidence-26, R1-evidence-31, R6-phase0-61). In the staged folder step 10a writes, in order, the level folder's files, `audio-proxy.mka` when kept, the `audio-proxy.json` beside it, and last the level folder's own `audio-proxy.json`, which is C3's marker; the job folder's level folder gets its `audio-proxy.json` only when the dataset copy lands (design 7.5).
- T5-R14 (R1-evidence-32, R1-evidence-33, R1-evidence-53, R4-commands-43). `JobRecord` gains `tuning_copy: Option<TuningCopy>` (`pending`, `done`; absent when no copy is owed). It becomes `pending` when the unit's Decide feeds `DecisionDone` for a job whose own settings keep tuning evidence, `done` when every file has landed, and absent again when an attempt finds that none of the job's titles has a staged tuning folder while the staging share answers (C2: nothing is owed, `CopyEnd::Nothing`). `TuningEvidencePending` is added only when a copy attempt finds `tuning_root` unreachable or short of space (spec 7's words) and stays after the copy lands. The cleanup and the relay read `tuning_copy`, never the note; "the job's dataset copy exists" means `tuning_copy == Some(TuningCopy::Done)`.
- T5-R15 (R1-evidence-41). The recordings refresh never writes or deletes `notes.md`, `audio-labels.json` or `subtitle-labels.json` (design 7.1's three owner files).
- T5-R16 (call 11). The refresh keeps `.lazymkv-refresh.json` in `<tuning_root>\recordings\<job id>\`, naming each file it wrote with its size and modification time; those are "its own earlier copies"; it deletes only a TMDb (The Movie Database) copy listed there that the job folder no longer holds. An orphan's copy fetched by a relayed `retune` (T5-R60) is never listed there.
- T5-R17 (R1-evidence-54, R6-phase0-75). An `audio-proxy.json` that kept no proxy has `"proxy": null`, `"gains_tried": [-18, -30]` and `encoding.gain_db` -30; `retune` leaves a title out of the dataset only when a level folder's `audio-proxy.json` parses and holds `"proxy": null` (`AudioProxy::kept_no_proxy`); a file without the key, as phase 0's and the existing tests' files are, counts as before (T5-16); design 7.5's sentence is amended (T5-26).
- T5-R18 (R6-phase0-74). The byte-for-byte check compares each series file whole, header included; the replay keeps phase 0's rip file name, so `file=` matches; a difference of name or value goes to the owner.
- T5-R19 (R6-phase0-56). Series files are UTF-8 without a BOM (byte order mark) with CRLF (carriage return and line feed) line ends on every system; `net_fixed1` reproduces the .NET Framework `0.0` format (the parsed double to 15 significant digits, then half away from zero), with a value that rounds to zero printed as Windows PowerShell 5.1 prints it; T5-16 reads the edge values' strings once on the mini PC.
- T5-R20 (R6-phase0-73). `sub-<n>.frames.json`'s `markers` keep phase 0's five keys `bracket`, `note`, `hash`, `speaker`, `upper` with `Count-Markers`' expressions ported (`scripts/phase0/ocr-subs.ps1` lines 58 to 74); `summary.json` gives their per-stream sums; design 10.12's "the text rule's markers" is read so.
- T5-R21 (R6-phase0-76). A crop is `s<n>\f-<pts in milliseconds, zero-padded to 9 digits>.png`, so name order is `pts` (presentation timestamp) order.
- T5-R22 (R2-decoders-57). The goldens pair events and frames as `text::pair_text` does; an unpaired event or frame is counted and reported, not failed, and any unpaired count goes to the owner with the numbers before the trial.
- T5-R23 (R2-decoders-60). A golden difference above 2 grey levels is a finding for the owner; the tolerance stays 2 (call 7).
- T5-R24 (R3-worker-45, R6-phase0-18, R6-phase0-20, R6-phase0-71, R6-phase0-78). B2 amends design 10.9 step 1 for its four inputs. A track missing its frame set takes an existing `ocrs-s<n>.jsonl` as arm F when one exists (row 2) and reports R' as not run; with neither frames nor an F reading, parity cannot be judged on it and the trial stops and goes to the owner. The proof of concept's readings may lack `pts` and `lines`: `FrameEntry.t` and `FrameEntry.pts` are both `Option<f64>`, `f-records` writes `t` alone for a frame without `pts` (the text rule then pairs by the proof of concept's rule of R6-phase0-18), and a frame with neither stops the trial at B2; `inventory` finds `evidence.mks` in the dataset copy, else at the staging path `summary.json` names under `evidence`, else at `$Staging\<slug>\<label>\evidence.mks` (R6-phase0-20).
- T5-R25 (R6-phase0-72). F3's `retune --subtitles` reads arm R's text through a temporary tree on C: laid out `<tmp>\<slug>\<label>\subs-ocr\sub-<n>.ocr.jsonl` (copies of arm R's records), deleted afterwards.
- T5-R26 (R3-worker-42). Call 21's order binds: the trial follows the goldens and precedes every engine OCR task and every driver that only steps 10 and 10a use (T5-13).
- T5-R27 (R3-worker-61, R4-commands-13). The `ocr-trial` feature's one CI build and test run on ubuntu.
- T5-R28 (R4-commands-25, 26, 54, 58). T5-0 reads and the owner approves with A1's plan-time yes: the MKVToolNix version per runner (apt on ubuntu, brew on macos with a version check, the MKVToolNix installer by URL and SHA-256 on windows), the static FFmpeg 7.x URL and SHA-256 for ubuntu, the `cargo-about` version, the ocrs models' Hugging Face card URL for the attribution, and the source and SHA-256 of the CC-BY-SA-4.0 legal code text.
- T5-R29 (R4-commands-32). `--text` with two or more `--evidence` exits 2 with `argument --text: only with a single --evidence; give DIR=TEXTDIR`.
- T5-R30 (R4-commands-34). A folder whose labels do not validate exits 2 at once, as today; 2 is an input error, outside D1's ranking of 3, 1 and 0.
- T5-R31 (R4-commands-37). A run with two or more folders writes no snapshot or baseline; each folder's baseline check reads its own `audio-dataset.json` when present.
- T5-R32 (R4-commands-41). `retune --jobs` without `tuning_root` is refused with `RETUNE_NO_TUNING_ROOT` (`retune --jobs needs tuning_root in the engine's settings (design part 9.5)`).
- T5-R33 (R4-commands-55). TMDb's address printed with its sentence is `https://www.themoviedb.org`.
- T5-R34 (R4-commands-61). `export-models DIR` exits 2 when either target file exists and then writes neither.
- T5-R35 (R4-commands-63, R5-code-44). `lazymkv-ocr.exe` moves from `$optional` to `$required` in `scripts/engine-task.ps1`.
- T5-R36 (R5-code-3, R5-code-4). The workspace keeps one `ureq` entry, its comment amended to name the models' build script; `lazymkv-ocr-models` lists it under `[build-dependencies]`, and lists `sha2` under both `[dependencies]` (for `pins.rs` in the library) and `[build-dependencies]`, which leaves its one `Cargo.lock` entry as it would be with either.
- T5-R37 (R5-code-37). `Spawn.env`'s doc names its one non-empty use, the worker's `RTEN_NUM_THREADS=3`, and `Spawn` derives `Default` (T5-9); `Spawn` gains `cwd: Option<PathBuf>` (T5-13, after the trial) for step 10a's pass, which runs, as phase 0's script did, in its level folder so every filter file name is relative.
- T5-R38 (R6-phase0-63). Step 10a runs one pass per title over the first job line `core::audio::dataset::levels_args` gives for the title (its `-Main` and `-Cand`); further dataset-only lines are not run in phase 2 (spec 6.4 step 10a's one pass) and `job.log` names them.
- T5-R39 (B1 (the worker's folder) read for step 10a). The pass runs in `<data>\work\levels-<job id>-<title key>\` (its working folder); after it ends the engine copies the files into the staged tuning folder (T5-R13's order) and the job folder's level folder through T5-R55's checked copy, then deletes the work folder.
- T5-R40 (R5-code-64, call 25). A several-folder `retune` reports a job-shaped folder (one holding `state.json`, which the refresh copies, T5-20) whose TMDb copy `clients::refetch::stale_copies` names as stale or missing as unmeasured instead of reading it; a relayed run fetches first, so only an orphan whose fetch failed reaches this.
- T5-R41 (calls 2 and 3, F1). A `models` job on ubuntu restores the cache keyed `ocrs-models-<detection sha256>-<recognition sha256>` at the relative path `.ocrs-models` with `enableCrossOsArchive: true`; on a miss it builds `lazymkv-ocr-models` with the variable unset (the build script downloads), copies the two files from its `OUT_DIR` into `.ocrs-models` with `shell: bash`, and saves the cache. The three matrix jobs `needs: models`, restore the same key with `enableCrossOsArchive: true`, and set `LAZYMKV_OCRS_MODELS` to the folder as the absolute path `${{ github.workspace }}/.ocrs-models` at the `test` job's level, which every build and test step reads (cargo runs a build script in its package's folder, so a relative path would not resolve), so a run downloads at most once.
- T5-R42 (F1's speed on CI and the mini PC). `rten`, its `rten-*` crates and `ocrs` build at `opt-level = 3` in the dev profile (`[profile.dev.package.<name>]`), which the test profile inherits.
- T5-R43 (E1, call 26). The notices file is `crates/ctl/crate-notices.txt`, made by `cargo-about generate` over the workspace with `about.toml` (dev- and build-dependencies ignored) and `about.hbs` (name, licence and licence text, no versions). T5-7 adds the ubuntu CI job in report-only form (it regenerates, prints the difference and uploads the artifact `crate-notices`); T5-23 commits the artifact of its parent commit's run and makes the job fail on a difference. Design 4.7's and 11.4's "the release job generates the crate notices" is amended by T5-26 (T5-R50).
- T5-R44 (design 10.9 arm R). The trial's arm R runs with the engine's fixed cap `time_cap_s(events)`; a kill on any track fails the trial, as speed on row 4 and as parity on the others; row 4's wall time is the one `ocr-trial read` prints, from the worker's start to its exit with the model load included (design 10.8's clock), with `run.json`'s `wall_ms` reported beside it.
- T5-R45 (RP21, the notes' rule on titles). The six trial tracks reach the goldens and the trial only through `C:\lazymkv\phase0\trial-tracks.txt` (git-ignored, outside the repository), one `<slug>/<label>/s<n>` line per row of design 10.9's table, which the controller writes on the mini PC at T5-11 and the scripts pass as `LAZYMKV_OCR_TRACKS`; the goldens and the trial's scripts print `row <k>` labels, never a slug or label.
- T5-R46 (design 7.5's tree). The dataset's `subs-ocr\evidence.mks` is copied from `<staging>\<job id>\evidence\<title key>.mks`; the staged `subs-ocr\` holds no second copy.
- T5-R47 (R3-worker-14, the Goals). The notes' one-English-stream UHD (Ultra HD Blu-ray) job runs step 10a only (step 10 is skipped by `step10_runs`), on the mini PC at T5-25. The several-stream job runs both on stand-ins only, since F2 allows no new disc: the two-stream scenarios of T5-15, T5-17 and T5-18 are the Goal's evidence for it, the trial's arm R is step 10's only real data, and engine.md reports no real several-stream run (T5-26).
- T5-R48 (R5-code-78). The stand-in's `Response` gains `creates_first`, files created before any output, so a stand-in worker can write its records and then hang (T5-15); its `Create` gains `in_cwd`, a file name in the stand-in's working folder, for step 10a's relative file names (T5-17).
- T5-R49 (R2-decoders-17, R4-commands-27, R4-commands-29). K11's skip below FFmpeg 7.0 stays as the rule for a machine without a new enough copy; CI's ubuntu runner gets the static 7.x and does not skip; windows installs MKVToolNix with its installer (call 20 over design 12.1's package managers).
- T5-R50 (A2, B1 (scope), B1 (the worker's folder), C1, E1, E3, calls 20, 22 and 23). T5-26 amends the design in place, each change marked with the notes' id: part 4.1's sentence on `dev` and `ocr` (A2) and its one-package `ocr` row (call 23); part 4.7's "`ocr`'s build script" (call 23) and its notices generated on the release runner (E1); part 10.11's "The `ocr` crate's `build.rs`" (call 23); part 11.1's ocrs row, "compiled into `lazymkv-ocr.exe` and `lazymkvctl.exe`, with both models embedded" (E3); part 11.4's "the release job generates the crate notices" (E1); parts 3.5 and 6.6's lists of the data folder, which gain `<data>\work\` (B1 (the worker's folder)); part 7.5's sentence on `audio-proxy.json` (call 22) and its sentence on where step 10a's pass and the OCR write (B1 (the worker's folder)); part 10.2 step 3 and part 10.7's output folder (B1 (the worker's folder)); part 12.1's ubuntu sentence (call 20); part 12.6's numbering (B1 (scope)).
- T5-R51 (R2-decoders-63, design 10.8, spec 8.7). `text::subs_run` gains `unread: &[(i64, String)]` (the `n` and `why` of `summary.json`'s unread streams); when it is not empty, before its two checks for missing text, it writes one line `stream s{n} {tag}: not read by the engine: {why}` per unread stream and returns refused, so a title with an unread stream gets that Skipped subtitles section and its text adds no review; with `unread` empty its output is unchanged, and phase 0's hint "read it with ocr-subs.ps1" stays for a stream without text that no `summary.json` marks unread (the golden `a_subs_run_with_an_english_stream_without_text_gives_no_subs_golden` unchanged). `retune --subtitles`' row 14 harness reads a labelled unread stream as it reads any labelled stream without text today ("Labels that cannot be judged:", the folder's code 3), and a several-folder run's code is then D1's worst.
- T5-R52 (R4-commands-23, F1). F1's "one rten build" is one per CI run, in the ubuntu job, with no build cache; "cached by the models' SHA-256 values" qualifies the model download. The `ocr-trial` build of T5-10 reuses that job's rten.
- T5-R53 (G2, call 12). The TEXTDIR half of `--evidence DIR=TEXTDIR` is read only with `--subtitles` and ignored otherwise, so the relay's `--evidence <tuning_root>\recordings=<tuning_root>\dataset` serves the audio harness unchanged; `--evidence DIR=TEXTDIR` given with `--text` exits 2 with `argument --text: not with --evidence DIR=TEXTDIR, which names the text already`, so a relayed `retune --jobs --subtitles --text X` exits 2 and the owner gives `--evidence DIR=TEXTDIR` instead.
- T5-R54 (R3-worker-54, R6-phase0-37, design 10.6 and 10.9 "One trial", K2, B3). The trial's build is T5-11's pushed commit with CI green and CodeQL at 0, checked clean before T5-12's build and recorded in `docs/phase2/ocr-trial.md` and the evidence line. From it on no task edits `crates/core/src/subtitle/bitmap/`, `crates/ocr/`, `crates/ocr-models/`, `crates/tools/src/ocr.rs`, `Cargo.toml` or `Cargo.lock`; a change there goes to the owner and reopens the trial. T5-9 writes `run_worker`'s `Spawn` with `..Spawn::default()` so T5-13's new field leaves `crates/tools/src/ocr.rs` untouched. On a FAIL, T5-12 still commits `ocr-trial.md` with its numbers and pushes it, and the pull request stops (B3).
- T5-R55 (B1 (the worker's folder), design 7.5's EVID-4 discipline). The data folder, staging and `tuning_root` are different volumes, so a rename between them fails: every copy between them is `staged::copy_checked` (copy to `<to>.partial`, flushed with `sync_all`, its size against the source's, rename to `<to>`), and the "moves" of B1 (the worker's folder) are `staged::move_checked` (that copy, then the source deleted); a copy whose target is in the job folder is `staged::copy_into_job` (the source's bytes, their count checked, then `write_atomic`), so every engine-written job-folder file goes through `write_atomic` (R5-code-30). A unit test reads the unit's sources and finds no other `fs::rename` or `fs::copy`.
- T5-R56 (design 5.6 step 6, review round 1 of pull request 3, V5). Steps 10 and 10a return `staged::UnitError`: `Failed(String)` (a tool, decode or argument failure) feeds `DecisionFailed { Unknown }` with its sentence; `Staging(String)` (a write error on the staging share) is classed by `staging_write_failure(self.staging_health())`, so the job waits as AwaitingShare or AwaitingSpace, never Unknown.
- T5-R57 (design 5.3 "Recording", 7.1, 10.12 item 3; R3-worker-40, R1-evidence-22). The worker's run is recorded capture-style at `<data>\work\ocr-<job id>-<title key>\s<n>.capture.txt` with its `.stderr`, `.timing.jsonl` and `.exit.json`; exit 3's reason is the first line of the `.stderr` file, read before the folder is deleted; no capture file reaches the job folder (design 7.1 has no row for it, and a panic's standard error can hold text); the run's exit code and wall time are kept in `summary.json` and its `job.log` line. Step 10a's setting-on pass keeps phase 0's `ffmpeg.txt` in its level folder; the setting-off streamhash pass runs in `<data>\work\levels-<job id>-<title key>\` too, and its capture is copied into the job folder's `<title key>\` as `streamhash-ffmpeg.txt` with its three siblings, beside `streamhash.txt`.
- T5-R58 (R3-worker-21, R3-worker-60, design 10.8). `step10::read_streams` takes `cap: &dyn Fn(usize) -> Duration`; `RealSteps` fills it with `time_cap_s` unless `EngineConfig.ocr_cap_override` is set, which only the testkit harness sets, as it sets `limits_override`; it has no settings key and the native main passes None. The engine's kill tests set it to 2 s times `ci_factor()` (a wait for the stand-in to write its records, so it scales) and pin the detail with that value.
- T5-R59 (R3-worker-6, R2-decoders-12). `dev::cmd::decide::subtitle_section` takes `clear_max: i64`; `lazymkv-dev decide` passes `TextRule::default().CLEAR_MAX_BYTES` and the engine the job's `sub_clear_max_bytes`, the value the worker and `check_events` take, so the text rule's events and the worker's records agree for any value.
- T5-R60 (call 25). An orphan's copy fetched by a relayed `retune` is written into `<tuning_root>\recordings\<job id>\` as `serde_json::to_vec_pretty` plus a final `\n`, through `<name>.partial`, a size check against the bytes and a rename; it is that copy's only home.
- T5-R61 (the notes' two B1s). They are cited as "B1 (scope)" and "B1 (the worker's folder)" in the code's docs, engine.md's rulings and the design's amendment marks.
- T5-R62 (K2, R3-worker-47, R6-phase0-28). Arm R starts the worker through `ocr_trial::read_with`, which the binary calls with `lazymkv_platform::native_guard()` (`JobProcessGuard` on the mini PC), so it runs below normal with the engine's cleared environment plus `RTEN_NUM_THREADS=3`, exactly as the engine starts it; arm R' runs in-process with `RTEN_NUM_THREADS=3` set by the script; the evidence line records both.
- T5-R63 (call 1, R2-decoders-41, R3-worker-8). T5-0 reads ocrs 0.13.1's feature table and public re-exports (metadata and published documentation only, Ruling T3-1-a): the features that load the `.onnx` models (call 1's "only the feature ocrs needs", which phase 0 built as `onnx` and `rten`) and whether the rectangle and point types `OcrEngine::recognize_text` takes are re-exported. If they are not, `lazymkv-ocr` takes `rten-imageproc` as a direct dependency pinned to the delta's exact version, so the lock keeps one entry; T5-1's manifest and both owner sentences name it.
- T5-R64 (R2-decoders-17, R4-commands-27, R4-commands-29; call 20, T5-R49). With `CI` set, as GitHub Actions sets it, a layer 2 test whose tool is missing or whose FFmpeg or ffprobe is below 7.0 fails with `layer 2 must run on CI (call 20, T5-R49): <reason>` (T5-24), and T5-13's ffprobe cross-check fails with `CI has no ffprobe, so call 24's cross-check cannot run on this runner`, instead of printing `skipped:`, since cargo hides a passing test's output and a skip on CI would hide that the check never ran; every runner installs the tools. Off CI, design 12.1's skip and K11's rule below FFmpeg 7.0 stand (the mini PC, a developer's machine). This narrows design 12.1's "a test whose tool is missing prints skipped and passes" for CI only; T5-26 records it in engine.md. T5-13, T5-24.
- T5-R65 (engine.md's S80, design part 12.1, G1; T5-26). The self-test's judging of spec 13's minimum versions of MKVToolNix and FFmpeg moves to pull request 6, with the release's FFmpeg build; layer 2 checks FFmpeg 7.0 in its tests only (T5-24, K11, T5-R64), and the self-test keeps printing each tool's version and path as at `07ccc48`. T5-26 amends S80's sentence in engine.md, which put those minimums with layer 2 in pull request 5, and lists the check under pull request 6 in "Left for later pull requests".

except two of the timing races, F8 to F10), each needing a ruling or recorded for the owner's knowledge:
- Findings in frozen paths (T5-R54), each left because an edit there, even a doc line, fails the freeze check and makes a new trial: the `pub(crate)` helpers `sha256_hex` (no doc) and the test helper `blocks` (no design part, no `Def:` tail); the `Def:` tails of `LINE_MARGIN` (`T5-5`, added by `164099d` in T5-12's fix) and `palette_luma` (`T5-4`, added by `d36f34f` in T5-11's fix); the model URL docs that say CI's models job only although the build script downloads in any build without `LAZYMKV_OCRS_MODELS`, with T5-5's and T5-11's naming minors (`Grey`, `DECODER_VERSION`, `bt601_luma` misnamed, `palette_luma` could be `pub(crate)`, `pgs.rs` importing from `vobsub.rs`, a test comment saying grey 136 where the chain gives 123); and the unbounded zlib inflation in `mkv.rs` (`decompress_to_vec_zlib` without a limit), which runs only in the sacrificial worker over mkvmerge's own output. The owner chooses: a doc-only edit with a ruling that it needs no new trial, this record, or pull request 6.
- Design limits: H3, the relayed retune's refresh of a held job's mirror runs outside the owner's one-refresh-per-job guard, so a concurrent refresh can share a `.partial` and the relayed retune can exit 3 once (a rerun clears it); H6, a `tuning_root` containing `=` corrupts the engine-built `DIR=TEXTDIR` word and `Settings::validate` has no check for it; a relayed `retune --jobs --evidence <clone>` cannot refetch a stale clone copy, because G3 narrowed design part 9.5's refetch to `<tuning_root>\recordings` and the child reads `TMDB_TOKEN` from an environment the engine does not give it (the workaround is a local `lazymkv-dev retune --evidence <clone>` with the token set); and a relayed `decide --job --title` of a job whose copy is done reads `--text <tuning_root>\dataset` as a flat folder for a title with no `subs-ocr`, which walks the whole dataset share (`--text <exact subs-ocr folder>` works around it).
- Known CI and build-box timing flakes: CF8, `owner::tests::a_no_event_step_gives_the_token_back_beside_a_stale_step_of_its_job` on windows (the stale Plan ended first at 3.76 s and 3.84 s against 3 s, once, on `62d096e`); `a_rip_result_that_cannot_be_persisted_fails_the_job_and_gives_the_token_back` on windows once (CI run 42567502713); two `engine_tuning` and `engine_disc_phase` races, fixed by the fix wave (F8 to F10, with F7's docs); and C.5, the build box's `ci_factor` of 1, which let `engine_file_phase` tests hit the 10 s harness wait under load (T5-18's first run, and the junction runs of this task, Pull request 5 on the mini PC, below).
- Observations: test constants in `crates/core/src/naming.rs` and `crates/core/src/settings.rs`, on `main` before this branch, use a UNC path whose host the owner should confirm is a generic device default and not the real name; a real film's name appears in more than 100 lines of `main`; and an unreachable staging share at `ExtractSubtitleEvidence`'s `staged_file` listing fails as `DecisionFailed` class `Unknown` where Probe has the same pattern (the fix wave's F2 guard covers the later `clear_incomplete` window only).
- `engine_file_phase`'s `rerun::a_retry_after_a_lookup_pending_decide_does_not_decode_again`: the box's wait margin for this test is the owner's call (a lighter scenario, or a wait factor for the build box).

### Pull request 5: what the plan's review focus and declined items left

The plan's five Review Focus items, each with the tests that pin it, and the ten items its skeleton critiques raised
that it took in part or in another form.

- `tuning_root` drops or fills while files land: `a_partial_left_by_a_lost_share_is_replaced_and_the_copy_lands_at_the_next_reach`, `a_copy_whose_size_differs_after_the_write_stays_pending_and_keeps_the_staging_data`, `a_full_tuning_root_leaves_the_copy_pending_with_the_note_and_the_decision_unchanged` and `a_pending_copy_is_retried_at_engine_start_and_only_copies`, in T5-19.
- The worker misbehaves: `a_worker_that_panics_leaves_the_stream_unread_with_its_exit_code_and_no_stderr_text`, `a_killed_worker_with_a_torn_last_record_counts_only_whole_records` and `a_worker_that_writes_more_records_than_events_is_unread_by_the_cross_check`, in T5-15.
- The unit is interrupted: `a_restart_after_a_torn_move_deletes_the_staged_folder_and_reads_again` and `an_incomplete_staged_folder_is_deleted_and_both_steps_run_again`, in T5-18; `engine_start_removes_a_leftover_work_folder` and `a_stop_during_a_worker_run_kills_it_and_leaves_no_work_folder`, in T5-15.
- mkvmerge-written Matroska beyond the minimal shape: `the_reader_skips_void_and_crc_elements_and_reads_unknown_size_clusters`, `cues_tags_and_attachments_around_the_clusters_are_skipped`, `a_block_group_with_a_duration_reads_as_its_block` and `a_negative_relative_timestamp_is_the_cluster_time_minus_it`, in T5-3.
- Level-series formatting at its edges: `net_fixed1_matches_dotnet_framework_on_the_edge_values` and `a_raw_file_with_crlf_and_a_trailing_blank_line_gives_the_same_series`, in T5-16.
- Coverage finding 4 (the CI half of call 24 before the trial): the cross-check is T5-13, after the trial and before step 10, because its packet driver is a step 10 driver.
- Coverage finding 5 (keep the worker's `.exit.json` in the job folder): none of the capture is copied; the exit code and wall time are kept in `summary.json` and `job.log` (T5-R57).
- Interfaces finding 5 (split T5-9): applied as T5-9 and T5-13, with `Spawn` deriving `Default` in T5-9 so the frozen `crates/tools/src/ocr.rs` needs no edit.
- Interfaces finding 7 (the engine's cap tests): a `cap` function filled from the test-only `ocr_cap_override` (T5-R58), not the 60 s floor.
- Interfaces finding 11 (proof that the checked copy is used): the source-scan test `the_unit_moves_files_across_volumes_only_through_the_checked_copy`, not a fault seam.
- Interfaces finding 15 (the notices job as its own task): the report-only job rides with the model cache in T5-7.
- Coverage finding 16 (F1's "one rten build"): the ruling T5-R52, one build per run in the ubuntu job, with no build cache.
- Coverage finding 7 (`subs_run`'s refusal): the ruling T5-R51 gives an unread stream its own line, and phase 0's hint stays for phase 0's shape.
- Coverage finding 11 (R4-commands-57): T5-23 is added to the map rather than the id dropped from its Covers.
- Coverage finding 3 and interfaces finding 12 (different freeze lists): T5-R54 takes their union.

## Rulings on the open points

These are the planner's rulings of 2026-10-24 on the nine points the plan left open (OPEN 1 to OPEN 9). The owner made
no override of them before execution, and the code follows each one as written.

- RP-1 (OPEN 1, later drive listings): (a) record each later listing as `drive-list-<n>.txt` with its run files, numbered from 2. Why: design part 7.2 records every external run; a listing that leaves no file breaks that rule. Cost if wrong: a few small files per job. Where: `crates/engine/src/steps.rs` (`listing`); `crates/fake-tools/tests/whole_job.rs` `LATER_LISTINGS`; `crates/fake-tools/tests/engine_disc_phase.rs` `later_drive_listings_are_recorded_as_drive_list_n`.
- RP-2 (OPEN 2, no DRV line for the drive id): (a) feed `ScanFailed` or `TitleRipFailed` with class `Unknown` and a message naming the drive id. Why: the tool is present; the disc or drive is the problem, and `ToolMissing` would send the owner to reinstall MakeMKV. Cost if wrong: a job fails where a wait would have served. Where: `crates/engine/src/steps.rs` (`listing`); `engine_disc_phase.rs` `a_missing_drive_index_fails_the_scan_naming_the_drive`.
- RP-3 (OPEN 3, a missing `appdata.tar` or `default.mmcp.xml`): (a) `ToolMissing { tool: makemkvcon }`, which leads to AwaitingTool. Why: the MakeMKV installation is incomplete, and AwaitingTool is the state the design gives an incomplete tool (ARCH-9). Cost if wrong: the owner sees "waiting for a tool" for a file problem; the waiting message names the file. Where: `crates/engine/src/steps.rs` (`run_scan`, the job profile's write); `engine_disc_phase.rs` `a_missing_appdata_tar_waits_for_makemkvcon`.
- RP-4 (OPEN 4, decide's plan section of a lookup-pending job): (a) `PlanEntry::Results` built around the persisted refreshed plan and the job's settings. Why: the decision must follow the plan the engine ripped by (ENG-9, R-B1); Task 29's scenario is not lookup-pending, so CI is unchanged. Cost if wrong: a lookup-pending job's `decide.json` differs from a fresh `plan --job`, which design part 12.7 already accepts and `compare-job` (PR 6) explains. Where: `crates/engine/src/file_phase.rs` (`engine_decide_inputs`); `crates/fake-tools/tests/engine_file_phase.rs` `a_lookup_pending_job_decides_with_the_refreshed_plan`.
- RP-5 (OPEN 5, where the one scan retry runs): (b) `scan` fails after the first timeout, and the engine lists the drives and scans once more. Why: design part 5.4 step 1 reads the drive index before every call and never caches it; the retry is then a visible engine step with its own records. Cost if wrong: one more drive listing per retried scan. Where: `crates/tools/src/makemkv.rs` (`scan`, `SCAN_PAST_LIMIT`) and `crates/engine/src/steps.rs` (`run_scan`); `crates/fake-tools/tests/makemkv_driver.rs` `a_scan_past_its_limit_is_killed_and_fails_as_unknown` and `engine_disc_phase.rs` `a_scan_past_its_limit_is_killed_listed_again_and_retried_once`.
- RP-6 (OPEN 6, the first scan's records after a `MSG:1009`): (a) the second scan replaces `scan.txt` and its run files, and `job.log` keeps the rejected scan's 1009 text. Why: the design lists no `scan-rejected.txt`, and the recording layout stays phase 0's. Cost if wrong: the rejected scan's lines survive only in `job.log`. Where: `crates/engine/src/steps.rs` (`run_scan`); `engine_disc_phase.rs` `a_1009_on_the_first_scan_switches_every_later_call_to_fallback_mmcp_xml`.
- RP-7 (OPEN 7, where the verified size lives): (a) `VerifyFile` in Remuxing sets `TitleRecord.filed` with the final path and the partial's size, and `File` overwrites it with the real mtime. Why: no new field, no second `mkvmerge -J`. Cost if wrong: a restart in Verified reads a size that `File` would have replaced anyway. Where: `crates/engine/src/steps.rs` (`verify_partial`, `file`; Task 25, PF-26); `crates/fake-tools/tests/engine_restart.rs` `a_restart_in_verified_with_the_file_already_filed_feeds_file_done` and `a_restart_in_verified_with_another_size_at_the_final_path_fails_as_unknown`.
- RP-8 (OPEN 8, a torn last line of `transitions.jsonl`): (a) `Store::persist` first writes `\n` when the file does not end in one. Why: one line of code keeps every later line parseable, and the log is read by people and tools. Cost if wrong: none worth naming. Where: `crates/engine/src/store.rs` (`Store::persist`, landed in Task 21, T21-c2); `engine_restart.rs` `a_torn_last_line_gets_its_newline_before_the_next_append`.
- RP-9 (OPEN 9, the MakeMKV check after a restart): (a) persist the recorded version and licence code in `<data>/selftest/makemkv-check.json`. Why: the comparison needs a baseline that survives a restart, and each job's own scan output may predate a MakeMKV update. Cost if wrong: one small file in the data folder. Where: `crates/engine/src/selftest.rs` (`MakeMkvState::open` and `observe`); `whole_job.rs` assertion 8 (T29-c).

- listing stored share names (S77 stays);
- `WNetCancelConnection2W` for lazymkv's deviceless connections, which live until logoff;
- a time bound on a share call (pull request 3 only moves the calls off the owner thread);
- a disc with several same-length playlists that TheDiscDB does not know: identified by TMDb alone, it has no main-title pick and plans copy mode (T4-R20);
- the reviewed identity (spec 11) carried in `ChainInputs`.

**Phase 5:**
- `Autostart` and the logon trigger, through Task Scheduler's Component Object Model (COM) interface (design part 3.9);
- a pipe client that stops reading with 64 KiB unread keeps its writer thread, its reader thread, one pipe instance and up to `CLIENT_QUEUE` queued lines until it reads or exits, even after S52's drop or `Clients::close` removes its queue. Design part 4.4's disconnect is only logical then. Phase 2 meets it with `lazymkvctl watch` in an SSH session whose network dropped, in `watch | more` paused, or in a console paused in QuickEdit. K12 holds, and a stop waits at most `CLIENT_DRAIN`. The UI's pull request closes it with an abort hook on `IpcStream`, not a write timeout, which would cut a slowly paged `command` (W10, T3-22-a) (review round 1 of pull request 3, R16).

**Not planned:** spec 4.4's registry-entry form of the default install lookup; spec 4.4 allows the folder or the entry, and W18 reads the folders, whose places come from the registry since F1.

**After pull request 3's whole-branch review** (deferred by the controller, 2026-10-29):
- an owner-based predicate for `find_tool`'s steps 2 and 4: a `resources` folder and its program file, or a `PATH` folder and its program file, taken only when SYSTEM, Administrators or TrustedInstaller owns them, and `PATH` taken from its system value under `HKEY_LOCAL_MACHINE`, not from the engine's own environment; W17's writability test tells whether a non-elevated process can change a path now, not whether one chose it, and a process that chooses the whole path can lock its own folder so that the test passes; the values of the names the Job Object runner keeps for a child (`PATH`'s user part, `TEMP` and `TMP`, the profile folders) still come from an environment `HKCU\Environment` and Volatile Environment shape; building them from `HKEY_LOCAL_MACHINE` and the token is the same work, and needs an expander that reads no environment, since the system `Path` holds `%SystemRoot%` (review round 1 of pull request 3, V1);
- F2's second layer: refusing a data folder whose parent grants the user's filtered token delete-child, `DELETE`, `WRITE_DAC` or `WRITE_OWNER` (not W17's whole folder mask, which the real ProgramData folder fails, since it lets users add files and folders);
- on a data folder the engine did not create, whose earlier list let a non-administrator write, nothing sees content written into an Administrators-owned entry before the first start, or an entry made between V3's walk and the set; an Administrators-owned entry with explicit or protected user-writable entries is not refused (the final review's platform-win-a-5 for the path race; review round 1 of pull request 3, V3);
- a design question for the owner: the elevated `makemkvcon` takes its Java from `app_Java` under `HKEY_CURRENT_USER\Software\MakeMKV` (phase 0's EXP-12), which a non-elevated process of the owner can write, so P6 cannot be fully met while MakeMKV runs elevated with the owner's own settings; the engine could read `app_Java`, as it reads `app_Key`, and refuse or warn when the program it names fails the owner test, and the same holds for whatever else MakeMKV reads to find Java; `JAVA_HOME` no longer reaches it (review round 1 of pull request 3, V1).

**Not measured in pull request 3:**
- whether a window in an SSH session receives drive notifications (design part 3.1), so a console run is never used with a disc;
- P7's fallback success branch, where lazymkv's stored credential reaches a share the session cannot, since the arranged share was not run (Owner decision 7).

**The pull request that builds copy mode:** `cancel_event` has no Extracting or Copying row, so a step that panics there maps to FileFailed, which those states reject: a CheckSpace in Extracting falls back to ShareLost, and an ExtractTitle, CopyDisc or EmptyCopyDirectory feeds nothing and leaves its job with no step (review round 1, V11). Adding the rows changes R-A7's table, a ruling.

**Never reached in phase 2** (design part 5.2): `DeleteJobData`, `CopyDisc`, `ExtractTitle`, `NetworkNeeded` and
`VersionMessage`. A refused `NetworkRetry` in AwaitingNetwork would end its 10-minute chain the same way a refused
`LookupPending` ended the hourly lookup chain, for the pull request that reaches `NetworkNeeded` (review round 1 of pull
request 3, R27).

**Noted, not scheduled:**
- a second failed verification for a wrong `.mkv` count sends the title on with no fresh `mkvmerge.json`, and Probe then fails on every Retry; RipTitle requires one `.mkv`, so the folder must change between the two steps (review round 1, V9);
- a waiting job's second copy of its disc inserted in another drive moves the job's drive there while the first copy stays in, and a pull of the second copy then leaves the job waiting for a disc that is still in the first drive (review round 1, V2);
- `Owner::delete_partials` still lists and deletes the library's `.lazymkv-partial` files on the owner thread at a forced shutdown, with no `reach` first, so a stalled library share delays the shutdown; moving it to a thread that asks `reach` first would close it (the review of Task T3-17);
- a user-initiated logoff or restart is never delayed while a step runs; blocking it with `ShutdownBlockReasonCreate` would change design part 3.8 and is the owner's call, most naturally with phase 5's tray window (review round 1 of pull request 3, V4);
- the engine itself does not refuse a drive's root as `--data`; `engine-task.ps1` refuses one for the task (V13), but a console run can still give one (review round 1 of pull request 3, a verifier's note outside the 70 findings);
- `rip_again` replies ok when its transition was not persisted (`Fed::NotPersisted`); an error reply in RR1-4's style would say so (review round 1 of pull request 3, a verifier's observation outside the 70 findings);
- RipTitle's `create_dir_all` can make a missing staging root and its missing ancestors on a restart into Ripping, past W15's "never deeper" (review round 1 of pull request 3, a verifier's observation outside the 70 findings);
- `lazymkv-tools`' tests make their temporary folders with the crate's own `temp_dir` and remove them at the end, so a failed assertion leaves one; moving them to core's `Scratch`, as R42 did for `lazymkv-platform`, would close it (review round 1 of pull request 3, the final review's W13);
- two of pull request 2's owner tests each failed once on `windows-latest` during pull request 3 and passed when run again, `owner::tests::already_ripped_sends_already_ripped_and_ejects_outside_the_reducer` and `owner::tests::a_waiting_jobs_disc_is_only_noted_and_takes_no_token`, each reading `drives.json` a moment before the engine had written it (a settle timing race in the tests).

**Accepted in pull request 2 and left as they are:**
- abbreviations of `--job`, `--jobs` and `--title` belong to `lazymkvctl` (PF-32), so `--t` is `--title`, not dev's `--text`;
- the index guard fails open when no `MSG:5014` or `MSG:5015` appears (Task 13, as the plan requires);
- KeepAwake is held through waits entered after a file-phase slot grant (the review of Task 24; confirmed in pull request 3 by W9, from spec 4.3's "while any job is active");
- `release_promises` never releases the rip check's Remux promise on a transition into NeedsReview or Discarded from the checked states; the next restart drops it (the review of Task 25).

**Accepted in pull request 3 and left as they are:**
- the build folders `C:\lazymkv\src` and `C:\lazymkv\target`, and the run folder's parent `C:\lazymkv`, let any authenticated user write, so a process that is not elevated could change a program before `engine-task.ps1` copies it, or replace the run folder while no engine runs; the owner accepted that risk on this development machine (Owner decision 13 and its addendum, `-AcceptWritableBuild`), and a protected build folder and parent wait for a later pull request (T3-24-c);
- the `command` relay forwards the client's words to the child `lazymkvctl`, any flag included, because the pipe admits only elevated processes of the owner's own account (P6), which already hold every right the child has; a pipe opened to anyone else would need an allowlist of words (Task T3-22, above);
- once lazymkv's stored credential has connected a share, the deviceless connection lasts for the logon session, so later `reach` calls get in through it and `access` reports "the session's access" until logoff (`WNetCancelConnection2W` is phase 3's, above);
- `testutil::cue` writes `CUE: <text>` with no leading newline, so libtest's progress text can share its line, and a reader of a desktop job's log searches for `CUE:` anywhere in a line (`findstr /c:"CUE:"`), not at a line's start (Task T3-25's first drive job).

**Accepted in pull request 4 and left as they are:**
- dev commands other than `plan`, `decide` and `retune` that read `tmdb.json` (`titles`, `levels-args`, `labels`, `golden`, `census`) never fetch a stale copy;
- a `--tmdb-search` or `--tmdb-record` file given by hand, and any engine job folder, is read by dev as it is (T4-R10);
- dev reads a folder without `lookup-status.txt` as nothing pending where the engine's book would be pending;
- the replay of saved live records runs in `clients`' tests only (T4-R19), though design part 8.5 names `core` and `clients`;
- https webhook delivery and the self-signed refusal of design part 8.1 are not exercised: not by CI, which posts http over loopback only and never reaches a TLS handshake (T4-R14), and not by this pull request's mini PC run, whose webhook URL was plain http on the local area network (LAN), design part 8.1's LAN case; a later live run with an https Home Assistant would exercise them;
- `retune --jobs` keeps W25's refusal (exit 2); relayed since pull request 5 (W25 closed, T5-22).

**Accepted in pull request 5 and left as they are:**
- no real disc with several English subtitle streams ran: that job ran on stand-ins, and the trial's arm R is step 10's only run on real data (F2, T5-R47);
- step 10a runs one pass per title over the first job line `levels_args` gives, and `job.log` names any further dataset-only line, which phase 2 does not run (T5-R38);
- a `retune` over two or more folders writes no snapshot or baseline, and `--snapshot`, `--compare` and `--text` need a single `--evidence` (D1, T5-R29, T5-R31);
- `lazymkv-ocr` builds and runs on CI's ubuntu runner only, and the Windows worker is first built on the mini PC (call 23, F1); the `ocr-trial` feature builds and tests on ubuntu only (T5-R27);
- the worker's capture, whose standard error can hold subtitle text, never reaches the job folder; its exit code and wall time are kept in `summary.json` and `job.log` (T5-R57);
- the trial's build is frozen from commit `164099d`: a later change to the bitmap pipeline, the worker, its driver, the models or the pins is a new trial (design parts 10.6 and 10.9, T5-R54);
- the final review's confirmed and left items, all doc, style or rare-case: about 24 pub items and four helpers cite no design part, `pub-docs.pl` sees only bare `pub` and not `pub(crate)`, `retune`'s `USAGE` writes `[--set NAME=VALUE]` where the command table writes `[--set NAME=VALUE ...]` (pinned byte for byte, K5), a relayed `retune --jobs --subtitles` exits 2 until the first dataset copy creates `<tuning_root>\dataset`, a one-folder relayed `retune --jobs` shows the engine's recordings as `corpus/owner/<job id>` (K5), `copy_checked_with` leaves a `.partial` after a failed write that the next copy removes, the recordings refresh's retry set is in memory so an engine restart forgets it, the ubuntu MKVToolNix pin is the noble package while the matrix runs on `ubuntu-latest`, the rten guard in `ci.yml` accepts any "did not match any packages", about 386 added lines exceed 120 characters (321 copied from the plan, 25 in frozen paths) and test fixtures are copied between test files (the final review's list `final-left.md`, items 1 to 20).

## Pull request 5 on the mini PC

These runs were on the mini PC, each in the session it names; no secret, webhook ID, account name, user SID or drive
serial is written, the owner's discs and phase 0's recordings are named only by their kind ("phase 0's kept UHD
all-tracks rip") and the trial's tracks by their row of design part 10.9's table, and a run that printed `skipped:`
counts as not run. Each line names the task and step it records; the job ID, the film folder, the title, the share and
the tuning dataset are written `<job id>`, `<film folder>`, `<title>`, `<share>` and `<tuning dataset>`.

`no_scenario_holds_a_clone_value` needs the evidence clone (`LAZYMKV_EVIDENCE`) and skips without it, so the RP21
guard over `corpus/scenarios/` runs only in the mini PC's junction run, not on CI. With the clone, on the mini PC:

```bash
scripts/box.sh "(if not exist corpus mkdir corpus)&& mklink /J corpus\owner C:\lazymkv\evidence\owner&& set LAZYMKV_EVIDENCE=C:\lazymkv\evidence\owner&& cargo test --workspace --locked"
```

Three notes on how the tests behave on CI. The characterisation test of `decide` compares bytes, after writing path
separators as `/`, on Windows and, on ubuntu and macos, compares the parsed documents structurally, with numbers within
1e-12 of the larger of their magnitudes, an absolute 1e-12 below magnitude 1 (R29.c1a), because the stored documents
hold floats computed with the Windows C library's mathematical functions, whose last digit can differ on the other
systems. The engine testkit's time bounds scale by 4 when the `CI` environment variable is set (R29.c2), so a slow CI
runner does not fail a test that is only waiting. `lazymkv-platform`'s tests scale a wait that only waits by their own
copy of `ci_factor`, and a bound that proves a call does not block (K12, the share stalls) does not scale.
